Status of CE-11b unit U6, second part (track u6b): the first part was integrated into central at `dad157b1`; this part was built over central `dad157b1` on branch `codex/deployment-ce11b-cut`, with five Lean commits (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostMark` at `36dd2bfe`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostSim` at `a518241d`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostSource` at `5a43a7ce`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostItem` at `83474917`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPostSteps` at `2184b67a`; 107 guarded theorems) and two documentation commits (sections "CE-11b unit U6, second part: ..." below), followed by a review fix pass (one Lean commit with docstrings only, `60721af7`, and one documentation commit; section "CE-11b unit U6, second part: review fix pass"). It builds the post-challenge simulation of the active branch as two functional simulations into a common middle execution of the compiled program: the decision step on the CE side (the final mark is the selected accept's returned session), the regrouping of item 2's post-challenge stack into an upper and an optional service layer with a budgeted simulation lemma which, once instantiated for item 2 with the source creates as its costly requests (open point 2), will also exclude a stop of the post-challenge router, the middle execution with the CE side's step commutation complete (under CE-3's merge invariant every deployment command commutes with CE-3's merge, the hidden steps included; the whole-program form `compile_simulationMid` also needs the middle invariant of open point 1, which remains), item 2's service layers on native skeleton steps with the erasure of the router transcripts and the skeleton's game observers, and item 2's view of the middle state with the first per-request steps (coins, BLAKE2b, the Server kernel reveal). The active branch (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealActive.ActiveBranch`) is not yet proved: the invariants of the middle execution, item 2's remaining per-request steps against the middle source handler, the selected accept's steps after encapsulation on item 2's side and the assembly remain ("CE-11b unit U6, second part: open points"). An independent review (semantic and hygiene) of `7d47499c` found no error in the Lean statements or proofs; its findings concern the documentation (results stated without their premises, the central moves, the description of the checks, the plan for item 2's invariant and field reads), the receipt, the registry notes and 31 helper theorems without docstrings, and the review fix pass fixes them without changing a statement. A confirming review of the fix pass at `02484ae3` found every finding resolved; its two nits, that central had moved past `760f056e` before the fix pass's documentation commit and that the track's notes kept an older next-steps bullet, are addressed by the documentation commit that records it ("CE-11b unit U6, second part: confirming review") and by the integration's merge commit. Not integrated into central at the commit that records the review. Central moved during the session to `78fb56cd` (Codex's reviewed structural origin publication; no CE-11b file), then to `1f6ec6b8` (Codex's reviewed global binding publication; no CE-11b file), during the review to `760f056e` (CE-11b unit U3, integrated from `codex/deployment-ce11b`) and, before the fix pass's documentation commit, to `da8f4859` (Codex's reviewed temporal record origin publication: ten `PqxdhRecordFactsOriginPublication*` modules, no CE-11b file) and `cffd06c6` (Codex's reviewed native prelude publication: `PqxdhBoundaryRecordPrelude`, no CE-11b file), and after that commit to `1639476c` (Codex's reviewed receive execution publication: `PqxdhBoundaryRecordExecution`, no CE-11b file), then, during the integration, to `f9243f89` (Codex's reviewed origin accounting publication: 27 `PqxdhRecordFactsOrigin*` modules, no CE-11b file) and `485608c2` (Codex's reviewed send execution publication: `PqxdhBoundaryRecordSend`, no CE-11b file); the topic merged none of them before its integration, whose first merge commit `49498fe7` merges `1639476c` and whose second merge commit merges `485608c2` (open points). A confirming review of the fix pass at `c02d3caf` found all nine findings resolved and approved with one minor documentation finding (the stopping point of the planned anomaly guesser) and two nits, addressed by the documentation commit that records it ("CE-11b unit U4: confirming review"). Not integrated into central at this commit: central is at `39fd1c50` (since `3d212990`: Codex's record and boundary modules of `d281c804`, `3915c3f0` and `39fd1c50`; no CE-11b file), which the integration merges. The plain-English explanation is the section "Common deployment experiment, CE-11b unit U3 remainder and unit U4 (first part): frames and provenance of the ideal environment" of [the analysis](../formal-verification-analysis.md); the receipt is [proof-receipts/common-deployment-experiment-ce11b-u4.json](proof-receipts/common-deployment-experiment-ce11b-u4.json).

Status of CE-11b unit U4, remainder (track u4b): the first part was integrated into central at `97befa84`; this track was built over `97befa84` on branch `codex/deployment-ce11b`, with four Lean commits (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealBeaconFacts` at `db127f48`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealBeacon` at `1e868198`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealGuess` at `38cbfb1e`, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyBound` at `77bd00a9`; 73 guarded theorems) and one documentation commit (sections "CE-11b unit U4, remainder: ..." below). It proves the Beacon converse of C5 (L4, `ideal_beacon`), the full C5 restated (`ideal_slots`: `IdealSlots ∨ Anomaly` on every outcome of the annotated record world with `envGD`), its prefix-closed form (`ideal_slots_prefix`) and `anomaly_le` (the anomaly has probability at most `ctGuessPk`, hence at most `ctGuess`), under `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack` and with no new assumption. An independent review (semantic and hygiene) of `1217aa58` found no wrong statement and no hidden assumption. It recorded two disclosed scope gaps, the form of the anomaly bound with a constructed guesser that does not read the selected secret key (`anomaly_le_pk`, the form CE-12a's IND-CCA reduction consumes) and unit U8; a minor gap, no bound for the anomaly on a prefix of the transcript, the exceptional alternative of `ideal_slots_prefix`; central's unrecorded move; two linter suppressions that suppressed nothing; and documentation nits. The review fix pass fixes them with one Lean commit (`de2deeaa`: `anomaly_prefix_guess`, `guess_le_pk_sup`, `anomaly_prefix_le_pk_sup` and `anomaly_prefix_le`, the two suppressions removed; 77 guarded theorems; no statement changed or weakened) and one documentation commit ("CE-11b unit U4, remainder: review fix pass"). Unit U4 is not complete: `anomaly_le_pk` and its prefix form are this track's next step, in the new module `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyGuesser` (registered, not yet created), and unit U8 is a step of its own ("CE-11b unit U4, remainder: open points"). Central is at `dd681f34` (the integration of the third part of unit U6: the four modules `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPost{Requests,Server,Hidden,Finish}`, 1866 handwritten Lean files), which is not an ancestor of this branch: it was published at 00:58 (UTC+2) on 2026-10-10, before this track's first Lean commit (01:03), so "central had not moved" in the track's report was inaccurate. A trial merge (`git merge-tree`, no writes) of `de2deeaa` with `dd681f34` conflicts only at the anchors that both CE-11b tracks append to: in `check-inventory.sh` the handwritten Lean count, the digest rows and the module blocks; the root's import list; the end of `reviewed-inventory.txt`; the end of the analysis; and this guide's status paragraphs and section block (the plan snapshot merges cleanly). The handwritten Lean count is 1870 after the merge (1866 on both sides, four new files on each). The integration merges `dd681f34` (or a later central) first, with central's side first at every anchor, then reruns `coord.sh inv recount` and `inv rehash`, the focused Lean checks with a LEAN_PATH derived from the published one of the merged central, and the focused inventory. Not re-reviewed; not integrated.

## CE-11a increment 1: what is proved

Increment 1 of CE-11a (unit 11a-1: the erasure engine and the layout) and the spike that plan section 32 runs alongside it (the local finish erasure, the engine applied to it and the labelled-kernel identities) add three modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 67 guarded theorems; no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains three imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `LazyOracleDiscard` | 29 | The generic engine, with no protocol content. `LawEq` (two probabilistic programs have the same `𝒟` measure after every continuation into any measurable space) with `refl`, `symm`, `trans`, `of_eq`, `bind`, `map`, `evalDist_eq`, `lawEq_swap` and `lawEq_drop`. Lenses `StateLens` (`id`, `fst`, `snd`, `comp`, `lift`) and `CacheLens`; `complete` (the lazy query at a point with its answer dropped) with `complete_run`, `complete_of_none`, `complete_of_some`, `query_of_none`, `query_of_some`; `StateLens.complete`, `completeOpt`, `query`. `Commutes` (completing any point before a step has the same law as completing it after the step) and `Blind`, with `complete_query_swap` (the cache-level lazy-sampling swap), `commutes_pure`, `commutes_bind`, `commutes_map`, `commutes_frame`, `commutes_query`, `commutes_simulateQ`, `complete_comp`, `commutes_lift` and `blind_proj`. The pointed-form glue `factor_of_discard`, `erase_tail`, the global erasure `discard_erase` and `discard_erase_proj`, and `impl_project` (one step of `PqxdhRecordLiftLazyTable.impl` with its counts forgotten is VCVio's lazy oracle). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenLayout` | 6 | The 108-byte layout of a hidden slot: `Material` (key32, nonce12), `joinOutput`, `splitOutput`, `split_join`, `join_split`, `joinEquiv`, `joinOutput_next` (the next chain of a joined output is `seed.1`), `factor_join`, `uniform_split` (a uniform 108-byte output has, through every continuation, the law of uniform material followed by a uniform `PqxdhRecordLiftSeed.Seed`) and `material_bound` (drawing the material costs at most 44 uniform queries). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenKernels` | 32 | The spike. The receive normal form `reached` with `program_reached` and `reached_cases`; the labelled `Scope` with `sequence_mapChains`, `scope_native`, `begin_scope`, `resume_scope`, `reached_scope` and `reached_sequence` (an opening that the receive reaches carries the decoded sequence). The C7 local part: `SeqOne`, `factoredBody`, `factored_body`, `discardedInput`, `finishErased`, `normalFinish_reply`, `body_discard` and `finish_erase_local`. Its CTX-cache instance: `ctxLens`, `openPoint`, `discarded_native`, `NativeM`, `ctxRandomOracle_query`, `nativePrim_open`, `nativePrim_commutes`, `primLabels_commutes`, `factoredBody_commutes`, `finishErased_commutes`, `finish_pointed_native`, `FinishSpec`, `finishOracle`, `finishOracleErased`, `finishOraclePointed`, `pointed_erased`, `finish_erase_native` and `finish_erase_codec`. The labelled-kernel identities: `serverInitial`, `serverInitial_exact`, `serverInitial_native`, `send_native`, `initial_send_requested`, `resolve_after_send`, `receive_begin_request`, `beaconInitial_native`, `receive_native` and `resolve_after_receive`. |

The engine (`discard_erase`). Let `H` be a stateful handler and `H₁` a pointed handler: each step of `H₁` returns its answer and an optional point of a lazily sampled cache, reached through a lens `L`. If every step of `H` has, in law, the form of the step of `H₁` followed by the completion of the point it reports (`factor`), and every step of `H₁` with the point forgotten commutes with completions (`oblivious`), then for every oracle program, every initial state and every continuation that cannot observe completions (`Blind`), simulating `H` and simulating the erased handler `fun q => Prod.fst <$> H₁ q` have the same law. The proof is an induction on the program: the completion produced by a step is moved past the whole erased remainder, which commutes with it by `commutes_simulateQ`, and is then removed by the blind continuation. `discard_erase_proj` is the special case in which the continuation keeps the result and a projection of the state that does not see the cache. The core fact is `complete_query_swap`: completing a point and then making one lazy query has the same law as making the query and then completing the point; when the two points differ and are both absent, the two fresh draws are exchanged (`lawEq_swap`).

The layout. `joinOutput (key32, nonce12) (next32, right32)` reassembles the 76-byte response from key, next chain and nonce with the existing `RawCtxKdfRequestTable.assemble` and appends the opposite chain; `splitOutput` is its inverse. `uniform_split` transports the uniform measure along the bijection `joinEquiv` (VCVio's `evalDist_bind_bijective_uniform_cross` with `SampleableType.evalDist_uniformSample`), on the discrete measurable structure of the two finite types.

The local part of C7 (`finish_erase_local`). For every lawful monad, primitive interpreter, send callback, application getter, frame decoder, prepared registration and slot factor, `PqxdhServerAuthBeaconFactor.factored` equals `finishErased` followed by the call `prim (.inr input)` on the reported opening input, with its reply discarded. `finishErased` runs the original body when the decoded frame has sequence one; otherwise it makes no primitive call, returns `none` and reports the opening input that the receive would reach (`discardedInput`). The proof follows the original receive: on a blank raw, a failed decode or an invalid frame the body rejects without a call; on a valid frame every KDF request is answered by the selected step without effect (`program_reached`), the reached effect is a rejection or an opening (`reached_cases`), the opening's input carries the decoded sequence (`reached_sequence`), so the opened message, if any, has that sequence, and `PqxdhBeaconPhase2Publication.afterOpening` rejects every sequence other than one.

The CTX-cache instance. With the native interpreter `PqxdhServerAuthBeaconFactor.nativePrim`, the discarded call changes the shared primitive state only by completing the point `openPoint input` of the CTX cache (`discarded_native`), so the hidden finish has the pointed form (`finish_pointed_native`). Every native primitive query, every labelled receive query and the erased finish commute with completions of the CTX cache (`nativePrim_commutes`, `primLabels_commutes`, `factoredBody_commutes`, `finishErased_commutes`, the last given an application getter that commutes). `finish_erase_native` instantiates `discard_erase` on `ctxLens` for the interface `FinishSpec`: an adaptive client chooses prepared registrations and slot factors for hidden finishes and queries BLAKE2b on the same lazily sampled cache; the real interface `finishOracle` and the erased interface `finishOracleErased` have the same output law. `finish_erase_codec` is the case of the experiment codec's pure application getter and frame decoder, with no premise left.

The labelled-kernel identities. The factored send and finish run labelled ratchet kernels with a hidden slot and turn them into concrete kernels only at the end (`resolve`). The existing labelled-to-native bridges need a target chain whose step is the challenge, which a uniform 108-byte value does not have, so these two identities compute the resolved kernels directly. `resolve_after_send`: if the labelled first send of the initial Server kernel `serverInitial right` (hidden send slot, known receive chain) requests a KDF, then `PqxdhRecordLiftSeed.serverKernel (nextChain output, right) = ok (resolve …)`, where `resolve …` is the kernel advanced on the selected step of the 76-byte response `fullResponse output`, resolved; `initial_send_requested` shows that this first send always requests the hidden slot. `resolve_after_receive`: let `k` be the kernel with `PqxdhRecordLiftSeed.beaconKernel (nextChain output, right) = ok k` (the hypothesis `hk`; `k` is the World's `kernel .beacon` of that seed, by `PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.kernel_exact`). Then, for every primitive interpreter and every observation that ignores the kernel of a failed receive, observing the resolved kernel after the labelled sequence-one receive from `PqxdhServerAuthBeaconFactor.initial right` is the same program as observing `k`. Both are proved by transport to the extracted computations `PqxdhRecordLiftSeed.sendOne_from_counters` and `receiveOne_from_counters`: with the hidden slot read as the unused chain, the labelled initial kernels resolve to the native zero-counter kernels (`serverInitial_native`, `beaconInitial_native`), and the extracted first send and sequence-one receive follow the labelled admission and resume (`send_native`, `receive_native`).

## CE-11a increment 1: decisions and deviations

- `LawEq` binds the continuation's result type explicitly: `∀ (β : Type) [MeasurableSpace β] (k : α → ProbComp β), …` instead of the implicit `∀ {β : Type} …` of the CE-11 design. With an implicit leading binder Lean instantiates `β` and its measurable space as soon as a `LawEq` proof is elaborated as a term, so `have h := …` and dot notation on `LawEq` proofs fail with stuck instance problems; downstream users would meet the same problem. The two forms state the same proposition.
- Helper theorems beyond the design list: `complete_of_none`, `complete_of_some`, `query_of_none`, `query_of_some`, `complete_query_swap`, `complete_comp` and `erase_tail` in the engine (35 increment-1 theorems instead of 28), and 22 helpers and instance theorems in the spike module (32 instead of the 10 theorems of the design's spike list).
- The spike went directly into the module `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenKernels`, which the design names as the 11a-2 module of the kernel identities, instead of a scratch file that 11a-2 would move. Its design statements are unchanged. The toy instance S1 was not built: `finish_erase_native` validates the engine on the real hidden finish instead.
- The CTX-cache instance and the native obliviousness lemmas (`finishErased_commutes` and the lemmas before it) were not in the spike list. They are the native-interpreter forms of two lemmas that the design plans for 11a-2, over the bare `SharedState Unit` instead of the `Services` wrapper of item 2's entry simulation: `finishErased_commutes_ctx`, the obliviousness of the erased hidden finish with respect to the CTX cache, and `finishFactor_pointed`, the pointed form of the hidden finish wrapper that the engine's `factor` premise needs.
- `finish_pointed_native` repeats the two cases of `factor_of_discard` instead of applying it: its left-hand side comes from `finish_erase_local`, whose `match` is a different auxiliary definition from the one in `factor_of_discard`, and Lean did not unify the two.
- Basis: central `d68e1ce4` instead of `7609c3c8`, at which the design was checked. The commits between them add only `PqxdhRecordLiftMaterialTable`, `PqxdhRecordLiftOrigins` and `PqxdhRecordLiftProjectionCut`, so every name and line that the design cites is unchanged.
- Documentation placement: the design named a new guide `doc/impl/deployment-ideal-world.md` and one receipt `doc/impl/proof-receipts/common-deployment-experiment-ce11a.json` with an entry per unit. This increment instead adds its sections to this guide, which the cooperation protocol with Codex names as the guide of handoff item 3, and writes one receipt per increment, `common-deployment-experiment-ce11a-inc1.json`, as the other CE stages do. The choice has no effect on any statement.

## CE-11a increment 1: names for consumers

- Engine (offered to Codex as well, for its own lazy-table erasures): `LazyOracleDiscard.LawEq`, `StateLens`, `CacheLens`, `complete`, `StateLens.complete`, `completeOpt`, `query`, `Commutes`, `Blind`, `commutes_query`, `commutes_simulateQ`, `commutes_lift`, `commutes_frame`, `factor_of_discard`, `discard_erase`, `discard_erase_proj`, `impl_project`. `LazyOracleDiscard` is not a `PqxdhDeployment*` module, so a `PqxdhRecordLift*` module may import it.
- Layout: `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenLayout.Material`, `joinOutput`, `splitOutput`, `joinEquiv`, `joinOutput_next`, `factor_join`, `uniform_split`, `material_bound`.
- C7 local part and its instance: `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenKernels.finish_erase_local`, `finishErased`, `discardedInput`, `SeqOne`, `openPoint`, `ctxLens`, `discarded_native`, `finish_pointed_native`, `finishErased_commutes`, `finish_erase_native`, `finish_erase_codec`.
- Labelled-kernel identities: `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenKernels.serverInitial`, `initial_send_requested`, `resolve_after_send`, `resolve_after_receive`; the sequence of a reached opening: `reached_sequence`.
- Use these short names qualified: `complete`, `query`, `lift`, `comp`, `id`, `fst`, `snd`, `Commutes`, `Material`, `reached`, `Scope`, `begin_scope`, `resume_scope`, `send_native`, `receive_native` and `FinishSpec`. Other central modules declare the same short names, for example `complete` in twenty modules, `Scope`, `begin_scope` and `resume_scope` in `RawCtxOwnerProvenance`, `RawCtxSelectedReceiveScope` and `RawCtxSelectedOpenScope`, `send_native` and `receive_native` in `RawCtxInterpretedSend`, `RawCtxInterpretedReceive` and `RawCtxChainRealization`, `Commutes` in `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerCompile`, `FinishSpec` in `PqxdhPhase2LiveFinish` and `Material` in `PqxdhOrderedChainHybrids`.

## CE-11a increment 1: contracts used

None. The engine and the layout use only VCVio's lazy random oracle and uniform sampling; the spike uses the existing native primitive interpreter, the existing extracted computations and no codec law. No new assumption or contract.

## CE-11a increment 1: open points

- 11a-2 (planned names): the hidden build and finish wrappers with the types of the fields of item 2's `PqxdhServerAuthEntrySimulation.Services`, and their pointed form `finishFactor_pointed`; `build_factor_split` and `finish_factor_split`, which state that the factored build and finish equal split forms that use only the material and receive the World's seed kernel (they use `resolve_after_send` and `resolve_after_receive`); `gameWith`, the native game with a registration handler chosen from the constructed Server; the obliviousness of the real build and finish with respect to the CTX cache of that game's state `XState`; `erase_game` and `erase_later`, C7 for that game without and with the later-quantum post phase; `represent` and `defer`, the 108-byte split at the first use of a slot and the deferral of the seed draw to the first hidden install; `finish_accounting`, at most 51 chain-step derivations and at most one opening per finish-time receive; and `erased_le`, the erased finish makes no more calls of any kind than the real one and none on a frame whose sequence is not one.
- `finish_erase_native` is an instance over a deployment-shaped interface, not over `CE.idealGame g`; C7 for `CE.idealGame g` closes in 11b-1.
- Item-7 production-boundary note: C7's premise is the sequence-one gate that the Rust reference implementation of this repository revision applies after the finish-time receive (`beaconcrypt/src/beacon.rs:459-470`, the gate at `:468-470`) and that the Lean model applies in `PqxdhBeaconPhase2Publication.afterOpening`; a Rust copy without that gate (HB-95) is outside C7's premise.
- Reviewed at `5ea61553` (APPROVE_WITH_NITS from both lenses); the review fix pass is not reviewed yet; not integrated.

## CE-11a increment 1: re-estimate

The CE-11 design planned 3-5 agent-days for 11a-1 and 3 for the spike, counted in 11a-2's 13-23, within 27-48 agent-days for CE-11a and 17-32 for CE-11b (plan section 32). Both were done in one working session, under one agent-day. The remaining 11a-2 work is estimated at 9-18 agent-days (10-20 without the spike in the design), because the native obliviousness of the hidden finish is done. CE-11a moves from 27-48 to about 21-39 agent-days (one spent, 11a-2 9-18, 11a-3 3-6, 11a-4 5-9, documentation and review 3-5); CE-11b is unchanged at 17-32.

## CE-11a increment 1: checks

Every module was checked on its own with Lean 4.34 (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) through `coord.sh lean claude` into the fresh scratch prefix `/tmp/bc-arc-scratch/common-experiment/ce11/olean`, a resolved symlink mirror (6027 links) of the first prefix of the published `LEAN_PATH` of central `d68e1ce4`, in front of that published `LEAN_PATH`; each check exited 0 with an empty log. The root import `BeaconcryptCore.lean` was checked the same way at each Lean commit (exit 0, empty log), and the focused `make -C beaconcrypt-core check-inventory` ran under `coord.sh lease mem claude @nix` in the minimal proof flake at each Lean commit (exit 0). `git diff --check` is clean. Not run: `make verify`, a full `lake build`, mutation testing and `nix-collect-garbage`. The receipt is [proof-receipts/common-deployment-experiment-ce11a-inc1.json](proof-receipts/common-deployment-experiment-ce11a-inc1.json).

## CE-11a increment 1: review fix pass

An independent review of `5ea61553` with a semantic and a hygiene lens returned APPROVE_WITH_NITS from both lenses. The fix pass is one commit. It changes no statement, adds or removes no theorem and adds no assumption; the module and statement counts above are unchanged. Per finding:

- Semantic, minor: the analysis implied that C7 for the ideal deployment game needs only the remaining CE-11a steps. Its last paragraph on this increment now says that the ideal game also needs item 2's ideal registration, so that C7 for it closes in CE-11b (11b-1), as the open points above say.
- Semantic, nit: the module docstring of `LazyOracleDiscard` said that the engine uses no independence premise, which undersold the hypotheses of `discard_erase`. It now says that `factor`, `oblivious` and `blind` are obligations on the given handler, pointed handler and continuation, proved for each instance, and that no probabilistic independence or noninterference assumption is taken.
- Semantic, nit: the paragraph on the labelled-kernel identities called `beaconKernel (nextChain output, right)` a fixed kernel, although it is a `RustM` result. It now names the kernel `k` with `beaconKernel (nextChain output, right) = ok k` and states the Server identity in the form `serverKernel … = ok (resolve …)` that Lean proves.
- Semantic, nit: the documentation placement differed from the design without being listed; it is now a deviation above and in the receipt.
- Hygiene, nit: the receipt's `topic.remote_head` named the spike commit, not the pushed tip. The field is removed; the receipt lists the commits it covers in `commits` and describes the commit that adds it in `commits_note`, as the other CE receipts do.
- Hygiene, nit: the analysis cited "central `beaconcrypt/src/beacon.rs:459-470`". It now names the Rust reference implementation of this repository revision, whose `beaconcrypt/src/beacon.rs` is byte-identical to central's; the production-boundary note above says the same.
- Hygiene, nit: the sections of this increment cited sections of the CE-11 design and of its critique, which exist only as scratch files. They now state the content they rely on and cite plan section 32 for the CE-11 split, order and estimate; the plan is snapshotted as `doc/impl/common-deployment-experiment-plan.md` on central since `84266ed4`.
- Hygiene, nit: `lawEq_swap`, `lawEq_drop` and `uniform_split` used VCVio's deprecated discrete-distribution interface with the deprecation linter disabled. All three now use only measure-level lemmas, and no module of this increment disables a linter. `lawEq_drop` uses `OracleComp.evalDist_bind_const`. `lawEq_swap` is proved by induction over both programs with `ProbComp.inductionOn`: each step of a probabilistic program is a uniform draw from a `Fin` type, two such draws are exchanged by `evalDist_bind_bind_swap_of_countable`, and `evalDist_bind_congr` carries the induction hypothesis under a draw, so the theorem keeps its statement for arbitrary result types, which VCVio's swap lemmas alone do not give. `uniform_split` transports the uniform measure along `joinEquiv` with `evalDist_bind_bijective_uniform_cross` and `SampleableType.evalDist_uniformSample` and no longer uses `RawCtxKdfRequestTable.full_partition`.
- The remaining hygiene findings concern the coordination with Codex and the later integration into central, not the proofs or this text; the receipt records them.

Checks of the fix pass. The three modules were rechecked in import order, and the root import `BeaconcryptCore.lean` after them, with Lean 4.34 (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) through `coord.sh lean claude` into a second fresh scratch prefix `/tmp/bc-arc-scratch/common-experiment/ce11/olean-fix1`, built like the first (a resolved symlink mirror, 6027 links, of the first prefix of the published `LEAN_PATH` of central `d68e1ce4`, in front of that published `LEAN_PATH`); each check exited 0 with an empty log, so every exact guard holds. `coord.sh inv recount` left the count unchanged, `coord.sh inv rehash` updated the rows of the two changed modules, the focused `make -C beaconcrypt-core check-inventory` under `coord.sh lease mem claude @nix` exited 0, and `git diff --check` is clean.

## CE-11a increment 2: what is proved

Increment 2 of CE-11a (unit 11a-2 of the CE-11 split of plan section 32 and the first part of unit 11a-2b, without the parts listed under "open points" below) adds five modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 137 guarded theorems, six of them added by the review fix pass; no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains five imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenEntry` | 31 | The hidden-entry wrappers with the field types of item 2's `PqxdhServerAuthEntryFactor.Services` (`HiddenBuild`, `HiddenAfter`): the factor forms `buildFactor` and `finishFactor` at `joinOutput m seed`, identified with the fields of `factorServices` by `buildFactor_services` and `finishFactor_services` (both `rfl`); the pointed and erased finish `finishPointed`, `finishErasedSvc` with `finishFactor_pointed`; the service instances `factorSvc` and `erasedSvc` with `erasedSvc_fields`. The CTX-obliviousness toolkit, a named lemma for each step so that the obliviousness premises of the erasure are proved rather than assumed: `openingImpl_hash`, `nativeImpl_commutes` (every request of the shared native primitive interpreter commutes with completions of the CTX cache), `compose_commutes`, `registrationImpl_commutes`, `tailAux_commutes`, `nativeTail_commutes`, `tailImpl_commutes`, `continueRust_commutes`, `afterInitialization_commutes`, `candidate_commutes`, `genericBuild_commutes`, and for the wrappers `buildFactor_commutes_ctx`, `finishFactor_commutes_ctx`, `finishErased_commutes_ctx`, `finishPointed_commutes_ctx`. The accounting of plan section 25: `requestCalls`, `finishRequests`, `factoredBody_requests`, `requests_bound`, `romPrim`, `nativePrim_rom`, `opening_hash_bound`, `simulateQ_total_of_boundP`, `isQueryBoundP_of_bind_left`, `finish_hash_bound`, `finish_accounting`, `erased_le`, `erased_total_le`, `erased_reject`, `finishErasedSvc_reject`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentBackend` | 47 | The extended state `XState` (deployment state `base`, `backend`, hidden-seed table `seeds`, LQ-F snapshot `snap` and ephemeral `log`), `RegImpl`, the lenses `baseLens`, `nativeLens`, `scheduleLens`, `gameLens`, `gameCtxLens`, `scheduleCtxLens`, `nativeCtxLens`, `ctxLens`, `seedLens` (`ctxLens_get`), `handlerWith`, `xinitial`, `gameWith` (the registration handler is chosen after the Server is constructed, from the constructed Server), `realReg`, `laterView`, `realStep_view`, `gameWith_real` and `gameWith_classical`. The generic tools `commutes_focus`, `commutes_dispatch`, `commutes_lift_comp`. Obliviousness of the fixed steps: `nativeHash_query`, `primitives_commutes`, `record_commutes`, `serverStep_commutes`, `beaconStep_commutes`, `fixed_commute_ctx`, `handlerWith_commutes_ctx`, `onBase_seed`, `fixed_commute_seed`, `handlerWith_commutes_seed`. Obliviousness of the real registration: the frame predicate `GameFrame` with `GameFrame.commutes`, `frame_pure`, `frame_bind`, `frame_local`, `frame_family`, `nativeGet_bind`, `nativeCoins_bind`, `nativeSet_run_bind`, `coins_frame`, `sourceRoot_frame`, `romRoot_frame`, `nativeClient_frame`, `tail_eq`, `acceptRest_frame`, `tail_frame`, `acceptLogged_frame`, `nativeHmac_frame`; `beaconNative_commutes`, `callbacks_commutes`, `finish_commutes`, `nativeBuild_commutes`; `realReg_commutes_of`, `realBuild_commutes_ctx`, `realFinish_commutes_ctx`, `realCreate_commutes_ctx`, `realBundle_commutes_ctx`, `realHmac_commutes_ctx`, `realAccept_commutes_ctx`, `realReg_commutes_ctx` and `realHandler_commutes_ctx`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentFinishErasure` | 20 | Pointed registration handlers `PointedReg L` for any lens `L` onto a lazily sampled cache of `XState`, with `PointedReg.erased`, `PointedReg.ofCommuting` and `PointedReg.ofCommuting_erased`; `factor_focus` and `oblivious_focus` (a pointed step on a sub-state is a pointed step of the whole state); `factor_prefix`, `oblivious_prefix`, `factor_suffix` and `oblivious_suffix` (a pointed step after a prefix, and a pointed step followed by a suffix that commutes with completions); `pointedWith` with `pointedWith_erased`, `pointedWith_factor`, `pointedWith_oblivious`; `outcomeMap` and the run-phase erasure `erase_game`; `xreveal`, `laterWith`, `laterTail`, `laterTail_blind` and the later-game erasure `erase_later`; the CTX instance (C7) `FinishPair`, `fullX`, `fullX_set`, `erase_game_ctx`, `xreveal_ctx`, `erase_later_ctx`; the real instance `simulateQ_lift`, `realPair`, `realPost`, `realPost_commutes` and `laterWith_real`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentSeedSplit` | 15 | `LawEq.bind_support` and the invariant-gated transport `lawEq_run_simulateQ_of_proj`; `OutputSpec`, `MaterialSpec`, `SlotService`, `tableService` (item 2's S7 table), `lazyService`, `splitService`, `ServiceProj` with `table_service_proj`, `BothOrNeither`, `joinTable`, `joinTable_cacheQuery`, `splitService_none`, `splitService_some`, `split_service_proj`; `mapBackend`, `CoreNatural`, `fixed_mapBackend`, `fixed_backend`, `represent`, `represent_split`; `seedFree`, `seedFree_set`, the generic seed deferral `defer`, its later-game form `defer_later` and `realPost_commutes_seed`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenSplit` | 24 | The split forms and their kernel identities (unit 11a-2b), stated as equations in which the World's kernel of the seed replaces a placeholder kernel and a failed seal publishes nothing: `dummySeed`, `placeholderKernel`, `withKernel`, `finishSplit`, `finish_factor_split`, `finishSplit_commutes_ctx`, `setPeerKernel`, `replaceRaw`, `buildSplit`, `build_factor_split`, `buildSplit_commutes_ctx`; the independence of the first seal and the sequence-one opening from the next and opposite chains: `unused`, `material_assemble`, `decode_full`, `step_material`, `sequence_one_shape`, `opening_unused`, `reply_unused`, `seal_unused`, `send_seal`; the kernels: `beaconKernel_seed`, `serverKernel_seed`, `afterOpening_kernel`, `withKernel_withKernel`, `body_split`, `erased_split`, `publish_setPeerKernel`, `afterInitialization_kernel`, `candidate_kernel`, `genericBuild_kernel`, `build_split_raw`, `replaceRaw_replaceRaw`. |

The wrappers. On a hidden entry, item 2 (its plan, `doc/impl/extract-composition-plan.md`, section 18) instantiates its two hidden services with the factored Server build and the factored Beacon tail at the entry's 108-byte value. `buildFactor m seed` and `finishFactor m seed` are those services at `joinOutput m seed`, the 108 bytes assembled from the environment material `m` (key32, nonce12) and the world seed (next32, right32), and they are literally the fields of `PqxdhServerAuthEntryFactor.factorServices`. `finishPointed` runs `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenKernels.finishErased` with the native primitives, and `finishFactor_pointed` states, as an equation of programs on the shared primitive state, that the factor-form finish is the pointed finish followed by the completion of the CTX point of the opening it discarded; this is the `factor` premise of the engine for a hidden finish.

Obliviousness. Each step has a named obliviousness lemma, so that the premises of the erasures are proved for the fixed steps and the real registration, not assumed. `nativeImpl_commutes` is the basic fact: in the shared native primitive interpreter the KDF and the base opening are pure and the BLAKE2b request is exactly the lazy query on the focused CTX cache, so every request commutes in law with completing any point of the cache (`LazyOracleDiscard.commutes_query`). Every interpreter built from it by an adapter commutes as well (`compose_commutes`), in particular the record primitives, the registration interpreter of the Server's response tail and the logged response tail, whose seal log is a frame of the cache. The generic Server build commutes whenever its send and auxiliary callbacks do (`genericBuild_commutes`, by its structure), so the factor-form build commutes with completions of the CTX cache of its seal state (`buildFactor_commutes_ctx`). In the native game, coins, BLAKE2b and the four record commands commute with completions of the CTX cache and of the seed table whatever the registration handler (`fixed_commute_ctx`, `fixed_commute_seed`), so the whole handler commutes whenever the registration handler does (`handlerWith_commutes_ctx`, `handlerWith_commutes_seed`). For the real registration all six commands commute (`realReg_commutes_ctx`): the build runs the logged response tail on the shared cache (`nativeBuild_commutes`), the finish runs its callbacks, including the finish-time receive, on the shared cache (`finish_commutes`), and creation, bundle, raw accept and HMAC are frames of the cache (`GameFrame`). The proofs use two generic tools: `commutes_focus` (a step that runs an inner program on a sub-state, where everything else it reads and writes is a frame of the cache, commutes when the inner program does) and `commutes_dispatch` (a step that first reads a frame of the cache commutes when every branch does).

The native game. `gameWith init reg` constructs the Server with the actual constructor and a generated seed, then runs the wrapped adversary (`PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.wrap`, the secrecy layer as a program) against `handlerWith (reg created)` from `xinitial created (init created)`; the registration handler and the backend's initial value are chosen from the constructed Server. The real registration handler `realReg` answers every registration command as the LQ-F run-phase handler does (the logged native accept, the native handler otherwise) and captures the snapshot before a finish. `gameWith_real` shows that the game with `realReg` is exactly the LQ-F run phase `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerGame.runPhase`, jointly over the Server's public data, the output, the ledger, the snapshot, the deployment state, the log and the runtime state, and `gameWith_classical` that, with the backend, the seed table and the LQ-F ghosts forgotten, it is exactly the classical experiment `PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.game`.

C7 in generic form. A pointed registration handler for a lens `L` onto a lazily sampled cache of `XState` pairs a handler `reg` with a pointed handler whose every step reports an optional point of the cache: each command of `reg` is, in law, its pointed step followed by the completion of the reported point (`factor`), and every pointed step commutes with completions (`oblivious`). `erase_game` applies `LazyOracleDiscard.discard_erase_proj` to the whole native game: when the fixed steps commute with completions of the cache, the games with `reg` and with the erased handler have the same law of the Server's public data, the output, the ledger and every projection of the final state that does not see the cache. `erase_later` is the same for the LQ-F later game `laterWith`, with a pluggable post-phase handler that commutes with completions (pluggable because in the ideal game the post-phase HMAC is answered from the backend's R1 tables, not by the native HMAC) and a reveal that does not see the cache; the post phase is moved after the completion by `commutes_simulateQ` and the completion is then dropped by the projection (`laterTail_blind`). The CTX instance `FinishPair` needs nothing more (`erase_game_ctx`, `erase_later_ctx`), and compares final states with the CTX cache emptied (`fullX`). The real registration handler is a pair with no point (`realPair`), and `laterWith_real` shows that the later game with `realReg` and the real post phase `realPost` is exactly the LQ-F game `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerGame.game`. Pointed steps compose: `factor_prefix` and `oblivious_prefix` put a prefix in front of a family of pointed steps (the pointed form needs nothing of the prefix, the obliviousness needs it to commute with completions), and `factor_suffix` and `oblivious_suffix` put a suffix that commutes with completions after a pointed step, which passes its point on. With `factor_focus` and `oblivious_focus` they give the pointed form of a step made of stateful, probabilistic steps, a focused pointed step and a commuting step after it, the shape expected for the ideal hidden finish of CE-11b (owner lookup, preparation coins, slot-service touch and root derivation before the hidden tail, and a commit after it).

The split and the deferral. `split_service_proj` is the 108-byte split at the first touch of a hidden slot: from a state in which material and seed are present together, the service that draws material and seed lazily is, in law, the lazy service on the joined table (`PqxdhDeploymentHiddenLayout.uniform_split`). `table_service_proj` shows that item 2's S7 table with its counts forgotten is the lazy service (`LazyOracleDiscard.impl_project`). For a registration core that reads slot outputs only through its service (`CoreNatural`), `represent` transports the whole game along a service projection with `lawEq_run_simulateQ_of_proj`, and `represent_split` compares the table and the split representations through the lazy one. `defer` is the seed deferral in generic form: an instance of `erase_game` at the hidden-seed lens, whose fixed steps commute by `fixed_commute_seed`. `defer_later` is the same deferral for the later game, an instance of `erase_later` at the hidden-seed lens; its premise that the post phase commutes with seed completions holds for the real post phase (`realPost_commutes_seed`), and the LQ-F reveal does not read the seed table.

The split forms and their kernel identities (unit 11a-2b). The factor-form hidden build and the erased hidden finish read the world seed (next32, right32) only through the kernel they publish or stage, and that kernel is the record world's kernel of the seed. The unerased factor-form finish does read the seed: on a frame whose sequence is 2 to 51 its receive derives chain steps from next32 and opens with the result before the sequence-one check rejects the frame, so the seed deferral applies only after the C7 erasure. `buildSplit m` and `finishSplit m` run the same programs on the material `m` with a fixed seed and publish or stage `placeholderKernel`; `build_factor_split` states that `buildFactor m seed` is `buildSplit m` with the published peer's kernel replaced by `PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.kernel .server seed` (`replaceRaw`, which acts only on a successful build, so a failed seal publishes nothing), and `finish_factor_split` that `finishErasedSvc m seed` is `finishSplit m` with the staged kernel replaced by `PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.kernel .beacon seed`. The proofs read every chain label as the unused chain: the first send of the hidden Server and the sequence-one receive of the hidden Beacon make one key derivation on the hidden slot, whose material (key32, nonce12) does not depend on the next chain (`decode_full`, `material_assemble`), and through the native admission and continuation the seal and the opening depend only on that material and on fields that hold no chain (`seal_unused`, `opening_unused`), so the sealed record and the opened reply are the same programs for every seed (`send_seal`, `reply_unused`). The kernels come from `resolve_after_send` and `resolve_after_receive` with `PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.kernel_exact`; on the Server side, `genericBuild_kernel` shows that two builds whose first sends are the same seal differ only in the kernel they publish.

The accounting of plan section 25. `finishRequests` is the finish-time receive of a hidden finish with its key-derivation and opening requests left as queries, and `factoredBody_requests` shows that the finish body runs exactly these requests under the labelled primitive interpreter. `finish_accounting` states, for every raw input, prepared registration and slot value: at most 51 chain-step KDF requests (`RawCtxChainReceiveBounds.receive_bound`), at most one opening request (`program_open_bound`), and, with the real HKDF and the BLAKE2b opening left as a CTX-oracle query (`romPrim`; `nativePrim_rom` shows that the native interpreter is this interpreter with BLAKE2b answered by the lazily sampled cache), at most one BLAKE2b query for the whole hidden finish (`finish_hash_bound`). `erased_le` and `erased_total_le` show that under any query-counting primitive interpreter the erased finish issues no more queries of any kind than the factor-form finish, and `erased_reject` that on a raw whose decoded sequence is not one it calls no primitive at all, in any monad; `finishErasedSvc_reject` is its native form, which leaves the shared state unchanged.

## CE-11a increment 2: decisions and deviations

- Scope: the capability-form costs `buildSplit_env_cost` and `finishSplit_env_cost`, the single-install lemmas `hidden_server_once` and `hidden_beacon_once`, the seed-lens obliviousness of the split build and finish as steps of the extended state, the move of the split service's seed table into `XState.seeds` and the step `split_game` (the C7-erased factor form equals the split form) are not in this increment; they are listed under "open points". The costs and single-install lemmas are stated in the capability form of the environment of unit 11a-3, and the seed move and `split_game` are stated for a registration core whose hidden build and finish are these wrappers, the shape of item 2's ideal registration.
- The split forms and their kernel identities are in the new module `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenSplit`. The CE-11 design (summarized in plan section 32) planned them inside `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenEntry` and `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenKernels`, but both modules were committed before this work started, and a new module keeps them unchanged. The split finish is defined from the erased finish at a fixed seed with the placeholder staged, and the split build from the factor-form build at a fixed seed with the placeholder published, rather than as separate programs; `build_factor_split` and `finish_factor_split` state, for these definitions, the planned equations: the factor form equals the split form with the placeholder replaced by the World's kernel of the seed, and a failed seal publishes nothing.
- `realReg` takes the record router `route` besides the setup, codec and checks, because it answers registration commands through the LQ-F run-phase handler `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerGame.handler`, whose type includes the router; registration commands never use it.
- The registration handler's backend type is named `Bk`, because `Σ`, the name the CE-11 design used, is reserved notation in Lean.
- `gameWith` is defined directly on the wrapped adversary (`PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.wrap`), so the theorem `gameWith_wrap` that the CE-11 design planned (the game is the wrapped adversary run against the handler) holds by definition and is not a separate theorem.
- The CE-11 design planned whole-program BLAKE2b factorings `build_hash_factor` and `finish_hash_factor` (the build and the finish rewritten so that their BLAKE2b requests are queries to a separate lazily sampled oracle), with per-request obliviousness as the fallback if the factoring proved costly. The factorings were not built: the obliviousness of the build and the finish is proved per primitive request (`nativeImpl_commutes` and its compositions), the fallback, which gives the same obliviousness lemmas. The capability-form factoring, in which the environment's BLAKE2b request becomes the world's `.hash` capability, belongs to the per-command environment steps of unit 11a-3.
- `PointedReg` is generic in the lens; `FinishPair` is its CTX instance. `erase_game` and `erase_later` take any projection of the final state that does not see the cache; the CTX instances use the state with the CTX cache emptied. `defer` is the same theorem at the hidden-seed lens.
- `FinishPair.ofHidden`, the single constructor of the C7 pair from a hidden finish that the CE-11 design planned, is provided as building blocks: `factor_focus` and `oblivious_focus` turn the pointed hidden finish `finishFactor_pointed` with `finishPointed_commutes_ctx` into a pointed step of the whole state, and (since the review fix pass) `factor_prefix`, `oblivious_prefix`, `factor_suffix` and `oblivious_suffix` add the steps before and after it; the constructor itself needs the ideal registration of CE-11b.
- `splitService` keeps material and seed in the backend's service state, as the CE-11 design specifies for the split service. Moving the seed table into `XState.seeds`, so that `defer` applies to the split game, is left to the remainder of 11a-2b.
- The projection under which the CE-11 design compares the final states for C7 (every coordinate except the CTX cache, named `fullX` there) is `PqxdhDeploymentFinishErasure.fullX`, the final state with the CTX cache emptied, read through `outcomeMap`.
- The receipt lists the theorems that the CE-11 design named (`design_statements`) and the helper and instance theorems beyond them (`helper_and_instance_theorems`).
- Basis: `claude/ce11a` at `1749e215`, which equals central `1749e215`; central moved to `764e0e24` during this increment (two record-lift integrations by Codex), and the branch was not merged, which belongs to the integration step. At the review fix pass central is at `f20cb4b0`: since `1749e215` it adds 13 Lean files of the record-lift line and changes, among existing files, only the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean`, `check-inventory.sh`, `reviewed-inventory.txt` and the analysis, so no module that this increment imports changed; the merge needs the usual resolution of these four shared files. At the confirming-review nit fix central is at `8b674fa4`: since `f20cb4b0` it adds two more Lean files of the record-lift line (`PqxdhRecordLiftCtxOracle` at `7045f0f6` and `PqxdhRecordLiftWorldProvenance` at `8b674fa4`, so 15 since `1749e215`) and changes, among existing files, the same four shared files, so still no module that this increment imports changed.

## CE-11a increment 2: names for consumers

- For Codex (the C7 engine and local lemma at the interface boundary): `LazyOracleDiscard.discard_erase`, `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenKernels.finish_erase_local`, `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenEntry.finish_accounting` and `erased_le`, `PqxdhDeploymentFinishErasure.erase_game` and `erase_later` (generic) with the CTX instances `erase_game_ctx` and `erase_later_ctx`. C7 for the ideal deployment game itself closes in CE-11b (11b-1). The confirmation in capability form, that the hidden wrappers use only `.hash` (at most one), `installServer keyId identity (.hidden j)`, `installBeacon owner shell (.hidden j)` and `advanceOwner owner .aborted` and read no seed or kernel, needs the environment of unit 11a-3 and arrives there as `buildSplit_env_cost` and `finishSplit_env_cost`; this increment gives the parts that do not need the environment: at most one BLAKE2b query per hidden finish (`finish_hash_bound`) and split forms that take no seed (`buildSplit`, `finishSplit`).
- Wrappers for item 2's S1b: `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenEntry.HiddenBuild`, `HiddenAfter`, `buildFactor`, `finishFactor`, `finishPointed`, `finishErasedSvc`, `factorSvc`, `erasedSvc`, `finishFactor_pointed`; split forms: `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenSplit.buildSplit`, `finishSplit`, `placeholderKernel`, `replaceRaw`, `withKernel`, `build_factor_split`, `finish_factor_split`.
- Native game: `PqxdhDeploymentBackend.XState`, `RegImpl`, `handlerWith`, `gameWith`, `realReg`, `ctxLens`, `seedLens`, `gameWith_real`, `gameWith_classical`, `fixed_commute_ctx`, `fixed_commute_seed`, `realReg_commutes_ctx`, `commutes_focus`, `commutes_dispatch`.
- Split and deferral for CE-11b: `PqxdhDeploymentSeedSplit.SlotService`, `tableService`, `splitService`, `ServiceProj`, `CoreNatural`, `represent`, `represent_split`, `defer`, `defer_later`, `realPost_commutes_seed`, `lawEq_run_simulateQ_of_proj`; for the pointed ideal hidden finish: `PqxdhDeploymentFinishErasure.factor_focus`, `oblivious_focus`, `factor_prefix`, `oblivious_prefix`, `factor_suffix`, `oblivious_suffix`.
- Use these short names qualified: `ctxLens` (also in `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenKernels`, as the CTX lens of the shared primitive state), `sharedLens`, `realReg`, `realPost`, `xreveal`, `laterWith`, `represent`, `defer`, `GameFrame`, `tail_eq`, `tail_frame` and `LawEq.bind_support`.

## CE-11a increment 2: contracts used

None. The modules use the existing native primitive interpreters, the existing extracted programs and VCVio's lazy random oracle and uniform sampling, and no codec law. The premises of the generic theorems (`factor`, `oblivious`, the commutation of a post phase, `CoreNatural`, `ServiceProj`) are obligations on the given handlers, proved for each instance; none is an assumption of a published theorem, and no new assumption or contract is added.

## CE-11a increment 2: open points

- Unit 11a-2b, remainder (its first part, the split forms and the kernel identities, is done): the split build and finish as steps of the extended state that install the World's kernel by a lazy lookup of `XState.seeds` at the slot, their seed-lens obliviousness, the move of the split service's seed table into `XState.seeds`, and `split_game` (the C7-erased factor-form handler equals the split-form handler, from `build_factor_split` and `finish_factor_split`). These are stated for a registration core that calls the hidden-entry wrappers at a slot, the shape of item 2's ideal registration, and may move into CE-11b's 11b-1 once that core exists. They start with the re-check of the next point, and the remainder also covers the split form of the accept that the re-check requires. Estimate 2-4 agent-days (1-3 before the confirming-review nit fix; one day added for the split form of the accept and its seed-lens obliviousness).
- Re-check before the remainder of 11a-2b, from item 2's increment 2 (item 2 plan section 20, committed in `doc/impl/extract-composition-plan.md` on branch `claude/extract-composition` at `feb46e22`, not yet on central). Item 2's built registration skeleton (S1b) reads the Server's hidden 108-byte value at the private root request of the selected raw accept (`PqxdhRegistrationJointKemPostSkeleton.resumeTail`) and keeps it in its marks until the build of that session, because the source derives the Server root at the accept, as the Rust Server does (`get_shared_secret` in `beaconcrypt/src/server.rs` calls `derive_root_key_input` before the response is built). So a Server slot is first touched at the selected accept, not at the hidden build that this increment and the CE-11 design expected; a Beacon slot is still first touched at its finish. The skeleton also keys its hidden reads by 128-byte coordinates, not by slots, so a first-touch allocator from coordinates to slots sits in front of `PqxdhRecordLiftLazyTable` (a new coordinate gets the next slot; equal coordinates, such as the Server's and those of the matching Beacon finish, share one slot); `CoreNatural` has to account for the allocator's state, and the allocator's map is the map from hidden coordinate to slot of C5. Before the split steps of the previous point, three things are re-checked. First, the commands at which the environment draws a slot's key and nonce (the first-touch classes of the allowance of unit 11a-3) include the accept. Second, the seed deferral with its pointed seed completion at the accept: `defer` and `erase_game` admit a completion at any registration command, and the obligation for the accept is that the erased accept commutes with completions of the seed table (`seedLens`); this holds only if the split form keeps the environment's material and the slot in the marks rather than the joined 108 bytes, so that the build reads the seed only through the lazy lookup at its kernel install. Third, the single-install lemma `hidden_server_once` and the cost `buildSplit_env_cost`, which count installs at the build, are unchanged. No committed statement changes: the seed draw is still deferred to the first installation of a hidden kernel.
- The later-game split, so that the composition of C6 with `erase_later` (C7) and the split, by which the shape committed to Codex for R6 reaches the later game of `CE.idealGame g`, is tracked: `represent_later`, the later-game counterpart of `represent`, needs besides `CoreNatural` a naturality premise for the post-phase handler `postWith` along `mapBackend`, because in the ideal post phase HMAC reads the backend's R1 tables, which share the backend `S × Γ` with the slot service state. The later-game seed deferral `defer_later` is built (review fix pass). Planned for the remainder of 11a-2b or for 11b-1.
- Unit 11a-3 takes over `buildSplit_env_cost`, `finishSplit_env_cost` (at most one `.hash`, no coins, at most one administrative capability per hidden build or finish), `hidden_server_once` and `hidden_beacon_once`, the capability-form BLAKE2b factoring of the build and the finish in the per-command environment steps, and the invariant `env_stripped`, which makes `placeholderKernel` a pure marker: every kernel the environment holds is the placeholder, no record operation runs on it, and the World's kernels fill it at the merge. Until then the placeholder is a concrete, predictable kernel (the World's Beacon kernel of the all-zero seed, on both sides), as its docstring says.
- C7 for the ideal deployment game closes in CE-11b (11b-1), from `erase_game_ctx` with the ideal registration's pointed hidden finish built by `factor_focus`, `oblivious_focus` and the prefix and suffix lemmas `factor_prefix`, `oblivious_prefix`, `factor_suffix`, `oblivious_suffix`; there the steps before the hidden tail (owner lookup, preparation coins, slot-service touch and root derivation on the backend) and the commit after it must be shown to commute with completions of the CTX cache.
- Item-7 production-boundary note, unchanged: C7's premise is the sequence-one gate of the Rust reference implementation of this repository revision (`beaconcrypt/src/beacon.rs:468-470`) and of `PqxdhBeaconPhase2Publication.afterOpening`.
- When the five modules of this increment are next touched (at the latest with the remainder of 11a-2b), their short critique labels become "CE-11 design critique Mx" or are dropped ("CE-11a increment 2: confirming review" explains how to read them until then).
- The review fix pass was checked by a confirming review of `379bcb9c`, whose four nits one documentation commit addresses; not integrated.

## CE-11a increment 2: re-estimate

After increment 1, 11a-2 was estimated at 9-18 agent-days. This increment took one working session, under one agent-day, and covers the four modules of 11a-2 and the split forms with their kernel identities, except the parts listed under the open points. Remaining for CE-11a: 11a-2b 1-3, 11a-3 4-7 (one day added for the costs and single-install lemmas), 11a-4 5-9, documentation and review 3-5, so about 13-24 agent-days, against 21-39 after increment 1 with one day spent then. CE-11b is unchanged at 17-32. The review fix pass does not change these figures: the later-game `represent_later` it adds to the open points fits in the 11a-2b remainder or in 11b-1. The confirming-review nit fix raises the 11a-2b remainder to 2-4 agent-days, because item 2's skeleton first touches a Server slot at the selected accept (open points), which adds the split form of the accept and its seed-lens obliviousness; remaining for CE-11a is then about 14-25 agent-days. CE-11b stays at 17-32: the coordinate-to-slot allocator's map is the map from hidden coordinate to slot that C5 already needs in 11b-1.

## CE-11a increment 2: checks

Every module (the four of 11a-2 and `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenSplit`) was checked on its own, in import order, with Lean 4.34 (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) through `coord.sh lean claude` into the fresh scratch prefix `/tmp/bc-arc-scratch/common-experiment/ce11/inc2/olean`, a resolved symlink mirror (1616 links) of the first prefix of the published `LEAN_PATH` of central `1749e215`, in front of that published `LEAN_PATH`; each check exited 0 with an empty log, so every exact guard holds. The root import `BeaconcryptCore.lean` was checked at each Lean commit into a fresh path (exit 0, empty log), `coord.sh inv recount` and `coord.sh inv rehash` updated the inventory rows at each Lean commit, and the focused `make -C beaconcrypt-core check-inventory` ran under `coord.sh lease mem claude @nix` in the minimal proof flake at each Lean commit and with the documentation (exit 0). `git diff --check` is clean. Not run: `make verify`, a full `lake build`, mutation testing and `nix-collect-garbage`. The receipt is [proof-receipts/common-deployment-experiment-ce11a-inc2.json](proof-receipts/common-deployment-experiment-ce11a-inc2.json).

## CE-11a increment 2: review fix pass

An independent review of `413f454b` with a semantic and a hygiene lens raised three minor findings and six nits. The fix pass is one Lean commit and one documentation commit. It changes no existing statement and removes no theorem; it adds six theorems, each with an exact axiom guard, and no assumption, so the counts above (137 theorems; `PqxdhDeploymentFinishErasure` 20, `PqxdhDeploymentSeedSplit` 15) include them. Per finding:

- Minor: the analysis said that the build and the finish of a hidden entry read the world seed only through their kernel, which Lean states only for the erased finish (`finish_factor_split` and `erased_split` are about the erased finish, which runs the finish body only on a sequence-one frame, and `body_split` assumes such a frame). The analysis now says "the build and the erased finish (after the C7 erasure)", and adds that the unerased finish reads the seed on frames of sequence 2 to 51, whose receive derives chain steps from next32 and opens before the sequence-one check rejects the frame, which is why the seed deferral comes after the C7 erasure. The split-forms paragraph above says the same.
- Minor: the split for the later game was neither built nor listed. The fix pass adds `PqxdhDeploymentSeedSplit.defer_later`, the seed deferral for `laterWith` (an instance of `erase_later` at the hidden-seed lens, for a post phase that commutes with seed completions; the LQ-F reveal does not read the seed table), and `realPost_commutes_seed`, which shows that the real post phase meets that premise. The later-game `represent_later`, which needs a naturality premise for `postWith` along `mapBackend` because the ideal post phase reads the backend's R1 tables, is a new open point for the remainder of 11a-2b or 11b-1, and the module docstring of `PqxdhDeploymentSeedSplit` records it.
- Nit: `factor_focus` and `oblivious_focus` covered only a focused inner step with deterministic post-processing. The fix pass proves the two missing composition steps next to them in `PqxdhDeploymentFinishErasure`: `factor_prefix` and `oblivious_prefix` (a prefix `pre` before a family of pointed steps: the pointed form of `pre >>= step` is `pre >>= pointed`, by `LawEq.bind`, and it commutes with completions when the prefix and every pointed step do, by `commutes_bind`), and `factor_suffix` and `oblivious_suffix` (a pointed step followed by a suffix that commutes with completions: the completion moves past the suffix by its commutation, and the suffix passes the point on). The open point on C7 for the ideal game names them.
- Nit: the capability-form confirmation promised for unit 11a-2 moved to 11a-3 without a note for consumers. "Names for consumers" now says, for Codex, that it arrives with 11a-3 as `buildSplit_env_cost` and `finishSplit_env_cost`, and which parts this increment already gives.
- Nit: `placeholderKernel` is a concrete Beacon kernel of the all-zero seed, also used for the Server peer. Its docstring now says so, says that it is meant only as a marker that no record operation may use and that the install always replaces, and that nothing in the module enforces this before the invariant `env_stripped` of unit 11a-3, which the open points now list.
- Nit: the implementer's report truncated the reason for the new module `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenSplit`. Nothing to change on the branch; the reason (both target modules were already committed, and a new module keeps them unchanged) is in the decisions above.
- Minor: the sections of this increment cited labels of the CE-11 design critique (M2, M3, M5, m4, m7) and the CE-11 design, which exist only as scratch files, and the committed plan snapshot has its own, unrelated critique labels. Each label is replaced by what it asked for: the registration handler chosen after the Server is constructed, named obliviousness lemmas, kernel identities stated as equations with a placeholder kernel, and a pluggable post phase. Each deviation now says what the CE-11 design proposed, with plan section 32 for the CE-11 split. The module docstrings keep their short critique labels, as those of increment 1 do; the guide no longer relies on them.
- Nit: the receipt's wording was stale after the fifth module and the second documentation commit. Its fix-pass version says five modules, names `root-docs2` and `inventory-docs2` as the checks of the second documentation commit, records the plan hash at the fix pass and keeps the earlier value as the hash of the plan before its section 36 (the plan cut before section 36 has that hash, so section 35 was already in it).
- Nit: central has moved past what the guide recorded. The basis bullet now records central `f20cb4b0` at the fix pass and the four shared files that the merge must resolve; the merge itself belongs to the integration step.

Checks of the fix pass.
