## CE-11a increment 3: what is proved

Increment 3 of CE-11a (units 11a-3 and 11a-4 of the CE-11 split of plan section 32, without the parts listed under "open points" below) adds two modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 148 guarded theorems, 14 of them added by the review fix pass, which also restated eleven join theorems relative to an admissibility predicate (see "CE-11a increment 3: review fix pass"); no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains two imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment` | 82 | The registration environment of the record lift (unit 11a-3). The environment state `EnvState` holds the registration state with every record coordinate stripped (`strip`: every Server peer kernel and every established owner kernel is `placeholderKernel`, the CTX cache is empty; commitment reply-final 4.4, critique M6), the registration handler's backend, the LQ-F ghosts (finish snapshot and Server ephemeral log) and the environment's own copy of the constructed Server; `realStep_stripped` shows that every real step keeps it stripped. `start` maps the actual Server constructor at a fixed runtime to `Option (Created Unit × EnvState)` (`start_created`, `start_initial`, `start_bound` with `qInit + 32` coins). The capability form: `coins`, `request`, `issue`, `onEnv`; the BLAKE2b request forwarded as the world's `.hash` (`primFwd`, `sharedFwd`, `nativeTailFwd`, `tailFwd`, `beaconFwd`, `callbacksFwd`); the record-relevant changes as capabilities (`ownerEffect`, `ownerChange`, `serverEffect`); the six real steps `createStep`, `bundleStep`, `acceptStep`, `buildStep`, `finishStep`, `hmacStep`, the snapshot `snapped`, `realStep` (the intended capability form of the LQ-F registration handler `PqxdhDeploymentBackend.realReg`; that it reproduces `realReg` is an open point) and `envReal`. The capability-form factoring of the BLAKE2b cache: `ctxEffects` (coins as coins, `.hash` as the lazy CTX oracle on a cache), the generic `factor_program` and `factor_stateless`, `primFwd_factor`, `registration_factor`, `nativeTail_factor`, `tail_factor` (the native logged build tail is the forwarded tail read through `ctxEffects`), `callbacksFwd_frame`, `callbacks_step` and `owner_factor` (the same for the native Beacon finish). C2a: `allowanceG` and `ceFirstTouch` (the accept and the finish, plan section 35), `baseAllowance`, the per-step bounds (`createStep_bound` to `hmacStep_bound`, `realStep_bound`), `frame_real`, and `frame_wrapped` for every environment that draws first-touch material through `touch` before a core step (`wrapStep`, `materialQuery_bound`, `touch_bound`); the effect bounds of the forwarded programs (`coins_bound`, `issue_bound`, `build_fwd_bound`, `owner_hash_bound`, `finish_fwd_bound` and their helpers). The split hidden wrappers in capability form `buildSplitEnv` and `finishSplitEnv` (intended forms of `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenSplit.buildSplit` and `finishSplit`; their factoring against those forms is an open point) with `buildSplit_env_cost` and `finishSplit_env_cost`. The single-install ingredients `buildStep_consumes`, `buildStep_idle`, `finishStep_terminal`, `finishStep_retry`. C4: `capsG`, `envTotal_hash`, `capsG_sharedHash`, `capsG_derivations`, `c4_instance`, `c4_closed`, `c4_later`, `c4_later_closed`, `c4_real`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentJoin` | 66 | The generic join with the record world (unit 11a-4). Reassembly `merge` (the environment's registration state with the World's Server record structure, established shells and kernels and shared CTX cache filled in by `fill`, plus the World's raw-attempt counters, produced frames and seed table), `forget` with `merge_forget`, the admissibility invariant `Good` and the obligation `Splits` relative to an environment-specific admissibility predicate `inv` conjoined with `Good`, which must be `Stable` (it reads the World only through `fixedView`); `stable_true` and `splits_true` give the obligation on `Good` alone for `inv := fun _ _ => True`, and the fixed steps keep `fixedView` (`effects_view`, `serverStep_view`, `beaconStep_view`, and `initialized_view` for the startup). The exact joins of the fixed steps `coin_join`, `hash_join`, `serverStep_merge` and `beaconStep_merge` (the World-to-native record-step projections of design section 8), their invariance lemmas (`serverStep_good`, `beaconStep_good`, `effects_frame`, `good_frame`), the per-step obligation `worldStep_step`, the invariant `worldStep_keeps`, and the main simulation `run_join` with its continuation form `run_join_bind`. The startup (`coins_world`, `merge_initial`, `good_initial`). C1 as `join` with the projections `join_observed`, `join_analysis` and `join_reveal` (the run-phase reveal); C3 as `c3_shared_cache`; C6 as `join_later` and `join_later_public`, with `run_split`, `postNative`, `envReveal`, `reveal_merge`, `revealOf_strip`, `revealOf_fill` and `view_merge`. The satisfiability check: a toy environment with one hidden Server install that the World admits, its handler and its admissibility predicate (`addPeer`, `strip_addPeer`, `toyFresh`, `toyAfter`, `toyStep`, `lazySeed`, `toyInstalled`, `toyReg`, `toyInv`), with `toy_stable`, `toy_start`, `toy_install_run`, `toy_admitted`, `toy_splits` and `toy_join`. |

## CE-11a increment 3: decisions and deviations

- Stripping is structural: every environment step commits `strip` of its new registration state, so the CE-11 design's invariant `env_stripped` is `realStep_stripped` for the real environment and holds the same way for any environment whose steps commit through `onEnv` or the same pattern. Every kernel the environment holds is therefore `placeholderKernel`, no record operation runs on it, and `merge` fills it from the World.
- `EnvStep`, `realStep` and `envReal` are generic in the slot count `n`; the CE-11 design used `n = 0` for the real environment, which is an instance.
- The real environment installs with known kernels: a published peer by `installServer keyId identity (.known kernel)`, an established owner by `installBeacon owner shell (.known kernel)`, a bundle and an abort by `advanceOwner`. `create` appends the owner in the World by `appendOwner keyId pin .fresh` and returns the environment's own index (equal to the World's handle on admissible states).
- The BLAKE2b factoring of build and finish that the CE-11 design planned as `build_hash_factor` and `finish_hash_factor` is `tail_factor` and `owner_factor`, stated against `ctxEffects admin`, the reading of the environment's effects on a CTX cache with any interpretation `admin` of the administrative capabilities (the forwarded programs issue none). The generic `factor_program` takes a cache lens and a forwarded handler that never touches the lens.
- The first-touch classes are the accept (class 3) and the finish (class 5), `ceFirstTouch`, following plan section 35 (the Server slot is first read at the selected accept, a Beacon slot at its finish). The build is not a first-touch class, because item 2's skeleton keeps the material in its marks from the accept to the build. `allowanceG` and `frame_wrapped` take the first-touch classes as a parameter.
- `frame_wrapped` is stated for an environment built by `wrapStep table slotOf core`: at a command that touches a slot the slot's material is obtained by `touch` (a lazy lookup of the backend's material table, 44 coins on a miss) and passed to the core step. The CE-11 design stated its premises informally ("the core's per-class bounds", "material is drawn only at first-touch classes"); they are the hypotheses `coreBound` and `touches`. In this shape the slot is a function `slotOf` of the command and the pre-state. Under item 2 plan section 20 a Beacon slot comes from the first-touch coordinate-to-slot allocator, keyed by the 128-byte root-input coordinate that the finish computes only after preparation, decapsulation and DH, so the ideal environment's finish may not have this shape; CE-11b may then prove `PqxdhRecordLiftInterface.Frame envG (qInit + 32) (allowanceG core ceFirstTouch)` directly, since `allowanceG` is the core allowance plus 44 coins at the first-touch classes either way.
- `baseAllowance` takes the existing per-command uniform bills: create `64 + keygen` (CE's create does not re-run runtime initialization, deviation D1), bundle 32, accept `96 + encaps`, build 0 (its BLAKE2b miss moves to `.hash`), finish `69 * max 64 decaps` (this bound already covers the finish's BLAKE2b draw, which the World also charges through `.hash`: an overlap in the bound, not a second draw), HMAC 64; one `.hash` at build and finish; one administrative capability at create, bundle, build and finish.
- `c4_instance` is generic in the environment, the startup coins, the allowance and the server key ID; the form agreed with the record-lift line for the ideal environment is its instance at `setup.serverKeyId` and `qInit + 32`. Later-mode C4 adds the post-phase BLAKE2b budget as a separate summand and leaves `Caps` unchanged, as agreed with the record-lift line for R6.
- The split hidden wrappers in capability form: `buildSplitEnv` is `PqxdhServerAuthLoggedSendFactor.factored` at the environment material and the dummy seed on the forwarded logged tail, settled as the native build settles, followed by the hidden install of the published peer; `finishSplitFwd` is the erased hidden finish with the ROM-level primitives (`PqxdhDeploymentHiddenEntry.romPrim`) whose BLAKE2b query is forwarded as `.hash`; `finishSplitEnv` adds the source commit with its two pin checks (`PqxdhBeaconPhase2Publication.finish`) and the hidden install or the abort. Their costs are proved; their factoring against the CE-side split forms is an open point.
- The single-install lemmas `hidden_server_once` and `hidden_beacon_once` are not stated as run-level theorems: they need the routing law of item 2's registration core (which session builds and which owner finishes a slot), which does not exist yet. The per-step ingredients are proved for the real build and finish: a build consumes its session's token on every outcome and a build without a token issues nothing (`buildStep_consumes`, `buildStep_idle`); a finish from an initSent owner leaves it terminal, and a finish at a terminal owner issues nothing (`finishStep_terminal`, `finishStep_retry`).
- `merge` takes the World's whole Server record structure (identity and peer map) when the World's Server is initialized, and `fillOwner` changes only an established owner, so the Beacon-side reveal of the reassembled state is always the environment's (`revealOf_fill`). `Good` compares the Server's peers with the environment's up to their kernels (`stripPeer`) and the owners phase by phase up to their kernels (`stripPhase`).
- `Good` constrains neither the World's used markers nor its seed table, and `merge` reads neither. The World admits a hidden install only while its slot marker is unused, so the CE-11 design's clause "used markers = hidden installs performed" depends on how an environment records its installs, and enters as a parameter (review fix pass): `Splits created reg step inv` and the join theorems take an admissibility predicate `inv` conjoined with `Good`. `inv` must be `Stable` (it reads the World only through `fixedView`: the used markers, the seed table with its counts, the Server's sender, identity and peers up to their kernels, and the owners up to their kernels, which every fixed step keeps) and must hold at the constructed Server's initial environment state and the initialized fresh World. With `inv := fun _ _ => True` the obligation is the one on `Good` alone (`splits_true`); that is how the real environment, which installs only known kernels, uses the join. The ideal environment of CE-11b supplies its own `inv`, together with its C2b admission lemma (it never issues a second hidden install for the same slot and role).
- The World-to-native record-step projections that design section 8 asked of Codex (or else of CE-11) are proved here as `serverStep_merge` and `beaconStep_merge`; the `chargeEffect` forget lemma is `merge_charge` (by definition) with `merge_sampled`; `run_split`, optional in R6, is proved here.
- The post phase of the native later game is `postNative`, the run-phase handler after `PqxdhDeploymentLater.postEmbed`; for the real handler this is LQ-F's `postImpl` (`PqxdhDeploymentLater.post_embed`). With it, `join_later` needs no separate hypothesis for the post phase. The public form `join_later_public` and the run-phase reveal projection `join_reveal` expose the record view (in the later form) and the Beacon-side reveal with the ephemeral log, that is the first and last components of `envReveal` (the agreed `laterView EnvState.reveal`), but not its Server seed component, which is not part of the native games' outcomes. That component is covered only inside `join_later`: there the second adversary receives `envReveal` on the World side and the native reveal `xreveal` on the native side, which are equal by `reveal_merge` and `Good.createdEq`. `reveal_merge` needs `Good` (for `createdEq` and the owners' phases), unlike the design's form for all states. The design's run-phase `join_reveal` is the theorem of that name, added by the fix pass.
- C3 is stated on the Server's public data, the output, the ledger and the final CTX cache (`c3_shared_cache`).
- Docstring labels in the two modules: "design-v2 section 5.x" is the CE-11 design summarized in plan section 32, "critique Mx" its critique, and "reply-final 4.4", "r6-shape reply", "r6-later reply" and "world-budget reply" are the agreements with the record-lift line on the environment state, the R6 later phase and C4, whose content is stated in this guide (the stripped environment state, the generic reveal, the post-phase summand); every Lean statement is self-contained. "Plan section 33" is the world-side C4 and R6 shape: the record-lift line's `PqxdhRecordLiftWorldBudget` gives `capsG`'s shared-hash cap and derivation count on the World's run with the premises C2a and `B.Respects` only, R6 takes a generic reveal that CE-11 instantiates with LQ-F's reveal from the environment's final state, and later-mode C4 adds the post-phase BLAKE2b budget, with the post-phase HMAC class 6 allowed 64 coins, no BLAKE2b query and no administrative request. "Plan section 35" is the first touch at the accept: the Server slot's 108 bytes are first read at the selected raw accept (as the Rust Server derives the root before it builds the response) and a Beacon slot's at the hidden finish, so the accept and the finish are the first-touch classes, while the seed part stays deferred to the build-time hidden install. Both sections are in the committed plan snapshot `common-deployment-experiment-plan.md` since the fix pass; the working plan had two sections numbered 35, and the earlier one (increment 2 built) is now 34a.
- The real instances `c1_real` and `c6_real` (cuttable, critique m12) and the per-command splits of the real registration handler are not built; see "open points".
- Basis: `claude/ce11a` at `8ef1af6c`, which equals central `8ef1af6c`. Central moved to `94a77e95` during this increment (Codex's integrations of `PqxdhRecordLiftMixedRecords`, `PqxdhRecordLiftFirstLookup`, `PqxdhRecordLiftGlobalCut`, `PqxdhRecordLiftProtocolHistory`, `PqxdhRecordLiftMixedWorld`, `PqxdhRecordLiftCtxProtocol`, `PqxdhRecordLiftCtxProtocolBounds` and `PqxdhRecordLiftGlobalSeedCut`, all new modules of the record-lift line), then to `0a177a98` (`PqxdhRecordLiftAvailability`, `PqxdhRecordLiftChildRelay`, `PqxdhRecordLiftFirstLookupPrefix`, `PqxdhRecordLiftOpaqueBounds` and `PqxdhRecordLiftSourcePrefix`), and during the review fix pass to `fa8d9a54`, which carries item 2's increment 3 (S3b, S4b with T7, and S7: eight new `PqxdhRegistrationJointKem*` modules) built on `claude/extract-composition`, which this branch did not touch. Since `8ef1af6c` central adds only new modules and changes, among existing files, only the shared files of the integration hot spots (the root import, `check-inventory.sh`, `reviewed-inventory.txt`, `trusted-boundary.md`, `composition-assumptions.md` and the analysis) and item 2's guide and plan, so no module that this increment imports changed. The branch was not merged: the integration step merges central `fa8d9a54` or later, updates this note, and reruns the focused checks, the inventory recount and rehash and the focused `check-inventory`.

## CE-11a increment 3: names for consumers

- For Codex: the C2a premise `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment.frame_wrapped` with `allowanceG` and `ceFirstTouch`; C4 `capsG`, `capsG_sharedHash`, `capsG_derivations`, `c4_instance`, `c4_closed`, `c4_later`, `c4_later_closed`; the confirmation in capability form that a hidden wrapper issues no coin, at most one `.hash` and at most one install or abort capability (`buildSplit_env_cost`, `finishSplit_env_cost`; the capability interface has no seed or kernel read); C1, C3 and C6: `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentJoin.join`, `join_observed`, `join_analysis`, `join_reveal`, `c3_shared_cache`, `join_later`, `join_later_public`, `envReveal`, `reveal_merge`, `view_merge`; since the fix pass they take an admissibility predicate `inv` with `Stable inv` and its start condition, and `fun _ _ => True` (`stable_true`, `splits_true`) recovers the earlier statements.
- For CE-11b: the environment pieces `EnvState`, `EnvStep`, `start`, `envInitial`, `onEnv`, `coins`, `request`, `issue`, `ownerEffect`, `ownerChange`, `serverEffect`, `hiddenServerEffect`, `hiddenOwnerEffect`, `createStep`, `bundleStep`, `acceptStep`, `buildStep`, `finishStep`, `hmacStep`, `snapped`, `touch`, `wrapStep`, `buildSplitEnv`, `finishSplitEnv`; the factoring `ctxEffects`, `factor_program`, `tail_factor`, `owner_factor`; the join tools `Good`, `Splits`, `fixedView`, `Stable`, `stable_true`, `splits_true`, `initialized_view`, `merge`, `fill`, `run_join`, `run_join_bind`, `worldStep_step`, `worldStep_keeps`; the toy environment (`toyStep`, `toyReg`, `toyInv`, `toy_splits`) as a pattern for the ideal environment's admissibility predicate.
- Use these short names qualified: `start`, `strip`, `coins`, `request`, `issue`, `touch`, `merge`, `fill`, `join`, `initialized`, `uncount`, `joined`, `postNative`, `Stable`, `addPeer`, `lazySeed`.

## CE-11a increment 3: contracts used

None. The modules use the existing extracted programs and native interpreters, the frozen record-lift interface, the published World, Budget, WorldBudget and Later modules, and VCVio's lazy random oracle; no codec law. The premises of the generic theorems (`Splits`, the admissibility predicate `inv` with `Stable inv` and its start condition, the start equation `born`, `Frame`, `B.Respects`, the PostBudget respect, the material wrapper's `coreBound` and `touches`) are obligations on the given environment, handler and adversaries; none is an assumption of a published theorem, and no new assumption or contract is added.

## CE-11a increment 3: open points

- The real instances (cuttable, critique m12): `c1_real` and `c6_real`, and the per-command splits of the real registration handler (`Splits created (realReg … created) (realStep …)`), which the CE-11 design keeps because CE-11b reuses them. They need, for create, bundle, accept and HMAC, that the native step commutes with filling the World's record coordinates; for build and finish, `tail_factor` and `owner_factor` with the World's reading of `.hash` and the known-kernel installations; and the snapshot correspondence. Estimate 3-5 agent-days. Proved in increment 4 (see "CE-11a increment 4: what is proved"), where the names-for-consumers section says what CE-11b can reuse of them.
- `hidden_server_once` and `hidden_beacon_once` as run-level statements, from the ingredients above and the routing law of item 2's registration core; planned for CE-11b (11b-1).
- The factoring of `buildSplitEnv` and `finishSplitFwd` against the CE-side split forms `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenSplit.buildSplit` and `finishSplit` (they run the same generic programs in another monad; the step is the naturality of `PqxdhServerAuthLoggedSendFactor.factored` and `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenKernels.finishErased` under simulation), needed by CE-11b's splits for hidden entries. Estimate 1-2 agent-days. Proved in increment 4 (`PqxdhDeploymentSplitFactor`).
- Unit 11a-2b, remainder, unchanged: it waits for item 2's registration core and the first-touch re-check of item 2 plan section 20.
- The ideal environment's admissibility predicate (CE-11b): its used markers are exactly the hidden installs it has performed, with the C2b admission lemma that it never issues a second hidden install for the same slot and role; the toy environment shows the pattern.
- The review fix pass was checked by a confirming review of `aeaad638`, whose two documentation nits one documentation commit addresses; not integrated into central at this commit.

## CE-11a increment 3: review fix pass

An independent review of `4f502f99` with a semantic and a hygiene lens raised one blocking finding, two minor findings and six nits. The fix pass is one Lean commit (`d83bb01b`) and one documentation commit. It changes the statements of `Splits` and of eleven join theorems, each to a form at least as strong as before (below), removes no theorem and adds 14, each with an exact axiom guard, and no assumption, so the counts above (148 theorems; `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentJoin` 66) include them. Per finding:

- Blocking: `Good` did not constrain the World's used markers, so `Splits` could not hold for an environment whose hidden install the World admits. The review's probe showed that `Good` holds for every choice of the used markers once it holds for one, that `merge` ignores them, and that the World refuses a hidden install whose marker is set; so the right side of `Splits` was the same at a World state and at that state with every marker set, while the left side differed for any environment that issues an admitted hidden install with positive probability. The CE-11 design had listed "used markers = hidden installs performed" in `Good`; the clause was dropped without a record. Fix: `Splits created reg step inv` takes an environment-specific admissibility predicate `inv : EnvState KemSK Bk → PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.State n → Prop`, conjoined with the unchanged `Good`. `inv` must be `Stable` (`fixedView w' = fixedView w → inv e w → inv e w'`, where `fixedView` is the used markers, the seed table with its counts, the Server's sender, identity and peers up to their kernels, and the owners up to their kernels, which the fixed steps keep: `effects_view`, `serverStep_view`, `beaconStep_view`); since agreement on `fixedView` fixes more than the agreement the review proposed (used markers, seed table, peer presence and identities, owner phases up to kernels), this requirement is weaker. `inv` must also hold at the constructed Server's initial environment state and the initialized fresh World; `initialized_view` carries it over the startup coins. Statements before and after:
  - `Splits created reg step` (for every command and every `Good` state: the law of the command is that of the handler, and every outcome is `Good`) became `Splits created reg step inv` (for every command and every state satisfying `Good` and `inv`: the same law, and every outcome satisfies `Good` and `inv`); `splits_true` proves `Splits created reg step (fun _ _ => True)` equivalent to the old definition.
  - `worldStep_step` and `run_join` take `inv` (and `run_join` also `stable : Stable inv`), `splits` relative to `inv` and `holds : inv e w`; their conclusions are unchanged.
  - `worldStep_keeps` and `run_keeps` take `inv`, `stable`, `splits` relative to `inv` and `holds`; their conclusions are `Good … ∧ inv …` instead of `Good …`.
  - `run_join_bind` takes `inv`, `stable`, `splits` relative to `inv` and `holds`, and its continuation premise `next` may also use `inv` of the outcome.
  - `join`, `c3_shared_cache`, `join_observed`, `join_analysis`, `join_later` and `join_later_public` take `inv`, `stable : Stable inv` and `startInv : ∀ created, inv (envInitial created (init created)) (initialized created PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.initial)`, and `splits` relative to `inv`; their conclusions are unchanged.
  - Each new statement is at least as strong as the old one: at `inv := fun _ _ => True` its extra premises hold trivially (`stable_true`, `fun _ => trivial`, `trivial`), `splits_true` turns the old `Splits` premise into the new one, and a conclusion with an added `∧ True` implies the old conclusion. A scratch probe states the eleven old statements with the old `Splits` and proves each from the new one in one line (all on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`; the receipt records it). The satisfiability check is the toy environment `toyStep` at a slot, a key ID and an identity: while its flag is unset and its own peer at the key ID is vacant, every command issues `installServer keyId identity (.hidden slot)`, sets the flag and records the peer with the placeholder kernel. `toy_admitted` shows that from every admissible state with that guard the World admits the install (in every outcome the slot's marker is set and the peer is present); `toy_splits` shows `Splits` for the handler `toyReg` (the seed of the slot from the extended state's lazy seed table, and the peer installed with the World's Server kernel of that seed) relative to `toyInv` (the World's marker of the slot is set exactly when the flag is); `toy_join` applies C1 to it. The decisions above, the receipt and the working notes record the change, and the analysis no longer says that the join as first stated serves the ideal game.
- Minor: the analysis said that the environment "holds no record secret". It holds no record coordinate (no Server peer or Beacon kernel and no commitment cache), but it keeps registration secrets: a pending root until the build consumes it, the Extract-family table, and for a hidden slot the sequence-one key and nonce. The analysis now says so.
- Nit: the module docstring and the docstring of `realStep`, this guide and the analysis presented `realStep` as the LQ-F handler `realReg` in capability form, and the docstrings of `buildSplitFwd` and `finishSplitFwd` presented them as `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenSplit.buildSplit` and `finishSplit`. They are now called intended capability forms, with the statement that the correspondence (the per-command splits behind `c1_real`, and the factoring of the split wrappers) is open and that only the costs are proved.
- Nit: `frame_wrapped` computes the slot from the command and the pre-state, which may not fit the ideal finish. The `frame_wrapped` docstring and the decisions above now say that CE-11b may prove `Frame envG (qInit + 32) (allowanceG core ceFirstTouch)` directly.
- Nit: the C6 public form omits the Server seed component of the agreed reveal, and the run-phase reveal projection existed only as a mapping in the receipt. The fix pass adds `join_reveal`, the run-phase reveal projection of `join` (by `revealOf_fill`); the decisions above and the docstrings of `join_later_public` and of the module say that the seed component is covered only inside `join_later`, through `reveal_merge` and `Good.createdEq`.
- Minor: the docstrings and this guide cited plan sections 33 and 35, which the committed plan snapshot did not contain, and the working plan had two sections numbered 35. The earlier one (increment 2 built) is now 34a, so section 35 is the first-touch section that the modules cite; the committed snapshot `common-deployment-experiment-plan.md` is refreshed to the working plan with sections 33 to 39; and the docstring-labels bullet above states the content of sections 33 and 35.
- Nit: the receipt's `commits_note` was stale after `4f502f99`. It now names the documentation commits that carry the receipt and the central moves, and says which checks ran on which tree.
- Nit: the status paragraph counted one documentation commit; it now counts the two before the fix pass and the fix pass itself.
- Nit (informational): central moved to `0a177a98` after `4f502f99` and to `fa8d9a54` during the fix pass. The basis bullet records both; the merge belongs to the integration step.

Checks of the fix pass. The two modules were checked in import order, and the root import `BeaconcryptCore.lean` after them, with Lean 4.34 (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) through `coord.sh lean claude` into a fresh scratch prefix `/tmp/bc-arc-scratch/common-experiment/ce11/fix4/final/olean`, a resolved symlink mirror (6074 links) of the increment's prefix without the two modules, in front of the published `LEAN_PATH` of central `8ef1af6c`; each check exited 0 with an empty log, so every exact guard holds. An earlier round of the same checks into `fix4/olean` preceded two docstring corrections in `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment`. `coord.sh inv recount` left the count at 1617, the module block of `check-inventory.sh` was regenerated for `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentJoin`, `coord.sh inv rehash` updated the rows of the two modules and of `check-inventory.sh` (a second rehash unchanged), the focused `make -C beaconcrypt-core check-inventory` under `coord.sh lease mem claude @nix` exited 0 before the Lean commit and with the documentation, and `git diff --check` is clean. Not run: `make verify`, a full `lake build`, mutation testing (no production Rust changed) and `nix-collect-garbage`.

## CE-11a increment 3: confirming review

A confirming review of the fix pass at `aeaad638` approved it. It confirmed that all nine findings of the first review are resolved, that no statement is weaker than before, that every theorem has its exact axiom guard and that the receipt matches the reviewed commit. It raised two optional documentation nits and one housekeeping note. One documentation commit addresses the nits in the analysis and the receipt and records the review in this guide. It changes no file under `beaconcrypt-core/`, so no statement, proof, module docstring or inventory row changed, and the Lean and inventory checks of the fix pass still apply. Per item:

- Confirmation (informational): all nine findings are resolved at `aeaad638`, no statement is weaker, the guards match the theorems and the receipt matches the commit. No action.
- Nit: the analysis glossed the part of the World that an admissibility predicate may read as "the Server's and Beacons' state up to kernels", which is broader than `fixedView`: that view reads the used markers, the seed table, the Server's sender, identity and peers and the Beacons' phases, each up to kernels, and not, for example, the Server's raw-attempt counter, which the fixed steps change. The parenthesis in the analysis now lists exactly these components. The decisions bullet above already described `fixedView` exactly.
- Nit: the receipt had no `git diff --check` row for the fix pass. Its `review_fix_pass` field now has one for the range `4f502f99..aeaad638` (exit 0, empty output).
- Housekeeping (not a defect of the commits): the reviewer left a temporary directory with copies of committed sources outside the registered scratch area. This agent may not delete paths that are not registered to it, so the directory is left to the owner; the receipt names it.

The committed plan snapshot `common-deployment-experiment-plan.md` was compared with the working plan at this commit: the working plan still has the SHA-256 recorded in the snapshot's header and the snapshot's body equals it, so the snapshot needed no refresh. The status paragraph above and the open points record the confirming review. The analysis no longer says that the fix pass is not reviewed and that the work is not integrated; its first paragraph now records the confirming review.

## CE-11a increment 4: what is proved

Increment 4 of CE-11a adds two modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 162 guarded theorems (157 before the review fix pass, which adds five real instances of the join's projections); no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains two imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentSplitFactor` | 22 | The factoring of the environment's split hidden wrappers (increment 3) against the CE-side split forms (increment 2). Read through `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment.ctxEffects` (coins as coins, the forwarded BLAKE2b request as the lazy CTX oracle on a cache, the administrative capabilities by any interpretation `admin`), the forwarded split build `buildSplitFwd` on a seal log is `PqxdhDeploymentHiddenSplit.buildSplit` on the log and the cache (`buildSplit_factor`), and the forwarded split finish `finishSplitFwd` is `finishSplit` on the cache (`finishSplit_factor`); the environment wrappers add only the settlement of the build, the source commit of the finish and the hidden install or abort request (`buildSplitEnv_factor`, `finishSplitEnv_factor`). The build goes through the naturality of the generic Server build under simulation of its callbacks (`continueRust_simulateQ`, `afterInitialization_simulateQ`, `candidate_simulateQ`, `genericBuild_simulateQ`, `sendLabels_simulateQ`, `factored_simulateQ`): both forms are their response tails run on one program `freeBuild` whose callbacks are left as queries (`factored_free`, `buildFactor_free`, `buildSplitFwd_free`), and `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment.tail_factor` relates the two tails. The finish goes through the ROM-level primitives: the native primitive interpreter is the ROM-level one on the CTX coordinate (`nativePrim_lift`), so the native erased finish is the ROM-level erased finish with BLAKE2b answered by the lazy CTX oracle (`receiveLabels_rom`, `factoredBody_rom`, `finishErased_rom`, with the focused-run tools `lift_run`, `lift_pure`, `lift_map`, `lift_simulateQ`), and the forwarded `.hash` read through `ctxEffects` is that oracle (`hashFx_ctx`). |

The tools: the World read on its CTX cache (`withCache`, `effects_query_ctx`, `effects_ctx`, `ctxAdmin`, `coins_noAdmin`, `effects_coins`, `effects_then`, `effects_then_free`), count-free programs ## CE-11a increment 4: decisions and deviations

- New modules instead of extending published ones: `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment` and `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentJoin` are published on central and frozen, so the factoring and the real instances are two new modules under Claude's registered prefix `PqxdhDeployment*`.
- The admissibility predicate of the real splits is the trivial one, `fun _ _ => True`. The real environment issues only known-kernel installations, which the World admits without reading or setting any used marker (a known kernel is always unused and marking it changes nothing), so the condition that an environment with hidden installations needs (its used markers are exactly its hidden installations) is empty here; `stable_true` and `fun _ => trivial` discharge the join's two conditions on the predicate. The most direct reason is the next bullet: the real instances run with no hidden slot at all.
- The real instances run with zero hidden slots (`n = 0`), a deviation from design m12, which expected CE-11b to reuse the per-command `realReg_splits` lemmas. `PqxdhDeploymentBackend.realReg` is fixed at `RegImpl KemSK Unit 0`, so the per-command splits and everything in the `Commands` section they rest on (`createStep_effects` to `hmacStep_effects`, `create_law` to `hmac_law`, `create_keeps` to `hmac_keeps`, `realReg_splits_create` to `realReg_splits_hmac`) are stated with `EnvState KemSK Unit`, `PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.State 0` and `realStep (n := 0)`. CE-11b works at `n = 2` with its own backend state, so these lemmas do not apply to it as stated; the tools before the `Commands` section (sections `Bridge` to `FinishShape`) are generic in `n` and the backend state. With no hidden slot the World has no used marker to track, which is also the most direct reason the trivial admissibility predicate is enough.
- Exactness: each per-command law is an equality of programs (hence of distributions), not only an equality in law; `realReg_splits_*` take it through `LawEq.of_eq`. The World's counts (uniform draws, primitive calls, effect charges) are erased by `uncount`, and neither the reassembly nor admissibility reads them.
- The World read on its cache: the bridge `effects_ctx` needs that the program issues no administrative effect, stated as an `IsQueryBoundP` premise; for the build it is `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment.build_fwd_bound` at the administrative class, for the finish the new `finishFwd_noAdmin` (the existing finish bound needs a KEM cost bound only for its coin part). The administrative continuations are count-free (`CountFree`), so `effects_then_free` composes the two without a premise on the continuation.
- The build reuses CE-3's override lemmas (`PqxdhDeploymentCompileRegistration.build_override`, `settle_override`) with the World's peer kernels as CE-3's local peer copies (`worldCopies`): on an admissible state the reassembled Server record is CE-3's override of the environment's stripped record. That a publication is at a key ID the World does not hold, and that the World therefore admits the known installation, comes from `build_shape` (a build publishes at most one peer, at a key ID that was vacant) and the admissibility of the peers.
- The finish reuses `owner_factor` and `frame_program` (increment 3) for the BLAKE2b cache and `callbacks_nframe` for the Server record; `finish_shape` gives the three outcomes (unchanged, aborted from initSent, established from initSent), and the World admits the corresponding phase advance or known Beacon installation because its owner is at initSent by admissibility.
- The bundle's phase changes are legal World advances because a bundle only publishes from a fresh phase, with or without retained coins (`bundle_shape`); it never moves a fresh owner to the retained-coins phase.
- The split factoring is stated, as `tail_factor` and `owner_factor`, against `ctxEffects admin` for any interpretation of the administrative capabilities. Its World form for CE-11b's hidden splits needs one more step: `buildSplitEnv` and `finishSplitEnv` end with an administrative request (the installation `installServer` or `installBeacon` at a `.hidden` slot, or `advanceOwner` for an abort), while `effects_ctx` applies only to a program without administrative effects, and `effects_then_free` needs a `CountFree` continuation, which `countFree_issue` gives only for `KnownAdmin` requests, and those exclude hidden installations. So CE-11b combines `buildSplitEnv_factor` or `finishSplitEnv_factor` with `effects_then`, the forwarded part going through `effects_ctx` and the hidden installation through an explicit computation of the World's answer, or with `effects_then_free` after proving a count-freeness lemma for hidden installations.
- `c1_real` is stated at the full reassembled state; its projections onto the classical game, the LQ-F run phase and the World's observed game, and `c6_real` onto the LQ-F view, compose with the published identities `gameWith_classical`, `gameWith_real` and `laterWith_real`.
- Basis: `claude/ce11a` at `65e20738`, which equals central `65e20738`. Central moved to `80560f92` during this increment (CE-9b: two new modules, the shared integration files, this guide, the committed plan snapshot and the analysis); no module that this increment imports changed. The branch was not merged: the integration step merges central and reruns the focused checks.

## CE-11a increment 4: names for consumers

- For CE-11b: the split factoring `buildSplit_factor`, `finishSplit_factor`, `buildSplitEnv_factor`, `finishSplitEnv_factor`; the World bridge `effects_ctx`, `effects_then_free`, `CountFree` with `countFree_issue`, `countFree_ownerEffect`, `countFree_serverEffect`, and `effects_append`, `effects_advance`, `effects_installServer`, `effects_installBeacon`; the frames `NFrame`, `frameGame`, `serverGame` with `acceptLogged_nframe`, `nativeHmac_nframe`, `callbacks_nframe`, `shared_run`, `shared_fill`, `shared_good`; the reassembly algebra `fill_some`, `fill_strip`, `fill_set`, `matched_set`, `good_intro`, `good_server`; the shapes `bundle_shape`, `build_shape`, `finish_shape`. All of these are generic in `n` and the backend state. CE-11b also reuses the proof pattern of the create, bundle and HMAC laws (`create_law`, `bundle_law`, `hmac_law`: the native step runs on the reassembled state as on the stripped one, through the shared-step frames and the reassembly algebra), but not the laws themselves: they and `realReg_splits_create` to `realReg_splits_hmac` are stated for `realReg` at `n = 0` with the unit backend state, so CE-11b re-proves the per-command laws for its ideal handler (or first generalizes them over `n` and the backend state against a generic handler). Its handler also differs from the real one in the commands that touch the challenge (design-v2 section 6, recorded in plan section 42): the create at creation index k uses the pre-generated challenge KEM key, the accept at raw-accept ordinal i performs the challenge encapsulation with a uniform shared secret, and owner k's routed decapsulation of the exact challenge ciphertext returns that shared secret.
- For the record-lift line: `c1_real_observed`, `c1_real_analysis`, `c1_real_classical_observed`, `c1_real_classical_analysis`, `c1_real_reveal`, `c1_real`, `c6_real` and `c6_real_public`, the real game's instances of the hand-off obligations C1 and C6 and of their projections, with `real_splits` as the `Splits` premise.
- Use these short names qualified: `lift_run`, `withCache`, `Matched`, `Built`, `Bundled`, `Finished`, `NFrame`.
- Cite `PqxdhDeploymentCompileStep.onNative_run` and `PqxdhRegistrationSchedule.onShared_run` rather than this module's `onNative_run` and `onShared_run`, which restate them (their docstrings say so); later increments add no further local copies of lemmas in the import closure.

## CE-11a increment 4: contracts used

None. The modules use the existing extracted programs and native interpreters, the published environment, join, World, Later and CE-3 modules and VCVio's lazy random oracle; no codec law. The premises of the per-command lemmas are the join's admissibility invariant `Good` on the given states and, for the bridge, the absence of administrative effects in the given program; none is an assumption of a published theorem, and no new assumption or contract is added.

## CE-11a increment 4: open points

- `hidden_server_once` and `hidden_beacon_once` as run-level statements: they need the routing law of item 2's registration core (CE-11b, 11b-1).
- Unit 11a-2b, remainder, unchanged: it waits for item 2's registration core and the first-touch re-check of item 2 plan section 20.
- CE-11b's ideal environment: its admissibility predicate (its used markers are exactly the hidden installations it has performed), its hidden-entry splits through the split factoring and the World bridge (with the hidden installation computed explicitly, see the decisions above), and per-command splits for its other commands, re-proved for its handler at `n = 2` along the pattern of the real ones, which are stated at `n = 0`. Its handler differs from the real one in the commands that touch the challenge (design-v2 section 6, recorded in plan section 42): the create at creation index k uses the pre-generated challenge KEM key, the accept at raw-accept ordinal i performs the challenge encapsulation with a uniform shared secret, and owner k's routed decapsulation of the exact challenge ciphertext returns that shared secret.
- Integration into central. The confirming review of the fix pass is done ("CE-11a increment 4: confirming review"), and the committed plan snapshot is refreshed with it, so that it carries plan section 39a (central's snapshot at `80560f92` has it as its second section 39) and sections 40 to 42.

An independent review of `7bb6dee9` with a semantic and a hygiene lens raised one minor finding and nine nits, one of which records the hygiene checks that passed. The fix pass is one Lean commit (`3b53ddd1`) and one documentation commit. It changes no existing statement or definition, removes no theorem and adds five theorems to `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentReal`, each with an exact axiom guard (all five depend on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`), and no assumption or contract, so the counts above (162 theorems; `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentReal` 140) include them. Per finding:

- Minor (the guide overstated how CE-11b can reuse the real per-command splits): the names-for-consumers bullet said that CE-11b reuses `realReg_splits_create`, `realReg_splits_bundle` and `realReg_splits_hmac`, "whose commands the ideal registration runs unchanged", and an open point said that CE-11b's other commands go through the real per-command splits. These lemmas and the `Commands` section they rest on are stated only for `realReg` at `RegImpl KemSK Unit 0`, while CE-11b works at `n = 2` with its own backend state, and the ideal registration's create uses the pre-generated challenge KEM key at creation index k. The consumer bullet and the open point now say that CE-11b reuses the generic tools and the proof pattern of the create, bundle and HMAC laws, re-proves the per-command laws for its handler (or first generalizes them over `n` and the backend state against a generic handler), and that its create differs at index k. A new deviations bullet records that the real instances run with zero hidden slots (`n = 0`), unlike what design m12 expected, and that this is the most direct reason the trivial admissibility predicate is enough. The module docstring of `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentReal`, the analysis and the receipt say the same, and plan section 42 records the revision of m12's expectation.

They now say that CE-11b combines the factoring with `effects_then`, the forwarded part going through `effects_ctx` and the hidden installation through an explicit computation of the World's answer, or with `effects_then_free` after a count-freeness lemma for hidden installations, because `effects_ctx` excludes administrative effects and `countFree_issue` covers only `KnownAdmin` requests.
- Nit ("plan section 39" was ambiguous): the working plan's second section 39 ("CE-11a increment 3 integrated; next work") is now section 39a, and the status paragraph, the re-estimate and the receipt cite section 39a. The committed snapshot on this branch predates that section (central's snapshot at `80560f92` has it as its second section 39); it is refreshed at integration, as the open points say.

## CE-11b increment 1: what is proved

Increment 1 of CE-11b adds three modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 35 guarded theorems; no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains three imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSlots` | 11 | Generic registration cores from slot programs. A slot program (`SlotProgram KemSK Γ n`) answers each registration command as a stateful client of coins and of value requests at item 2's 128-byte DH coordinates (`Coord`, `SlotSpec`); `slotCore prog svc created` turns it into a registration handler on the backend `S × (Alloc Coord n × Γ)` (service state, item 2's first-touch allocator, the program's own backend) by running the program under item 2's allocator `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemSecondEntry.slotImpl` in front of the slot service `svc` (`detach`, `attach`, `slotCore_run`, `detach_attach`, `attach_detach`, `mapBackend_attach`, `detach_mapBackend`). `slot_natural_law` is the in-law variant of item 2's exact-form `slot_natural` (item 2 plan section 21, note 5): a projection of service states that carries every service step to the corresponding step of another service in law, from states satisfying an invariant, and keeps the invariant, carries every client of the allocator in law with the allocator unchanged (one step: `slot_step_law`; invariants: `slot_keeps`, `simulateQ_keeps`); `slot_natural_proj` bundles the premise as CE-11a's `ServiceProj`. `slotCore_natural`: every core built by `slotCore` satisfies CE-11a's `CoreNatural`, the premise of `PqxdhDeploymentSeedSplit.represent` and `represent_split`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealParams` | 11 | The challenger's draws of route R3 (CE-11b design section 3.1) and the draw swap of item 2's prefix (section 8.3, step 1). `Target count` is a raw-accept ordinal and a KEM creation index (`Target.ofCE` reads it from a CE designation with `PqxdhDeploymentKemTransfer.selectedKem`; `ofCE_kem`, `ofCE_accept`); `Params KemSK count` holds the selected KEM key pair, the family of the other `count` key pairs and the challenge ciphertext, drawn by `drawParams` in item 2's order and type. The pool `Params.pool t p` is the list `List.ofFn (t.kem.insertNth chosen others)` (`pool_eq`, which only unfolds the definition), the form in which item 2's prephase builds the stopped router's reservation (`preOpaquePool` passes this list to the router by definition, and `preOpaque_pool` relates it to item 2's prephase), with one entry per creation index (`pool_length`), the selected pair at index `t.kem` (`pool_kem`) and the other pairs in index order (`pool_other`). `preOpaquePool` is item 2's stopped-source prephase `sourcePreOpaque` with the family as an argument; `preOpaque_pool` moves the family draw before the Server constructor (a failed constructor discards it), `draws_swap` moves the challenge encapsulation before the prephase, so item 2's prefix `sharedStart` has the law of `drawParams` followed by the prephase at the drawn pool (`startPool`), and `slotGame_pool` lifts this to item 2's `n`-slot ideal world: `𝒟[slotGame … n] = 𝒟[drawParams >>= slotGameAt … n]`, where `slotGameAt` is the same world at fixed parameters (`entryClientAt`; tools `slot_liftComp`, `internalize_run`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentDraw` | 13 | C1 for environments that draw a parameter at startup. `worldRest` is the record world's program after the startup (`program_rest`); `join_from` is CE-11a's `join` from any World that differs from the initial one only in its counts (the World after counted startup coins); `join_draw` (with `join_draw_observed`, `join_draw_analysis`): if the environment's startup is a draw followed by the actual Server constructor with the initial backend `init p`, and for each drawn `p` a stable predicate `inv p` holds initially and the environment splits the registration handler `reg p` relative to it, then the draw followed by the common deployment experiment with `reg p` has the law of the record world, at the full reassembled state. The parameter in the backend: `paramReg`, `paramStep`, `paramInv` turn families indexed by a parameter into one handler, step and predicate that read it from the backend (`withX`, `dropX`, `withE`, `dropE`; `merge_withE`, `good_withE`, `stable_param`, `splits_param`), the experiment with the parameterized handler is the experiment at the parameter (`gameWith_param`, through `handlerWith_param` and `simulateQ_embed`), and `join_param` is C1 for `paramEnv`, the environment that draws the parameter at startup and keeps it in its backend. |

## CE-11b increment 1: decisions and deviations

- Module layout. The CE-11b design puts `Params` and `drawParams` in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSteps` (unit U1) and `draws_swap` in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCut` (unit U5). They are in their own module `PqxdhDeploymentIdealParams`, because they do not depend on the CE-native step definitions that the spike still has to settle; `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSteps` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCut` keep the rest of their units. The module name was checked unused and registered first, as were `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSlots` and `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentDraw`.
- `envGD` without `bindStart`. The design (section 6.1) defines `envGD t := bindStart (drawParams …) (envG t)` on the environment state `P × E` and proves `run_bindStart`, an equality in law of `World.observed` of the World run of `bindStart draw envs` and of `draw` followed by the World run of `envs p`. That needs a World-level fact that no central lemma provides: the World counts the startup draw (uniform and effect counts), so the two runs differ in their counts, and an equality of the observed projections needs that no World step reads the counts. Instead, the drawn parameter is kept in the environment's backend (state `EnvState KemSK (P × Bk)`), and C1 is proved directly against the native side: `join_draw` handles the counted startup draw exactly as CE-11a's `join` handles the identity seed (through `coins_world` and a World that differs from the initial one only in its counts), and `join_param` packages the parameterized handler and step. For CE-11b, `envGD t` is `paramEnv drawParams (fun p => idealStep t p) …`, still one environment per target that draws `p` itself, so the headline form of plan section 9 is unchanged, and the startup draw still takes the frozen `Frame` startup route (`coinLift`; `join_draw` passes it through `coins_world`). The startup bound changes: `envG`'s `Frame` startup bound is `qInit + 32`, as agreed with the record-lift line, while `envGD`'s is `qInit + 32 + (count + 1)·kg + ec`, where `kg` and `ec` are the interpreted costs of one KEM key generation and one encapsulation under `KemBound`, for the `count + 1` key pairs and the challenge encapsulation that `drawParams` draws (design-v2 section 6.3, `frame_idealD`; amendment A2). The review fix pass records A2 as accepted and the matching notice to the record-lift line (N2) as settled within Claude's work, since that line is Claude's since plan section 40. The startup coins do not enter C4's hash bound.
- What `join_param` replaces. `join_param`, instantiated with `regs := fun p => idealSplit t p` (the erased split handler of design-v2 section 5.2), `steps := fun p => idealStep t p` and `inv := fun _ => IdealInv t`, with the per-parameter splits `ideal_splits`, replaces design-v2's `c1_split` and `run_bindStart`. It is not `c1_ideal` by itself: its left side is the draw followed by the experiment with `idealSplit t p`, not `CE.idealGame g`. `c1_ideal` composes `ideal_to_split` (C7 `erase_ideal`, then `split_game`; unit U2), pointwise in the drawn parameters under `drawParams` (`LawEq.bind`), with `join_param` (unit U3).
- Further consequences of `paramEnv` (planned work). The central consequences of the join that assume the startup is exactly the Server constructor (premise `born : env.start = PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment.start …`) do not apply to `paramEnv`, whose startup is `draw >>= …`: `c3_shared_cache` (C3), `join_reveal` (the run-phase reveal projection), `join_later` (C6) and `join_later_public`. C3 and the reveal projection for an environment that draws at startup follow from `join_draw` (through `join_param` for `paramEnv`) by a map, as the central versions follow from `join`; they are planned in unit U3 (about half an agent-day together). C6 for `envGD` needs a new generic `join_later_param` (or `join_later_draw`), built from `join_later`'s proof with a `join_from`-style counted startup and a `laterWith_param` analogue of `gameWith_param`; it is planned in the deferred unit 11b-L before `c6_ideal` (about 0.5-1 agent-day more). So CE-11a's generic `join_later` meets the C6 commitment made to the record-lift line for the later phase only for environments whose startup is the Server constructor, and design-v2 section 10.1, which said that only the instance waits, is corrected. Stating C6 per parameter on `envG t p` and averaging afterwards is no shortcut: it needs the World count-obliviousness fact that this deviation avoids.
- The C5 support transport (planned work). The design's `run_bindStart_support` (C5 on the supports of the plain and of the provenance-annotated World run, unit U3) has no counterpart yet. It is planned in unit U4 as an invariant argument over `paramEnv`'s startup support and the annotated World handler: a startup-support lemma (after the startup, the environment state is `envInitial created (p, init created)` and the World equals the initial one up to its counts; the reasoning of `join_draw`, not yet a lemma) and an annotated analogue of `run_keeps` (`keeps_simulateQ` on the annotated World step, or the transport `schedule_erase`). Central `run_keeps` covers only the plain World handler `PqxdhRecordLiftWorld.handler`.
- Publication order. The design (sections 8.5 and 11) runs the spike first and publishes U1 only after the spike's exit criteria are met, each unit reviewed and integrated before the next is published. This increment publishes generic, spike-independent parts (the generic parts of U1 and U3, and U5 step 1, which is the spike's item (b)) while the spike's items (d), (e) and (f) are open and its item (a) exists only in scratch (`create_merge_pool` proved, the pool compile simulation stated, not proved). The published statements do not depend on the spike's outcome. If the spike forces route P-b, the draws stay as they are on item 2's side and in `envGD`, because P-b changes only how the CE-side prefix reaches them (through an equality of a lazily and an eagerly drawn pool), so `Params`, `slotGame_pool` and `paramEnv` are expected to stay in use. If it forces a change of route R3 itself, they could go unused; they would then stay published as true but unused statements (nothing is deleted), and the guide would list them as unused.
- `slotGame_pool` states the draw swap for item 2's world in the measure notation `𝒟[…]`, the form of the design's `ideal_compile` statement; `draws_swap` and `preOpaque_pool` are equalities in law (`LawEq`) of the prefix.
- `simulateQ_keeps` restates `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentJoin.keeps_simulateQ` so that `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSlots` does not import the environment modules; consumers that import both should cite `keeps_simulateQ`.

## CE-11b increment 1: names for consumers

- For the CE-11b units: `SlotProgram`, `slotCore`, `slotCore_natural` (the `CoreNatural` premise of `represent_split` for the ideal registration, which is a `slotCore`); `Target`, `Target.ofCE`, `Params`, `Params.pool`, `pool_kem`, `pool_other`, `drawParams`; `draws_swap`, `slotGame_pool`, `slotGameAt` (the prefix of the second compile lemma, pointwise in the drawn parameters); `paramEnv`, `join_param`, `join_draw`, `join_draw_observed`, `join_draw_analysis` (the environment side of C1 for `envGD`; `c1_ideal` composes `join_param` with `ideal_to_split`, see the decisions above).
- For any environment that draws at startup: `join_draw` needs only the shape of the startup and per-draw `Splits`; `join_from` gives CE-11a's join from a World that has already counted startup coins.

## CE-11b increment 1: contracts used

None. The modules use item 2's published prefix, allocator and lazy table, CE-11a's slot services, join and World, VCVio's lazy oracles and the exchange and removal of independent draws (`LazyOracleDiscard.lawEq_swap`, `lawEq_drop`); no codec law, and no new assumption or contract.

## CE-11b increment 1: spike

The spike of the CE-11b design (section 8.5) runs before the CE-native steps are published. Its item (b) is the module `PqxdhDeploymentIdealParams` above. Items (a), (c) and (d) produced checked scratch definitions, not published:

- The selected accept. On a Phase 1 message whose pure verification (`PqxdhRegistrationJointRawSignatures.verified`, which `verifyInput_source` shows is what the native verification computes) yields the challenger's selected PQ key, the CE-native accept runs the native steps before encapsulation (`beforeLogged` under the native Server callbacks); on success, item 2's candidate-free suffix (`PqxdhRegistrationJointKemOpaqueSuffix.program`) with the challenge ciphertext and the challenge marker under the native callbacks, and item 2's marker tail (`PqxdhRegistrationJointKemOpaqueTail.tail`) under a CE-native resume tail whose challenge-marked root request reads the hidden value at the four DH coordinates through the allocator, marks the session with those coordinates and returns the sentinel root (item 2's `resumeTail`). On every other Phase 1 message the step is the LQ-F logged accept, by definition (scratch lemma `acceptSelected_logged`). So the accept case of the post-phase simulation compares two interpreters of the same two programs.
- The marked build: the native build with the session's response tail given by the hidden service at the session's value (with the native services at a root it is `nativeBuild` exactly, scratch lemma `buildHidden_native`).
- Owner k's challenge finish: item 2's grouped Beacon owner at the native owner type, with native preparation callbacks in which the decapsulation of exactly the challenge ciphertext answers the challenge marker without reading a key, and every other ciphertext is decapsulated with the owner's secret key; the grouped request is answered as item 2's `groupStep` (a hidden read at the four DH coordinates and the hidden continuation for the marker, the native root and continuation for a known decapsulation result).

- The prefix route (item (a)). The pool create (`PqxdhRegistrationSchedule.create` with the KEM key pair read from the pool at the new owner's index) commutes with CE-3's merge (scratch lemma `create_merge_pool`, proved like `PqxdhDeploymentCompileRegistration.create_merge`, because merging keeps the number of owners), and the pool variant of CE-3's compile simulation is stated (scratch `compile_simulation_pool_stmt`, both handlers with the pool create). The route P-a (pointwise in the pool) is kept. The layers it re-parametrizes are three: CE-3's compile simulation (the create case of the step lemma and of the invariant's preservation; for every other command the two handlers agree on every query the compiled step issues, which CE-3's per-kind budget of the compiled steps gives), CE-3's game wrappers (`compile_game`, `compile_full_game`), and the initialized source-to-native step (`PqxdhRegistrationJointInitializedSchedule.initialized_native`, with the source's KEM creations answered from the pool, `PqxdhLiveKemReservation.reserved`). From there item 2's existing pointwise steps apply: `PqxdhDynamicKemJointSource.program_source` (the reserved creations are the normal router at the same pool), the stopped router up to the selected accept, and `exposeAt_scan`, `scan_opaque`, `preRaw_opaque` to the prechallenge context of `sourcePreOpaque`; the CE-side scan and its compile image are new but generic.

Not done: the proof of the pool compile simulation, item (d)'s post-phase simulation of owner k's challenge finish, item (e) (a record command on the hidden session), item (f) (a non-challenge finish of owner k through the router and the CCA evaluation), and the faithfulness lemma of the CE-native finish on non-challenge ciphertexts (through `PqxdhBeaconPhase2Shared.owner_realization` and a native analogue of item 2's grouping lemma).

## CE-11b increment 1: open points

- Unit U1, rest: `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSteps` with `IdealCore` (accept counter, active flag, marks with coordinates, the two install ghosts), `SplitBk`, `splitInit`, `createPool` (creation index j reads pool entry j), the three CE-native hidden steps of the spike, `idealProg`, `idealReg`, `idealGame`, `idealCoreErased`, `erasedHidden`.
- Units U2 to U7 as in the CE-11b design, with the changes of the decisions above: C7, the split and the seed deferral with `seed_at_install`, and `ideal_to_split` (U2, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeys`); `idealStep`, `envG`, `envGD` as `paramEnv`, `IdealInv`, the per-command splits `ideal_splits`, `c1_ideal` as `ideal_to_split` under `drawParams` composed with `join_param` (which replaces `c1_split` and `run_bindStart`), C2a with `frame_idealD` at the startup bound `qInit + 32 + (count + 1)·kg + ec`, C3 and the reveal projection for `paramEnv` as maps of `join_draw` (through `join_param`), C4, the single-install facts (U3, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv`); C5 as `IdealSlots` with the anomaly bound, its support transport for `paramEnv` as an invariant argument over the startup support and the annotated World handler (U4, `PqxdhDeploymentIdealProvenance`); the CE-side scan, the pool-variant prefix and `prefix_compile` (U5); the post phase and `ideal_compile` (U6); the hand-off `ideal_world` (U7).

## CE-11b increment 1: review fix pass

An independent semantic, hygiene and composition review of `9f89f395` found no error in the Lean statements or proofs: each module checks with every exact axiom guard (only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`), theorems and guards correspond one to one, no assumption or contract is new, no central module changed, and scratch probes confirm that `join_param` is not vacuous (all its premises are discharged with the real registration), that `slotCore_natural` feeds `represent_split`, and that `slotGame_pool` composes at CE-9's instantiation with S7 and stage T at `n = 2`. Its findings concern the documentation, the plan and three docstrings. The fix pass changes no Lean statement or proof.

- Minor (`c1_ideal` is not `join_param`): this guide, plan section 47 and the open points said that `c1_ideal` becomes `join_param`. `join_param`'s premise relates `idealStep t p` to the erased split handler `idealSplit t p`, so its left side is not `CE.idealGame g`. ## CE-11b increment 2: what is proved

Increment 2 adds one module under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 19 guarded theorems (17 at `caad5a07`; the review fix pass strengthens two of them and adds two); no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains one import). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSteps` | 19 | The ideal registration for a target `t` (a raw-accept ordinal and a KEM creation index) and the challenger's draws `p` (design-v2 section 3), as a slot program `idealProg t p hidden created` with its own backend `IdealCore` (raw-accept counter, active flag, hidden Server session with the coordinates of its hidden read, and the Server and Beacon install ghosts, on which no reply and no non-ghost state depends). Create reads the new owner's KEM key pair from the pool (`createPool`; beyond the pool it is the native create, `createPool_beyond`; `createReg` is the create command); bundle and HMAC are the real commands (`realRegAt`, the real registration handler on any backend and slot count, equal to `PqxdhDeploymentBackend.realReg` at the unit backend by `realRegAt_real`; `idealProg_real`). The raw accept compares the pre-increment counter with the target ordinal (`acceptStep`; `acceptStep_other`, `acceptStep_accepts`); the selected accept is `acceptExposed`: on a Phase 1 message whose pure verification yields the selected PQ key, the native steps before encapsulation and, when they reach the encapsulation, the active path (item 2's candidate-free suffix with the challenge ciphertext and the challenge marker, then item 2's marker tail under `tailCE`, whose challenge-marked root request reads the hidden value at the root input's coordinates, marks the session with them and returns the sentinel root); on every other message the LQ-F logged accept (`acceptExposed_logged`). The build of the marked session with a pending token reads its hidden value and runs `buildHidden`, the native build with the hidden build service (`buildHidden_native`); a published peer sets the Server install ghost; every other build is real (`buildStep_real`: every build while no session is marked, `buildStep_unmarked`, and, once a session is marked, every build of another session and every build of the marked session without a pending token, `buildStep_other`). After an active selected accept, a finish of the selected owner in the `initSent` phase runs `finishChallenge`: item 2's grouped Beacon owner at the native owner type, whose preparation answers the decapsulation of exactly the challenge ciphertext with the challenge marker (`preparationCE_challenge`) and decapsulates every other ciphertext as item 2's CCA evaluation does (`preparationCE_eval`), and whose grouped request reads the hidden value at the four DH coordinates and runs the hidden continuation on a challenge marker (`groupCE`); an establishment after a hidden read sets the Beacon install ghost; every other finish is real (`finishStep_real`). `idealCore`, `idealReg` (over item 2's persistent lazy table), `erasedHidden`, `idealCoreErased` and the naturality of both cores (`idealCore_natural`, `idealCoreErased_natural`, the premise of `represent_split`); `idealGame` is `CE.idealGame g` (the challenger's draws, then `gameWith` with `idealReg` at the target of `g` from `idealInit`, identity index 0). `tailCE_challenge`, `groupCE_challenge` and `groupCE_known` relate the hidden steps to item 2's services skeleton under its public-table layer (`publicLocal_coins`): the challenge-marked root request answers as `resumeTail` and the challenge-marked grouped finish as `groupStep`, both with the factored services, and a known decapsulation result is item 2's native grouped answer `groupNativeN`. The two challenge lemmas are joint equations: the replies are equal, item 2's game state is the CE game state without ghosts, and the CE-native Extract family state and item 2's table cache are unchanged. For the root request the CE mark is item 2's mark with the coordinates in place of the value, and the whole CE-native state is unchanged; for the grouped finish the recorded coordinates become the four DH coordinates (when the DH outputs are nonzero) and item 2's marks are unchanged. |

## CE-11b increment 2: decisions and deviations

- The selected accept branches on the pure verification of the Phase 1 message (`PqxdhRegistrationJointRawSignatures.verified`; item 2's scan uses the same function, and `verifyInput_source` shows that it is what the native verification computes) before it runs anything. On every message without the selected PQ key it is the LQ-F logged accept itself, so `acceptExposed_logged` holds by definition and the fallback of design risk R3 (an extra equality for a restructured non-selected path) is not needed.
- `active` is set exactly when the selected accept reaches its encapsulation with the key match, which is when item 2's `postSkel` takes its active branch (critique S12). A rejection before the encapsulation leaves the accept inactive.
- The hidden Server session is marked at the private root request of the selected accept, as item 2's `resumeTail` marks it; design-v2 section 3.2 says "when the accept returns a session". A mark whose session never receives a pending token is never built (the build of a marked session requires its pending token), so nothing observable changes, and the post-phase relation compares the two marks directly (`tailCE_challenge`: the CE mark records the coordinates where item 2's mark records the value read there).
- The finish of the selected owner is hidden only in the `initSent` phase (design-v2 section 3.2); in every other phase it is the real finish, which in those phases changes nothing (the grouped owner, like the native owner, acts only in `initSent`). The grouped request records the coordinates of its hidden read in the step's state, so the Beacon install ghost is set exactly after an establishment that followed a hidden read; an establishment from a known decapsulation result is a known install and sets no ghost.
- `realRegAt` is a copy of `PqxdhDeploymentBackend.realReg` on every backend and slot count (central's `realReg` is fixed at the unit backend and no slot); `realRegAt_real` shows that it is `realReg` there. Its CTX obliviousness, which C7 needs, is not yet proved for other backends (open points).
- The agreement lemmas with item 2's skeleton (`tailCE_challenge`, `groupCE_challenge`, `groupCE_known`, with the tool `publicLocal_coins`) are per-step facts of unit U6's post-challenge simulation; they are published in U1 because they are statements about U1's definitions and are the spike's evidence for items (c), (d) and (f). `preparationCE_challenge` and `preparationCE_eval` likewise.
- Beyond the pool `createPool` draws a fresh key pair, as the native create does (`createPool_beyond`); the compile lemma assumes the budget, under which this branch is not taken.
- Publication before review: increment 2 was pushed as one Lean commit and two documentation commits for review; the review fix pass adds a merge of central, one Lean commit and one documentation commit; the confirming review's nits add one commit (module docstrings, inventory row and documentation); it is not integrated at this commit.

## CE-11b increment 2: spike

The spike of the CE-11b design (section 8.5) is finished. Its outcome, item by item:

- (a) Prefix route. Route P-a (pointwise in the pool) is kept; P-b stays the fallback. The first of the three layers P-a re-parametrizes, CE-3's whole compile lemma at the native level with both creates reading the pool, is proved in scratch (about 260 lines): every non-create deployment command compiles to a program without a create query (from CE-3's per-kind budget `PqxdhDeploymentCompileBudget.step_bound`), so the pool handlers agree with CE-3's handlers on every compiled step but the create, whose case is the commutation of the pool create with CE-3's merge and the preservation of CE-3's invariant by the pool create; the step lemma, the simulation, the invariant preservation and the native compile lemma follow as in CE-3. The second layer (CE-3's game wrappers) is short. The third (the source side down to item 2's reservation form) consists of inductions over the program in which only the create case changes: the create cases of `PqxdhRegistrationJointServerClient.program_source` and `PqxdhRegistrationJointServerSchedule.program_native`, the initialized wrappers and the link to `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemInitialization.commands`.
- (b) The draw swap: done in increment 1.
- (c) The selected accept: `acceptExposed` with `acceptExposed_logged`, and `tailCE_challenge` (published).
- (d) Owner k's challenge finish: `finishChallenge` and `groupCE_challenge` (published).
- (e) A record command on the hidden session: item 2's skeleton answer to a kernel reveal is the native scheduler's answer, because the reveal reads only the peers (proved in scratch); with CE-3's record step this covers the record commands of the hidden session.
- (f) A non-challenge finish of owner k: `preparationCE_eval` and `groupCE_known` (published); the split of item 2's native grouped answer against `groupStep` is item 2's existing `split_group`.
- (g) The measured re-estimate is the section "CE-11b increment 2: re-estimate".

The spike also identified the shape of U6's post-challenge relation: item 2's `SplitN` relation (a native state is the join of the table cache and the skeleton state, up to the analysis observers), extended by the ghost seals, the marks as coordinates against marks as values, the allocator and table below the slot service, the post-challenge router state and the two install ghosts.

## CE-11b increment 2: open points

- Unit U2 (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeys`): C7 for the ideal registration (`idealPair`, `erase_ideal`), the `represent_split` instance, `seed_move`, the conditional-read kernel identities, the two-point deferral, `split_game`, `seed_at_install` and `ideal_to_split`. It needs, first, the CTX obliviousness of `realRegAt` on every backend (central proves it for `realReg` at the unit backend only) and a commutation lemma that carries the obliviousness of a slot program through `slotCore`. Both exist as checked scratch proofs, not published: `realRegAt` on any backend is `realReg` on the state with the backend and the seed table forgotten and then reattached, so CE-11a's `commutes_focus` transports `realReg_commutes_ctx`; a coins-only slot step runs under the allocator with the allocator and the service state unchanged, so `commutes_focus` carries its obliviousness through `slotCore`; with them the create, bundle and HMAC commands of the ideal core commute with completions of the CTX cache over every slot service. Still open for C7: the selected accept as a frame of the CTX cache, the hidden build through CE-11a's `buildFactor_commutes_ctx`, the pointed form of owner k's challenge finish (`finishFactor_pointed` lifted by `factor_focus`, `factor_prefix` and `factor_suffix`, through `slotCore`), the dispatch of the conditional steps, and the equality of the erased pair with `idealCoreErased`.
- Unit U5: the second and third layers of route P-a, the glue to item 2's prephase and the CE-side scan; the first layer is in scratch and is published with U5.
- Unit U6: the post-challenge relation and the flattening of item 2's post-challenge stack, the per-command simulation and the decision step; the per-step facts above are in place.
- That a run reads hidden values at no more than two coordinates (the selected accept's, and the selected Beacon's challenge finish's; the marked build reads the accept's coordinates again) is not proved: it is the allocator part (I5) of U3's planned invariant `IdealInv` (design-v2 section 6.2), whose preservation uses that a finish from the `initSent` phase always ends established or aborted, so the selected Beacon's challenge finish reads at most once.
- The faithfulness of `finishChallenge` to the native finish on a raw message whose ciphertext is not the challenge is not proved; no planned consumer needs it (U6 compares against item 2's grouped form).
- Units U3, U4, U7, U8 and the deferred unit 11b-L as in increment 1's open points. The design's questions Q1 to Q3 are decided (Claude decisions under the owner's standing instruction, section 49 of the plan snapshot [common-deployment-experiment-plan.md](common-deployment-experiment-plan.md)): Q1, the later-quantum (LQ-F) headlines are scheduled after the classical main line, with item 2's ideal-key transfer over the logged LQ-F source, a later-quantum compile lemma, unit 11b-L and the record lift's later-quantum lifts; Q2, option (b): CE-12a bounds the anomaly probability by a predict-then-compare IND-CCA reduction under the existing IND-CCA assumption, so `ctGuess` does not appear as an unbounded headline term, and U4 still delivers `anomaly_le`; Q3, U8 (CE's own key-first and key-last) is built in native form.
