## CE-11b increment 2: review fix pass

An independent review (semantic and hygiene) of `2445723b` found no error in the Lean statements or proofs. The reviewers rechecked the module (exit 0, empty log, 17 theorems and 17 exact guards) and the root import, read the definitions against central and the Rust reference (the order of the create draws and the owner index, the accept ordinal, the branch on the pure verification, the active flag, the session mark, the first touches, the preparation, the finish, the erased services and `idealGame` under A1), and proved in scratch that at the selected ordinal a message without the selected PQ key gives the real accept and that the marked build with the native hidden services is one allocator read followed by the real build, up to the Server install ghost. Their findings and the fixes:

- Minor: `tailCE_challenge` and `groupCE_challenge` proved less than their docstrings, this guide and the analysis said: they dropped the CE-native Extract family state, item 2's table cache, item 2's marks and the recorded coordinates. Both are strengthened while the module is unintegrated (below), and the docstrings, the "what is proved" row and the analysis now say exactly what the statements give.
- Minor: "every other build is real" was attributed to `buildStep_unmarked`, which covers only the states with no marked session. `buildStep_other` covers the builds while a session is marked (of another session, or of the marked session without a pending token), and `buildStep_real` combines both.
- Minor (reported twice): the guide cited plan section 49, then only in the working plan, for the decisions on Q1 to Q3. The open points now state the decisions, and the committed plan snapshot is refreshed through section 51.
- Nit (reported twice): the analysis said that only the selected accept and the selected Beacon's finish read hidden values; the marked build reads one too, at the accept's coordinates. Reworded, and the two-coordinate bound is listed as part of U3's planned invariant (open points).
- Nit (reported twice): commit counts after `2445723b`. The status paragraph and the publication bullet now count every commit, including those of this fix pass.
- Nit: "ghosts that no step reads" is inaccurate, since the steps that set a ghost read its earlier value. The module docstring, the `IdealCore` docstring and this guide now say that no reply and no non-ghost state depends on the ghosts.
- Nit: the receipt's `commits_note` and plan hash were stale. The receipt is regenerated: it records the working plan's hash at generation with a dated copy in scratch, keeps a reconstructed copy of the plan at the earlier recorded hash, and says that the commit which last regenerated it is not listed.
- Nit (scratch only): design-v2 section 16 stood after Appendix A; it is moved before it, and a section 17 records the corrections of this fix pass.
- Two records of the reviewers' checks: no change needed.

Changed statements. Both are equations of distributions between the CE-native step and item 2's skeleton step under its public-table layer, and both sides of each are now mapped to longer tuples that contain the old ones, so the old statement follows by mapping both sides with a projection (`Functor.map_map`; checked in scratch, `ce11b/inc2fix/probe/OldFromNew.lean`). Below, `CE` is the CE-native step run from `(native, recorded)` and `item2` is item 2's step run from `(clean native.2, marks)` and the table cache `cache`.

- `tailCE_challenge`, before: `(fun r => (r.1, clean r.2.1.2, r.2.2)) <$> CE = (fun r => (r.1.1, r.1.2.1, mark r.1.2.2)) <$> item2`, where `mark` replaces the value in item 2's mark by `coordsOf input`. After: `(fun r => (r.1, r.2.1, clean r.2.1.2, r.2.2, cache)) <$> CE = (fun r => (r.1.1, native, r.1.2.1, mark r.1.2.2, r.2)) <$> item2`. It adds, jointly with the old components, that the whole CE-native state stays `native` and that item 2's table cache stays `cache`.
- `groupCE_challenge`, before: `(fun r => (r.1, clean r.2.1.2)) <$> CE = (fun r => (r.1.1, r.1.2.1)) <$> item2`. After: `(fun r => (r.1, r.2.1.1, clean r.2.1.2, r.2.2, marks, cache)) <$> CE = (fun r => (r.1.1, native.1, r.1.2.1, coords, r.1.2.2, r.2)) <$> item2`, where `coords` is the four DH coordinates when the DH outputs are nonzero and `recorded` otherwise. It adds that the CE-native Extract family state stays `native.1`, that the CE recorded coordinates become `coords`, and that item 2's marks and table cache are unchanged.
- New: `buildStep_other` and `buildStep_real`, with the conclusion of `buildStep_unmarked` under the hypotheses stated above.

Checks. Central `98b906d9` was merged into the topic first (merge commit `2655cb0f`): the conflicts were only at the hot spots (root imports, inventory rows, check-inventory blocks, analysis sections) and were resolved with both sides whole and central's first, then `inv recount` (handwritten Lean files 1673 to 1674) and `inv rehash` (root and `check-inventory.sh`) ran in that order. At the merge the module and the root import were checked with Lean 4.34 (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) through the coordination wrapper into a fresh prefix that mirrors the first prefix of central's published `LEAN_PATH` at `98b906d9` by resolved symlinks (both logs empty), and the focused `check-inventory` passed. After the Lean change the module was checked into a second prefix of the same kind (19 theorems, 19 exact guards allowing only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, empty log), the root import against it (empty log) and the scratch projection probe (exit 0); `check-inventory.sh` gained the two theorem rows and the counts 19, the module row and `check-inventory.sh` were rehashed, and the focused `check-inventory` passed on the Lean commit with a clean tree and again with this documentation in the working tree; `git diff --check` is clean. ## CE-11b increment 2: confirming review

A confirming review of the fix pass at `e29c26fa` rechecked the module without `-o` against central's published `LEAN_PATH` at `98b906d9` (exit 0, empty log; 19 theorems and 19 exact guards allowing only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`), compared the receipt's hashes with the branch head and the working plan, rechecked every inventory digest, and derived in scratch the earlier statements of the two challenge lemmas from the strengthened ones by projection. It found every finding of the review of `2445723b` resolved in the Lean: only `tailCE_challenge` and `groupCE_challenge` changed, both strengthened; `buildStep_other` and `buildStep_real` were added; no definition changed. It raised one minor documentation finding and two nits, which one commit addresses before the integration. That commit changes three docstrings of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSteps` (the module docstring, the `IdealCore` docstring and the docstring of the section on the agreement with item 2's skeleton) and no statement, proof or definition; the module's inventory row is rehashed. Per item:

- Minor: four summaries (the module docstring, the section docstring on the agreement with item 2's skeleton, the "what is proved" row of this guide and the analysis) said that the grouped finish's recorded coordinates correspond to item 2's marks, but `groupCE_challenge` states no relation between them: it sets the recorded coordinates to the four DH coordinates when the DH outputs are nonzero (and leaves them unchanged otherwise), and it leaves item 2's marks unchanged. All four now say that for the root request the CE mark is item 2's mark with the coordinates in place of the value, and that for the grouped finish the recorded coordinates become the four DH coordinates (when the DH outputs are nonzero) and item 2's marks are unchanged.
- Nit: the ghost parentheticals that the fix pass added to the module docstring and the `IdealCore` docstring described the wrong precedence: a step that sets a ghost overwrites its earlier value (`Option.or` prefers its first argument). Both now say that a step that may set one reads its earlier value only to keep it when it sets nothing.
- Nit: the receipt's `focused_inventory_note` lacked a sentence boundary before "Review fix pass:". The receipt is regenerated with it and also records the confirming review and this commit.
- Record of the confirming review (original findings resolved, checks run): no action.

Checks. The changed module was checked with Lean 4.34 (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) through the coordination wrapper without `-o` against central's published `LEAN_PATH` at `98b906d9` (exit 0, empty log, so every exact guard still matches); `inv recount` (unchanged, 1674) and `inv rehash` (the module row changes; the root, `trusted-boundary.md` and `check-inventory.sh` are unchanged) ran in that order; the focused `check-inventory` passed with the changes of this commit under `beaconcrypt-core/` in the working tree; `git diff --check` is clean. The committed plan snapshot is refreshed from the working plan, whose section 51 now records the confirming review. The integration's own checks run on the topic tip after this commit.

## CE-11b unit U5: what is proved

Unit U5 adds three modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 85 guarded theorems (76 at `86485fe1`, nine added by the review fix pass); no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains three imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPool` | 11 | The first layer of route P-a (design-v2 section 8.3): CE-3's compile lemma with both creates reading the challenger's pool through `PqxdhDeploymentIdealSteps.createPool` (the spike's scratch proof, published). The pool handlers are the registration scheduler, source14's native handler and the native deployment handler with the pool create (`handlerPool`, `revealPool`, `deploymentPool`) and the compiled handler under the pool source14 handler (`compiledPool`). Every deployment command other than `create` compiles to a source14 program without a create request (`compile_noCreate`, from CE-3's per-kind budget `PqxdhDeploymentCompileBudget.step_bound`), so the pool handlers agree with CE-3's on every compiled step but the create (`revealPool_agree`, `compiledPool_other`, through the generic `simulateQ_congr_none`). The pool create commutes with CE-3's merge (`create_merge_pool`) and appends one fresh owner (`createPool_support`); the step lemma, the simulation and the invariant preservation follow as in CE-3 (`step_merge_pool`, `compile_simulation_pool`, `good_create_pool`, `good_step_pool`), and `compile_native_pool` is `PqxdhDeploymentCompileInvariant.compile_native` for the pool handlers. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPoolSource` | 27 | The third layer of route P-a: the pool carried through item 2's source layers down to item 2's KEM command program under item 2's stopped selected-first router at the pool. Erasure: the annotated source schedule with the pool create (`sourcePool`, `createSourcePool`, `materialPool`) erases to source14's native execution with the pool create (`createPool_install`, `createSourcePool_native`, `sourcePool_native`, `programPool_native`). Encoding: the open command backend executed with KEM creations answered from the pool (`runWith`, `executeWith` with `reservedAt`, item 2's `PqxdhLiveKemReservation.reserved`) has the encoded distribution of the annotated pool source execution (`program_reserved`) from a state with the source-side invariant `PoolOwned` (item 2's source ownership facts `PqxdhRegistrationJointServerSchedule.Owned` and one KEM key pair per owner); the create case is `create_reserved` (the indexed constructor reads the pool at the KEM pool's size, `client_reserved`, `compile_create`, `compile_poolLift`), every other request compiles to a private-service program without a KEM creation (`client_noCreate`, from `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemCommands.creations`), on which the reserved and the actual services agree (`isCreate_class`, `reserved_agree`, `reserved_noCreate`, `coins_reserved`, `executeWith_noCreate`), so item 2's `PqxdhRegistrationJointServerClient.query_source` applies (`query_reserved`); `source_counts` and `query_poolOwned` keep the invariant. Router: a private-service program within the pool and without KEM reveal, run by `PqxdhDynamicKemJointRouter.stopped` at the pairs from the router state of a prefix pool (`routerOf`, `PoolPrefix`, `empty_prefix`), is its run under the reserved services, with every reply present and the router state of the final pool, as an exact equality (`stopped_step`, `reserved_step_prefix`, `stopped_program`; `reserved_program_prefix` for supports). Composition: `kemCommands_reserved` and `kemCommands_stopped` for item 2's KEM command program of a source program that reads no identity or KEM field and creates no more KEM keys than the pool holds. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCut` | 47 | The cut of both sides of the second compile lemma at the selected raw accept, pointwise in the challenger's draws. The CE-side scan `scanCE` returns the raw bytes and continuation of the raw accept at a position unissued (`finishCE`, `scanCE_finish`, `run_scanCE`, `scanCE_pure`, `scanCE_accept_zero`, `scanCE_accept_succ`, `scanCE_other`); item 2's source accept scan of CE-3's compiled program is the compiled CE scan (`compile_scan`, `compileCut`; `scan_query_other`, `scan_bind_noAccept`, `compile_noAccept`). Before the cut the ideal registration is the real one with the pool create: every request other than the selected accept, from a state in which the selected accept has not run (`PreCut`), projects to the native deployment handler with the pool create (`preStep`, through `slotImpl_lift`, `slotCore_lift`, `realRegAt_base`, `realRegAt_keeps`, `onBase_base`, `idealReg_lift`, `idealReg_real`), and so does the scan up to the selected accept, with the backend changed only in its accept counter (`preScan`, `bump`, `withAccepts`). The LQ-F ghosts are written but never read, so no run of the ideal handler depends on them (`idealRun_unghost`, through `run_reset`, `unghost`, `realRegAt_clean`, `realRegAt_unghost`, `onBase_unghost`, `onSlots_unghost`, `slotCore_unghost`, `idealProg_unghost`, `idealStep_unghost`). Hence `ideal_cut`: the ideal run is the pool scan followed by the ideal game resumed from a canonical state at the cut (`cutState`, `resumeAt`). The pool scan is the erasure of the annotated pool source execution of the compiled scan (`pool_scan_source`), and the source prefix of item 2's opaque scan under the stopped router at the pool is its encoding (`stopped_scan_source`), so both sides share one computation, `commonPrefix`. `ideal_prefix`: the common deployment experiment with the ideal registration at the draws (`gameWith` with `idealReg`) is item 2's annotated Server birth, then `commonPrefix`, then `resumeAt` on the merged deployment state. `item2_prefix`: item 2's prephase at the drawn family (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealParams.preOpaquePool`) for the compiled program is the same birth, then `commonPrefix`, then the rest of item 2's opaque scan (`opaqueRest`, `opaqueScan_split`) under the stopped router. `CutRel` and `cut_rel`: on every outcome of `commonPrefix`, the source-side invariant at the pool (`cut_owned`), CE-3's merge invariant of the local copies and the erased source state (`cut_good`), and the KEM pool a prefix of the challenger's pool with at most the budget's creations (`cut_prefix`). Two separate facts on the same outcomes (review fix pass): `cut_residual` (through the generic `scan_residual` and `sourcePool_keys`): for a source budget of the whole compiled program, the KEM key pairs created so far are at most the budget's creations, and at a pending compiled cut every reply's compiled continuation stays within the budget with only the remaining creations (the budget's creations minus the key pairs created so far); `cut_indexed` and `cut_entry_key` (through `PoolIndexed`, `indexed_entry`, `set_kemIndex`, `source_kemIndex`, `query_indexed`): every owner entry's KEM index is its position, so the active KEM key pair of the owner at position `j` is entry `j` of the challenger's pool. |

## CE-11b unit U5: decisions and deviations

- Route P-a (design-v2 section 8.3, decided in the spike) is built with its first and third layers. The second layer (CE-3's game wrappers `compile_game` and `compile_full_game` re-stated for the pool) is not built: both sides are stated after the same Server construction, item 2's annotated birth `PqxdhRegistrationJointServerBirth.source`, which `ideal_prefix` relates to the deployment game's constructor once (with CE-3's `construct_fields` for the key ID and the empty peer map).
- The glue to item 2's prephase through `exposeAt_scan`, `scan_opaque` and `preRaw_opaque` (design-v2 section 8.3, step 3) is not needed: item 2's side is stated on `PqxdhDeploymentIdealParams.preOpaquePool`, already the opaque prephase at a given family, which `slotGame_pool` (increment 1) connects to item 2's two-slot world.
- The router step is proved directly against item 2's stopped selected-first router, as an exact equality with the router state of the final pool (`routerOf`), instead of through `PqxdhDynamicKemJointSource.program_source`, which relates the normal router to the reserved services only after a projection.
- Both prefixes are images of one computation (`commonPrefix`, the annotated pool source execution of the compiled CE scan), not two prefixes related by a coupling. Unit U6 can then compare the two continuations pointwise, for each outcome of the common prefix, and conclude by bind congruence.
- The relation at the cut is placed at the scan output, before the selected accept's verification, not after the selected accept's steps before encapsulation (design-v2 section 8.3, step 4). The verification and those steps are in `opaqueRest` on item 2's side and in `acceptExposed` on the CE side; U6's accept step compares them (on item 2's side through the joint identity service at the Server's slot, on the CE side natively with the Server's seed; `cut_owned` gives the slot).
- The LQ-F ghosts (the Server ephemeral log and the finish snapshot) are written by the ideal registration but never read, which `idealRun_unghost` proves for every program; this lets the CE state at the cut be a function of the deployment state (`cutState`), so the CE side is an image of the common prefix too.
- The source budget of item 2's scan of the compiled program (no identity or KEM field read, at most as many KEM creations as the pool holds) is a premise of `item2_prefix`, `stopped_scan_source`, `cut_prefix`, `cut_rel` and `cut_entry_key`. Its instantiation from the CE budget (`compile_full_certified`, `compile_full_unread`, `PqxdhRegistrationJointAcceptCut.scan_bound`, `transported_room`) is left to the assembly of `ideal_compile` in U6. `cut_residual` takes the source budget of the whole compiled program instead, because the continuation after the cut is not a query of the scan; it implies the scan's budget (`scan_bound`), and CE-9's `compile_full_certified` gives it directly.
- The remaining creation budget (`cut_residual`) and the owner positions (`cut_indexed`, `cut_entry_key`) are separate lemmas on the outcomes of `commonPrefix`, not new fields of `CutRel`, so that `cut_rel` keeps its premises (review fix pass).
- The third layer has its own module `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPoolSource` (registered with the other two); design-v2 section 11 lists U5's modules as `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCut` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPool`.
- `slotImpl_lift` and `slotCore_lift` come from the scratch lemmas `slotImpl_coins` and `slotCore_onSlots` of increment 2 (`ce11b/inc2/spike/U2Spike.lean`), published here in U5's namespace because U5 runs in parallel with U2. U2 published its own versions in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealErase` on `codex/deployment-ce11b` (since `2f46f1a5`): `PqxdhDeploymentIdealErase.slotImpl_coins` has the statement of `slotImpl_lift`, and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealErase.slotCore_onSlots` overlaps `slotCore_lift` (it takes a slot program of the form `onSlots step` instead of a coins-only run). Once both units are integrated, U2's two names are the canonical ones for later units; the U5 copies are kept (theorems are never deleted) only because the two tracks ran in parallel.
- Publication: three Lean commits (one per module, each with its root import, inventory row and check-inventory block) and one documentation commit, on branch `codex/deployment-ce11b-cut` over central `0b70190e`; the review fix pass adds one Lean commit (`9020dd1f`) and one documentation commit, and one documentation commit records the confirming review; not integrated at that commit.

## CE-11b unit U5: names for consumers

- For U6 (post-challenge simulation and `ideal_compile`): `commonPrefix`, `ideal_prefix`, `item2_prefix`, `resumeAt`, `cutState`, `opaqueRest`, `opaqueScan_split`, `compileCut`, `CutRel`, `cut_rel`, `cut_owned`, `cut_good`, `cut_prefix`, `cut_residual`, `cut_indexed`, `cut_entry_key`, `PoolIndexed`, `indexed_entry`, `sourceInit`, `pool_scan_source`, `stopped_scan_source`, `ideal_cut`.
- Generic tools: `scanCE`, `finishCE`, `scanCE_finish`, `run_scanCE`, `compile_scan`, `scan_residual`, `preStep`, `preScan`, `PreCut`, `withAccepts`, `idealRun_unghost`, `unghost`, `run_reset`, `slotCore_lift`, `slotCore_unghost` (for `slotCore_lift` and `slotImpl_lift`, prefer U2's `PqxdhDeploymentIdealErase` names once integrated; decisions above).
- Pool layers: `deploymentPool`, `revealPool`, `compiledPool`, `compile_native_pool`, `compile_simulation_pool`, `good_step_pool` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPool`); `sourcePool`, `programPool_native`, `reservedAt`, `executeWith`, `program_reserved`, `PoolOwned`, `PoolPrefix`, `routerOf`, `stopped_program`, `reserved_program_prefix`, `kemCommands_reserved`, `kemCommands_stopped` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealPoolSource`); `sourcePool_keys`, `source_kemIndex`, `query_indexed` (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealCut`).

## CE-11b unit U5: contracts used

The approved codec contracts `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`, only through CE-3's compile lemma and invariant (`compile_native_pool`, `cut_good`, `pool_scan_source`, `ideal_prefix`). No new assumption or contract. The other premises are hypotheses on the program and the state (the source budget of item 2's scan, for `cut_residual` the source budget of the whole compiled program, the Server's identity slot, the empty KEM pool, the constructed Server's key ID and empty peer map, all discharged by the construction except the budgets, which CE-9's caps discharge from the CE budget).

## CE-11b unit U5: open points

- Unit U6: the selected accept (CE `acceptExposed` against item 2's `opaqueRest` followed by `opaquePost` in the skeleton `postSkel`), the post-challenge relation and the flattening of item 2's post-challenge stack, the per-command simulation from `CutRel`, the decision step and `ideal_compile`, with the instantiation of the source budgets from the CE budget (above). After the cut, item 2's post router answers a creation beyond its reservation with a stop, while the CE side draws a fresh key; U6 shows that no post-challenge run stops from `cut_residual` (the keys created before the cut plus every reply's compiled continuation's creations are at most the budget's creations, which fit the pool; the router state at the cut counts the keys created, `routerOf`), in the form of `PqxdhDynamicKemStopping.normal_capacity`'s capacity premise. That owner `j` holds pool entry `j` is `cut_entry_key` (review fix pass; the owner-entry index open point is closed).
- Integration of this unit (PROTOCOL section 6): the committed plan snapshot is refreshed through section 55 by the commit that records the confirming review; resolve the hot spots shared with U2's branch `codex/deployment-ce11b` (this guide's sections, which both units insert after "CE-11b increment 2: confirming review", the root imports, the inventory rows and the check-inventory blocks) with `inv recount`, then `inv rehash`, then a focused `check-inventory`; keep both copies of the slot lemmas (decisions above) and consider consolidating later uses on U2's names without deleting either theorem.
- U6 needs U4's `ideal_keymatch` for the decision step (or `ideal_keymatch` moves into U6, design-v2 section 11).
- Units U2 (in progress on `codex/deployment-ce11b`), U3, U4, U7, U8 and the deferred unit 11b-L as in the increment 2 open points.

## CE-11b unit U5: review fix pass

An independent review (semantic and hygiene) of `86485fe1` found no error in the Lean statements or proofs. The reviewers rechecked each module once without `-o` (exit 0, empty logs; 11, 27 and 38 theorems with as many exact guards allowing only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`), rebuilt the three modules and the root import into their own mirror of central's published first prefix (empty logs, oleans byte-identical to the unit's), reran the focused `check-inventory` on `86485fe1`, and checked in scratch that `item2_prefix` instantiates at CE-9's compiled program with every premise discharged from the CE budget, that every constructed Server of item 2's annotated birth meets the state premises of `cut_rel`, and that `ideal_prefix` composes with `CE.idealGame g` under the challenger's draws. Their findings and the fixes:

- Minor: `CutRel` gave the continuation after the cut no remaining creation budget, which U6 needs. After the cut, item 2's post router (`PqxdhDynamicKemRouter.router` through `PqxdhDynamicKemJointRouter.post`) answers a creation beyond its reservation with a stop, while the CE side's `createPool` draws a fresh key there, so the pointwise comparison of the two continuations needs the keys created before the cut plus the compiled continuation's creations to fit the pool. `CutRel`'s bound covers only the scan, whose pending continuation is a value and not a query; item 2's `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemCutBounds.residual` gives the continuation the full cap; `PqxdhDynamicKemStopping.normal_capacity` needs the created keys plus the creation bound to be at most the number of pairs. Fixed by a separate lemma, `cut_residual` (below), proved now instead of in U6; the U6 plan (with the CE-11b design notes) and the open points above use it.
- Minor: the docstring of `cut_prefix` said that owner `j` holds pool entry `j`, which neither `cut_prefix` nor `CutRel` states: with `PoolOwned` an owner's active key is the KEM key pair at the owner's KEM index, but nothing fixed that index. The docstring now says that the KEM key pair at KEM index `j` is pool entry `j`, and the owner position is proved: `cut_indexed` (every owner entry's KEM index is its position) and `cut_entry_key` (the active KEM key pair of the owner at position `j` is entry `j` of the challenger's pool). This closes the owner-entry index open point.
- Nit: `PqxdhRegistrationJointServerSchedule.Owned` was called CE-3's source ownership facts; it is item 2's source-schedule representation invariant, and no CE-3 module uses it. Fixed in the module docstring and the `PoolOwned` docstring of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPoolSource`, the docstring of `cut_owned` and the `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPoolSource` row of "CE-11b unit U5: what is proved".
- Nit (reported twice): the analysis said in one paragraph that the compiled program never reads identity or KEM fields and in another that this is a hypothesis. The first paragraph now says that the deployment adversary's budget (CE-9's caps) gives it and that it is a hypothesis of this unit, discharged when the second compile lemma is assembled.
- Nit: `slotImpl_lift` has the statement of U2's `PqxdhDeploymentIdealErase.slotImpl_coins`, and `slotCore_lift` overlaps `PqxdhDeploymentIdealErase.slotCore_onSlots`. The decisions above now say that U2's names are canonical for later units and why the U5 copies stay.
- Nit: the receipt pinned the CE-11b design at its hash before the design's section 19 was added, without naming the copy that has that hash. The regenerated receipt names that copy and records the design after this fix pass with its own dated copy and its sections.
- Nit: the coordination registry described `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPool` with game wrappers and source initialization layers that it does not contain, and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCut` with a `prefix_compile` that does not exist; both descriptions are refreshed.
- Nit: the integration follow-ups (plan snapshot, the hot spots shared with U2, the duplicated slot lemmas) are listed in the open points above.
- Three records of the reviewers' checks: the Lean of the three modules is correct (the prefix is an exact equality; `scanCE` at the target's ordinal matches item 2's accept scan; ignoring the LQ-F ghosts is sound for decisions that read only the result and the deployment state); the focused `check-inventory` recorded on `a7c3a8e7` also passes on `86485fe1`; the guard, forbidden-token, rebuild, mirror and anchor checks pass. No change needed.

Changed statements. No existing statement or definition changed: `CutRel`, `cut_rel` and every other theorem of the three modules keep their statements and proofs; only docstrings changed in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPoolSource` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCut`. The two facts are separate lemmas rather than new fields of `CutRel`, because a new field would add the premise of `cut_residual` to `cut_rel`. `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCut` gains one definition and nine theorems (38 to 47, each with an exact guard):

- `scan_residual`: for item 2's accept scan of a program with at most `cap` requests of a class, run by any handler whose state carries a counter that grows by at most one on a request of the class and not on any other request, every outcome has counter growth at most `cap`, and at a pending cut every reply's continuation has at most `cap` minus that growth requests of the class.
- `sourcePool_keys`: every request of the annotated pool source schedule adds one KEM key pair if it is a creation (source kind `.inl 1`) and keeps the KEM key pairs otherwise.
- `cut_residual`: for an empty starting KEM pool and a source budget of the whole compiled program (`∀ source, (compile work).IsQueryBoundP (sourceKind · = source) (budget source)`), every outcome `reached` of `commonPrefix` has `reached.2.pool.2.keys.length ≤ budget (.inl 1)`, and if `compileCut reached.1 = .pending raw next`, then for every reply and every source kind, `next reply` is bounded by `Function.update budget (.inl 1) (budget (.inl 1) - reached.2.pool.2.keys.length)` at that kind. Its budget premise implies the scan budget of `cut_rel` (`PqxdhRegistrationJointAcceptCut.scan_bound`) and is what CE-9's `compile_full_certified` gives from the CE budget.
- `PoolIndexed` (definition: `state.owners.map Entry.kemIndex = List.range state.owners.length`) with `indexed_entry` (the entry at position `j` has KEM index `j`), `set_kemIndex` and `source_kemIndex` (requests other than a creation keep every KEM index), `query_indexed` (every request of the annotated pool source schedule keeps `PoolIndexed` from a state with `PoolOwned`: a creation appends an owner whose KEM index is the KEM pool's size, which is the owner count), `cut_indexed` (`PoolIndexed` on every outcome of `commonPrefix`, from the Server's seed at its identity slot and an empty KEM pool) and `cut_entry_key` (with the premises of `cut_prefix`: for the owner entry at position `j` whose active KEM key pair is `keys`, `(p.pool t)[j]? = some keys`).

Checks. No full `lake build`, `make verify`, mutation testing or `nix-collect-garbage` was run.

## CE-11b unit U5: confirming review

A confirming review of the fix pass (`9020dd1f` and `58a57965`) at `58a57965` rechecked `PqxdhDeploymentIdealPoolSource` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealCut` with Lean 4.34 (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) through the coordination wrapper without `-o` against the fix pass's prefix (exit 0, empty logs), counted 11, 27 and 47 theorems in the three modules with as many exact guards allowing only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound` and no forbidden token, and found that the diff of the three modules from `86485fe1` with the docstrings removed only adds lines: no existing statement, proof or definition changed, and `CutRel` and `cut_rel` are unchanged. It compared the receipt's hashes with the branch head, the inventory rows and the dated copies of the plan and the design, and checked this guide, the analysis, the scratch notes and the registry descriptions against the findings. A scratch probe (`ce11b/u5/confirm/probe/ConfirmU5Probe.lean`, exit 0, empty log) proved three consequences of the new lemmas: at CE-9's compiled program, on every outcome of `commonPrefix` and for every reply of the suspended accept, the keys created so far plus the continuation's creation bound fit the challenger's pool (the capacity premise of `PqxdhDynamicKemStopping.normal_capacity`, with the counter of `routerOf`); `cut_entry_key` holds at CE-9's compiled program after item 2's annotated birth with every premise discharged from the CE budget and the birth; and `PoolIndexed` fails for a state whose first owner entry has KEM index 1, so it is not trivial. It found every finding of the review of `86485fe1` resolved and raised no new finding.

This commit records the review in this section and the status paragraph, the Review paragraph of the U5 section of the analysis, the receipt (`review.confirming_review` and the plan record) and the committed plan snapshot, refreshed from the working plan through section 55 (sections 52 to 55, with the confirming-review paragraph at the end of section 55). No Lean file, inventory row or check changes; the integration's own checks run on the topic tip after this commit.

## CE-11b unit U2: what is proved

Unit U2 adds four modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` with 186 guarded theorems; no existing proof module changed (the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains four imports). Every theorem depends at most on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, recorded by an exact `#guard_msgs` over `#print axioms`. All statements are pointwise in the target `t` and the challenger's draws `p`, except `erase_idealGame`, which is stated for `CE.idealGame g` after the draws.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealErase` | 77 | C7 for the ideal registration (design-v2 section 4). `idealPair t p svc` is CE-11a's `FinishPair` at the ideal core `idealCore t p svc`, over any slot service, whose only point is owner k's challenge finish (`idealPair_reg`: its handler is `idealCore`; `idealPair_erased`: its erased handler is `idealCoreErased`). `erase_ideal` is `PqxdhDeploymentFinishErasure.erase_game_ctx` at this pair over item 2's table service: the common deployment experiment with `idealReg t p` and with `idealCoreErased t p tableService` have the same law of the Server's public data, the adversary's output, the ledger and the final state with the CTX cache emptied (`fullX`). `erase_idealGame` is the same for `CE.idealGame g` and `CE.idealGame° g` (`idealGameErased`). The proof needs: the CTX obliviousness of the real commands on every backend (`realRegAt_commutes_ctx`, central's `realReg_commutes_ctx` transported through `realRegAt_forget`) and of create, bundle and HMAC of the ideal core (`idealCore_real_commutes_ctx`); a calculus of slot steps on pairs of the program state and the allocator and service state (`onPair` with its bind, pure, map, focus, dispatch and factor lemmas; `SlotFrame` for slot steps that neither read nor write the CTX cache), which `slotCore_pair`, `slotCore_commutes` and `slotCore_factor` carry through `slotCore`; the selected accept as a frame of the CTX cache (`acceptExposed_frame`, `acceptStep_commutes`); the marked build's commutation (`buildStep_commutes`, from CE-11a's `buildFactor_commutes_ctx`); and owner k's challenge finish restructured (`ownerRun`) with its pointed form (`finishStepPointed`), an exact factorisation (`finishStep_factor`, from `finishFactor_pointed`), the erasure of the report (`finishStep_erase`) and the commutation of the erased finish (`finishStep_erased_commutes`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeys` | 10 | Design-v2 section 5.2 steps 1 and 2. `represent_ideal` is CE-11a's `represent_split` at the C7-erased core (premise `idealCoreErased_natural`): the game with item 2's persistent lazy table, read through the table's cache, and the game with CE-11a's split service, read through the joined table, have the same law. A generic seed relocation: for slot programs that keep the extended state's seed table (`idealProg_seedFrame`), the game with the seeds in the service state, read with the seeds moved into `XState.seeds` (`moveSeeds`), is exactly the game with `slotCoreX`, whose service keeps its material in the backend and its seeds in `XState.seeds` (`slotCore_moveSeeds`, `game_moveSeeds`). `seed_move` is its instance; its right side is the first-touch split form `idealSplitX t p` on `SplitBk`. `ideal_to_splitX` composes `erase_ideal`, `represent_ideal` and `seed_move`, read through `splitView` (the native deployment state with the CTX cache emptied, the allocator, the ideal backend, the LQ-F snapshot and the ephemeral log). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKernel` | 44 | Design-v2 section 5.2 step 3. `split_kernel`: `idealSplitX t p` is, command by command and exactly, the eager split form `idealSplitEager t p`, whose service `eagerDummy` draws the material and the seed of a slot at its first touch but hands the slot program the dummy seed, and whose marked build and owner k's challenge finish are followed by a seed fix that reads the slot's seed from the seed table without drawing (`xPeek`) and puts the World's Server kernel of that seed on the published peer (`buildApply`) or the World's Beacon kernel on the established owner (`stepApply`). The selected accept ignores the value of its hidden read (`acceptStep_ignores`, through the closure lemmas of `Ignores` and `IgnoresS`), so it runs alike over two services with the same state effects (`slotCoreX_same`, `eagerDummy_same`). The native kernel identities are `buildHidden_seed` (from CE-11a's `build_split_raw`), `nativeCtx_erased_seed` (from `erased_split`) and `finish_withKernel`; on pairs they become `buildStepC_seed` and `finishStepC_seed` (`buildStepC` and `finishStepC` are the build and finish steps that also report the coordinates of their hidden read; `buildStepC_fst`, `finishStepC_fst`), and `slotRunX_seed` carries them to the extended state. `ideal_to_splitEager` composes `split_kernel` with `ideal_to_splitX`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentIdealDefer` | 55 | Design-v2 section 5.2 step 4 and the composition. The final split form `idealSplit t p` runs the C7-erased slot program over `lazyDummy` (the material of a slot drawn at its first touch, joined with the dummy seed; the seed never drawn there), and follows the marked build by `serverInstall` and owner k's challenge finish by `beaconInstall`: a hidden install (`xInstall`) that, when the build published a peer (`publishedKey`) or the owner is established (`establishedAt`), reads the seed of the slot of the step's hidden-read coordinates lazily (drawing it if absent) and puts the World's kernel of it on the peer or the owner. `split_game`: the games with `idealSplitEager t p` and with `idealSplit t p` have the same law once the seed table is emptied (`seedFree`). `ideal_to_split`: the common deployment experiment with `idealReg t p` from `idealInit` and with `idealSplit t p` from `splitInit` have the same law of the Server's public data, the adversary's output, the ledger and `splitView` of the final state (`splitView_seedFree`: the split view does not see the seed table). The engine: CE-11a's erasure of a discarded lazy query generalized to lists of points under a state invariant (`completeList`, `discard_erase_list`), and the corresponding seed deferral for registration handlers (`PointedSeeds`, `defer_seeds`, with `fixed_seeds` for the fixed steps); eager against lazy slot reads (`eager_lazy`, `slotRunX_eager_lazy`: from a well-formed state, a slot program over `eagerDummy` is, in law, the program over `lazyDummy` followed by the seed draws of the slots it allocated, `slotsFrom`); the install lemmas (`installStep_commutes`; `xInstall_peek`: from a well-formed state the eager form's seed fix is the install, because the fix is void when the gate is closed, `buildApply_void`, `stepApply_void`); the invariant `InvX` (the allocator's counter is at most two, every allocated slot lies below it, every slot below it has its seed), which holds initially (`initial_invX`) and is kept by the fixed steps (`fixed_invX`); and the deferral pair `splitSeeds` (eager form, pointed final form `splitPointed`) with its three obligations and `splitSeeds_erased`. |

## CE-11b unit U2: decisions and deviations

- Four modules instead of design-v2's single `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeys` (each registered before it was created): `PqxdhDeploymentIdealErase` (C7), `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeys` (represent step, seed relocation, `ideal_to_splitX`), `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKernel` (kernel identities) and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealDefer` (deferral, final split form, `ideal_to_split`). Each was published as a whole module with its own commit.
- C7 follows the designed route (`erase_game_ctx` at a pair whose only point is owner k's challenge finish). Instead of lifting `finishFactor_pointed` through `factor_prefix` and `factor_suffix` at the level of the game state, the pointed finish is built as a slot program on pairs and lifted through `slotCore` by `slotCore_factor`; to expose the hidden continuation, owner k's grouped finish is restructured (`ownerRun`: preparation as coins, at most one grouped finish request, then the source commit).
- Kernel identities (design-v2 section 5.2 step 3). The design rewrote the hidden build and finish with seed reads conditional on the install, valid under an invariant ("a marked session's slot has its seed"). The unit adds an intermediate eager split form instead, whose service draws the seed at the first touch but hands the dummy seed to the slot program, and whose installs read the seed without drawing. `split_kernel` is then an exact equality per command, with no invariant, and the deferral below handles the move of the draws.
- Deferral (step 4). The design's two-point `PointedReg` with CE-11a's single-point `defer` is replaced by a list-point generalization under a state invariant (`discard_erase_list`, `defer_seeds`, invariant `InvX`). In the eager form every first touch draws a seed, a command can allocate up to two slots, and "eager equals lazy followed by the new slots' seed draws" holds only from states in which every allocated slot already has its seed; the pointed form therefore reports the list of slots a command allocated. The installs of the final form are gated lazy seed queries; from a well-formed state they equal the eager form's seed fixes.
- The final split form's shape, which U3's environment step will follow: lazy material at the first touch, seeds only at the two hidden installs (after the marked build when it published a peer, after owner k's challenge finish when owner k is established), seeds in `XState.seeds` (where `PqxdhDeploymentEnvironmentJoin.merge` puts the World's seeds).
- `seed_at_install` (F4) moves to U3. The statement (on every outcome of the final split game, a slot's seed is present exactly when the slot carries a hidden install ghost) does not follow from the deferral alone. A step that sets a ghost overwrites the earlier one, so a second establishing challenge finish of owner k at other coordinates would leave a seed without a ghost; excluding it needs that a finish from `initSent` ends terminal and that owner k never returns to `initSent` (U3's I4), and tying the Server ghost's coordinates to one slot needs that the selected accept runs once and its session mark never changes afterwards (I5). These run facts are U3's per-command invariant work, so `seed_at_install` is proved most directly as one more conjunct of U3's invariant, using that the seeds are drawn only by the two installs (`lazyDummy` never draws a seed; `fixed_seeds` for the fixed steps). No statement is weakened.
- Design-v2 section 5.2 asks to re-check item 2 plan section 20 note 3 (a)-(c) before these steps. (a) holds: the first-touch classes `ceFirstTouch` (`PqxdhDeploymentEnvironment`) are the accept (class 3) and the finish (class 5), and in `idealSplit` the marked build re-reads, through the allocator, the coordinates that the selected accept read and marked, so it is an allocator hit and no first touch (a reading of the steps; no U2 statement depends on it). (b) holds: the selected accept's pointed form is the plain slot step over `lazyDummy`, which commutes with seed completions (`plain_oblivious`, `slotRunX_lazy_commutes`), because the marks carry coordinates, not seed bytes. (c), the count of installs at the build (`hidden_server_once`, with CE-11a's `buildSplit_env_cost`), is U3's.
- `ideal_to_split` is stated up to `splitView`, which drops the service state (item 2's table on one side, the material cache on the other) and the seed table; U3's `c1_ideal` composes it with `join_param`, whose game side keeps the whole final state, by reading both through this view.
- Central moved from `0b70190e` to `14f51c34` (the record-lift CTX track) during the unit. It touches none of U2's dependencies and is not merged into the topic; the integration merges it (hot spots: root imports, inventory rows, check-inventory blocks, analysis sections).

## CE-11b unit U2: names for consumers

- For U3 (environment and C1): `idealSplit`, `serverInstall`, `beaconInstall`, `xInstall`, `lazyDummy`, `slotRunX`, `slotCoreX`, `SplitBk`, `splitInit`, `splitView`, `ideal_to_split`, `split_game`, `fixed_seeds`, `InvX`, `slotsFrom`; the kernel helpers `buildApply`, `stepApply`, `buildStepC`, `finishStepC`, `peerFix`, `setOwnerKernel`; `publishedKey` and `establishedAt` from U1.
- For later units comparing with the C7-erased ideal game: `erase_ideal`, `erase_idealGame`, `idealGameErased`, `idealPair`.
- Generic tools: `onPair`, `SlotFrame`, `slotCore_pair`, `slotCore_commutes`, `slotCore_factor` (slot programs on pairs); `Ignores`, `IgnoresS` (slot clients that ignore the values they read); `game_moveSeeds` (seed relocation); `discard_erase_list`, `PointedSeeds`, `defer_seeds` (list-point deferral under an invariant); `eager_lazy`.

## CE-11b unit U2: contracts used

None. The modules use CE-11a's erasure engine, finish erasure, represent step, split service and hidden-entry kernel identities, central's `realReg_commutes_ctx` and `buildFactor_commutes_ctx`, and U1's definitions; no codec law, and no new assumption or contract.

## CE-11b unit U2: open points

- U3 (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnv`): the environment step as the capability form of `idealSplit`, `IdealInv` with `seed_at_install` as a conjunct, the per-command splits, `c1_ideal` (`ideal_to_split` under `drawParams` composed with `join_param`), the hash-cache and reveal maps of `join_draw`, the frames, `c4_ideal`, `hidden_server_once`, `hidden_beacon_once` and `env_admitted`. Not started. `ideal_to_split` reads both games through `splitView`, which empties the CTX cache and drops the seed table and the material cache, so a statement about the final CTX cache (`c3_ideal`, C3) or about the seed table (`seed_at_install`) is a statement about the final split game (`gameWith … splitInit (idealSplit t p)`, as in design-v2 section 6.3) and is not carried to `CE.idealGame g` by `ideal_to_split`; the observed projection of `c1_ideal` and the reveal inputs (owners, snapshot, log) factor through `splitView`.
- The two-coordinate bound (at most two coordinates read in a run) is still U3's I5; U2 needs it nowhere (`InvX` bounds the allocator's counter by the slot count, which holds for every run).
- Units U4, U6, U7, U8 and the deferred unit 11b-L as before. U5 was built on its own track, branch `codex/deployment-ce11b-cut`, and is integrated into central (`db7bda73`, contained in `07551131`); its sections "CE-11b unit U5: ..." precede this unit's sections.
- Integration of this unit: central `07551131` is merged into the topic with a merge commit, keeping both sides of the hot spots whole (root imports, inventory rows, check-inventory blocks, the analysis and this guide, where U5's sections come first and U2's sections follow; the committed plan snapshot was refreshed through section 58 by the commit that records the confirming review, so the merge keeps this branch's version, whose body extends central's). The merge commit also points the U5 references of these sections (the open point above and the re-estimate) at the sections "CE-11b unit U5: ..." and updates the status paragraph's sentence about central.

## CE-11b unit U2: review fix pass

An independent review (semantic and hygiene) of `091b9b48` found no error in any statement or proof of the four modules. The reviewers rechecked each module once without `-o` (exit 0, empty logs; 77, 10, 44 and 55 theorems, 186 in all, each with one exact guard allowing only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`), rebuilt the modules and the root import into their own mirror of central's published first prefix (empty logs, oleans byte-identical to the unit's), reran the focused `check-inventory` on `091b9b48`, compared the receipt with the branch, and checked in a scratch probe (`ce11b/u2/review-semantic/U2ReviewProbe.lean`, exit 0, empty log) that the observed projection and the analysis projection of the final state factor through `splitView`, that `ideal_to_split` under the challenger's draws composes with `join_param` (`regs := idealSplit t ·`, `init := splitInit`) at the observed projection when U3's obligations are taken as hypotheses, also for `CE.idealGame g` itself with its draws, and that the lazy slot steps keep the seed table, so that only the two gated installs draw seeds. Their findings and the fixes:

- Minor: the receipt's `axiom_profile` was keyed by the last segment of each theorem name, so dotted names (`SlotFrame.pure`, `SlotFrame.bind`, ...) lost their prefix, and in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKernel` the profiles of `Ignores.pure` and `IgnoresS.pure` (and of the two `bind` lemmas) shared one key (42 entries for 44 theorems; the colliding profiles were equal, so no wrong value was recorded). The receipt script now keys the profile by the name inside the module's namespace and checks that its keys are exactly the module's theorems; the regenerated receipt has 77, 10, 44 and 55 entries. It also records the focused `check-inventory` run on `091b9b48`.
- Nit: the docstring of `ideal_to_split` and the last sentence of the analysis paragraph "Drawing the seed only when it is needed" could be read as saying that the split view drops the allocator and the ideal bookkeeping. `splitView x = ((fullX x).base, x.backend.2, x.snap, x.log)` keeps both and drops only the CTX cache (emptied), the slot service state and the seed table. The docstring now says "the native state with the CTX cache emptied, together with the allocator and the ideal backend, the LQ-F snapshot and the log", and the analysis says the same in plain words.
- Nit: the module docstring of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeys` described the rest of the chain as designed (kernel identities with seed reads conditional on the hidden installs, `seed_at_install` later in the chain). It now names the modules that follow: `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKernel` (`split_kernel` through the eager form `idealSplitEager`, exact and without an invariant, and `ideal_to_splitEager`) and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealDefer` (`idealSplit`, `split_game`, `ideal_to_split`), and says that `seed_at_install` moved to U3.
- Nit: the docstring of `idealSplit` cited `CE.idealGameSplit° g`, the first design's name for the final split game, which no declaration or maintained document defines; the label is dropped.
- Nit: design-v2 section 5.2's re-check of item 2 plan section 20 note 3 (a)-(c) was not recorded. It is now a bullet of "CE-11b unit U2: decisions and deviations", with (a) and (b) as the reviewers checked them and (c) left to U3.
- Nit (for U3): facts about the final CTX cache or the seed table cannot pass through `splitView`. The U3 open point now says that `c3_ideal` and `seed_at_install` are statements about the final split game and which projections do factor through `splitView`.
- Nit: the coordination registry described `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeys` with C7 and the seed deferral (they are in `PqxdhDeploymentIdealErase` and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealDefer`) and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealErase` as an optional split module; both descriptions are refreshed.
- Nit: the block comment of `PqxdhDeploymentIdealDefer` in `check-inventory.sh` had no final period; it has one now, and `check-inventory.sh` is rehashed.
- Nit: three passages of this guide assumed that U5 is still only on its own branch and that central is at `14f51c34` (the U5 open point, the re-estimate and the status paragraph); central is now `07551131`, which contains U5. The status paragraph and the U5 open point now say so, and the open points list the updates due when central is merged into the topic at the integration (the reviewers' simulated merge shows the expected append conflicts in the five hot-spot files).
