## CE-11b unit U4, remainder: review fix pass

An independent review (semantic and hygiene) of `codex/deployment-ce11b` at `1217aa58` found no wrong statement, proof or hidden assumption. The four modules and the root recompiled from the committed sources (exit 0, empty logs; the hygiene part also from a `git archive` against its own mirror, with oleans byte-identical to the track's); 73 theorems with 73 exact guards within `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`; no forbidden token; footers, pushed head, receipt and inventory rows checked; the focused inventory passed. Probes (not published) showed that `ideal_slots`, `annotatedRun_erase` and `anomaly_le` compose to the bound on the failure of C5 restated that design-v2 section 9.1 needs, and that the anomaly on a prefix implies the transcript guess event; a negative control (`ideal_slots` does not give `IdealSlots` alone) failed as expected. Both parts approved with findings, resolved as follows.

- Major, scope gap (disclosed): `anomaly_le_pk` and the constructed `anomalyGuesser` are not delivered, so CE-12a's Q2(b) reduction cannot use anything yet. Unit U4 is not recorded as complete (status paragraph, open points, plan sections 80 and 82, receipt). `anomaly_le_pk` and its prefix form are this track's next step, in the new module `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyGuesser` (registered, not yet created), with a guesser that never takes `chosen.2` or `p.challenge` as input ("open points").
- Major, scope gap (disclosed): unit U8 not started. It is scheduled as a step of its own after `anomaly_le_pk`, in the new module `PqxdhDeploymentIdealKeyFirst` (registered, not yet created); plan section 14's row stays open until it is proved.
- Minor: no bound covered the exceptional alternative of `ideal_slots_prefix`, the anomaly on a prefix, which is not contained in the anomaly of the final transcript because designation is not monotone. New theorems: `PqxdhDeploymentIdealGuess.anomaly_prefix_guess` (on every outcome of the record world with `envGD`, under both read-backs, `Anomaly … pre` for a prefix `pre` of the final transcript implies that the transcript guess of the final transcript is the drawn challenge ciphertext; the reviewer's probe as a theorem), `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyBound.guess_le_pk_sup` (the transcript guess event on the record world with `envGD` at most `ctGuessPk`, without a read-back premise: the `c1_split` transfer factored out of `anomaly_le_pk_sup`), `anomaly_prefix_le_pk_sup` (the event `∃ pre rest, transcript = pre ++ rest ∧ Anomaly … pre`, that is `BeaconLinked … pre ∧ FinishBefore t pre`, at most `ctGuessPk`) and `anomaly_prefix_le` (at most `ctGuess`). The bound by `ctGuessBy` for this event comes with `anomaly_le_pk`.
- Minor and nit (both parts): central's move to `dd681f34` before this track's first Lean commit was unrecorded, and the track's report said that central had not moved. The status paragraph and the open points now record it, with the trial merge's conflict set and the handwritten Lean count of 1870 after the merge; plan section 80 is corrected; the integration merges `dd681f34` first.
- Nit: two `set_option linter.deprecated false in` lines (on `lawEq_until_stop` and `evalDist_decide_mono`) suppressed nothing. Both are removed and both modules recompile without a warning. The two proofs still go through VCVio's bridges from relational triples and event probabilities to the `𝒟` measure (`evalDist_eq_of_evalSPMF_eq`, `evalSPMF_bind`, `evalSPMF_eq_of_relTriple_eqRel`, `probEvent_eq_eq_probOutput`, `probEvent_map`, `probEvent_mono`). These lemmas are not deprecated themselves, only the definitions under them, and 64 published modules use the same bridge `evalDist_eq_of_evalSPMF_eq` (114 uses); every theorem statement uses only `𝒟`. The optional rewrite with measure-level lemmas was not done.
- Nit (both parts): "contracts used" omitted `written_read`, which takes `Phase2ReadBack` only. Added, together with the new theorems.
- Nit: "reads the challenge ciphertext only at the selected raw accept" (the `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyBound` row of "CE-11b unit U4, remainder: what is proved", plan section 80) was imprecise: owner `k`'s hidden challenge finish reads it again after that accept (`PqxdhDeploymentIdealEnvFinish`, `preparationCE setup codec p.challenge`). What is proved, and what the text now says, is that the final split game does not read it before the selected raw accept, where it is first read.
- Nit: open point (a) cited `realFinish_none` alone for "the first successful finish is the first one in `initSent`". It now cites both facts: a finish outside `initSent` is rejected (`realFinish_none`; `beacon.rs` lines 402-406), and the first finish made in `initSent` establishes or aborts the Beacon (`finish_some`; `beacon.rs` lines 487-490).
- Nit: the U8 route in the open points omitted the preliminary projection from item 2's counted table to VCVio's lazy cache, which only the track notes recorded. It is added with its reason (the table's draw counter); the estimate is rechecked at 1.25-2.25 agent-days.
- Hygiene record without a finding: the receipt's focused inventory records now carry the SHA-256 of each log.

Statements before and after: no existing statement changed or was weakened. `anomaly_le_pk_sup` keeps its statement; its proof now goes through `guess_le_pk_sup`, which is the same transfer factored out. The four new theorems only add statements. The prefix bounds have the same right-hand sides as `anomaly_le_pk_sup` and `anomaly_le`, and their event contains the anomaly of the final transcript (the case with an empty rest), so they are at least as strong as the published bounds. The Lean commit `de2deeaa` changes only `PqxdhDeploymentIdealGuess` (10 theorems, was 9) and `PqxdhDeploymentIdealAnomalyBound` (24, was 21), their inventory blocks and three digest rows; the handwritten Lean count stays 1866. The documentation commit records the fix pass in this section, the status paragraph, the table, the decisions, the names, the contracts, the open points, the re-estimate and the checks of this unit, the analysis's Review paragraph and Limits, and the receipt (`review`). Not re-reviewed; not integrated.

## CE-8: what is proved

CE-8 embeds the common deployment experiment into the existing Server-authentication game, one Beacon at a time, and adds the injectivity of the resulting matching. Sixteen new modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` carry 253 guarded statements: 242 from the first pass, 8 from the review fix pass and 3 from the second fix pass (4 new, 1 removed); no module from before CE-8 changed. With the 526 statements at `e90d54d9`, the deployment modules now carry 779.

| Module | Statements | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthEvent` | 20 | The registration view `Reg`, `regsOf` (transcript) and `regsOfSource` (source14 journal), the summary `summarizeReg` (`summarize_regs`: the transcript summary), `accepted c` (entries strictly before owner `c`'s accepting finish and its raw input), `bundleOfReg`, `keysOfBundle`, `publishedKeys`, `MatchedAt`, `Matched`, `Unmatched`, `Stop`, `untilStop`, `protectedBinding`, `A1At`; the scan, prefix and stop lemmas (`accepted_snoc`, `accepted_prefix`, `unmatched_stop`, `unmatched_untilStop`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthEmbed` | 2 | `freezeImpl`/`freeze` (journal until a stop, then defaults without queries), `stopAt`, `coins`, `forward`, the local Beacon programs `localCreate`, `localBundle`, `localRoot`, `localCallbacks`, the slot `Slot`, the state `Emb`, `inputBinding`, the embedding `embed`, `embedded` and `adversary` (`R_c`); `coins_route` and `localRoot_route`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthStable` | 14 | No source14 operation reads the two analysis histories (`Stable`, `onShared_stable`, `callbacks_program`, `localFinish_route`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthLocal` | 31 | The native handler `base`, the eager handler `eager` with the target's material `Pre` drawn early, the singleton step, the reassembly `multiOf`, the bad reads `Bad`, `Ready`, and the one-step simulation `step_commute` (with `step_create`, `step_bundle`, `step_finish`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthLocalize` | 34 | Stacked handlers (`stack`, `stack_run`), the singleton and eager sides, the run invariant `Inv` and `localize`: on a program with no identity, KEM, prekey or one-time read, the singleton side reassembled is the eager side up to the analysis histories, jointly over result, frozen journal and final state. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthEager` | 10 | `draw` (the target's create and first bundle drawn before the run) and `swap`: from every state with at most `c` owners, the native run and the eager run on the early draw have the same distribution. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthFreeze` | 9 | `journal`, `journal_erase`, `frozen_journal` (the frozen journal is distributed as the full journal cut at its first stopping prefix), `unmatched_frozen`, `stopAt_nil`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthTrace` | 14 | `compiled_regs`: the registration view of the compiled program's source14 journal is the registration view of its transcript. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthOutcome` | 6 | `game_failure`: on an outcome of the Server-authentication game, a successful finish in the public journal and no session with an earlier publication of the accepted decoded record and an accept carrying the target's published keys give the common authentication failure (`finish_some`, `path_grows`, `game_accepted`, `generation_keys`, `agreement_keys`). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthJournal` | 7 | A ServerAuth client's own journal is the public projection of the callback receipts of the same root-audited run (`execution_log`, `audited_log`), its return value is a possible return of the client (`audited_client`), the root-audited outcome is an outcome of the game (`audited_game`), and `audited_failure`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthTrack` | 33 | The invariant `Track` between the registration view of the frozen journal, the embedding state and `R_c`'s own ServerAuth journal, kept by every step (`track_step`, `track_run`); `adversary_unmatched`: on every outcome of `R_c`, the A1 failure of `c` gives a successful ServerAuth finish and no published, key-carrying session in `R_c`'s journal. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthChain` | 11 | The per-seed chain `seed_bound` (`native_frozen`, `frozen_single`, `typed_draw`, `typed_root`, `root_failure`), with `compiled_unread`, `draw_support` and `bundle_published`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuth` | 18 | `reduction`, `serverAuthAdversary`, `serverAuthBound`, `ReductionCaps`, `A1NoFailure`, the pinned matching `PinnedMatchAt` and `PinnedMatch`, `A1NoPinnedMatch`; `a1_le_ordered`, `a1_bound`, `a1_range`, `a1_sum`, `noFailure_a1At`, `pinned_matched`, `noFailure_noPinnedMatch`, `build_entry`, `build_pin`, `matched_pinned`, `noPinnedMatch_noFailure`, `ce_a1`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthBills` | 12 | `Weights`, `stepCost`, `embed_weight`, `freeze_bound`, `reduction_weight`, `sum_stepCost`, the caps `hashCap`, `romCap`, `coinCap`, `totalCap`, `reduction_caps` and `ce_a1_billed`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthInjective` | 20 | Owners keep their identity seeds (`base_identities`), the eager identity draw `seedEager` with `seeds_eager` and `eager_identities`, `Collision`, `owners_collision`, `joint_correct`, the zero-query collision reduction `collider` (`B^coll_t`) with `LowerCollision` and `colliderForgery`, `collider_game`, `lower_wins`, `lower_le_advantage`, `collision_lower`, `collision_bound`, `collider_queries`, `collider_bills`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthInjectiveGame` | 12 | `bundleOfReg_regsOf`, `accept_registrations`, `session_unique`, `keys_identity`, `SharedMatch`, `identities_merge`, `shared_collision`, `compiled_creates`, `injective_bound`, `ce_a1_injective`. |

The event. CE-A1 is stated on the registration view of the final transcript, which keeps every create whose pin parses and every bundle, accept, build and finish with its reply. `accepted c regs` returns the entries strictly before owner `c`'s accepting finish (its first successful finish while the public summary has `c` in InitSent) and that finish's raw input. A session `i` matches `c` (`MatchedAt`) when, before the acceptance, an accept returned `i` on bytes whose raw candidate the source attached verification accepts with exactly the identity key and key tuple read without a signature check from `c`'s first published bundle, and a build of `i` returned a response whose decoded record is the decoded record of the raw input `c` accepted. `A1NoFailure setup codec c outcome` says that a Server was constructed, `c` has no rejected bundle call in Fresh in the whole run (`NoBundleFailureAt`), `c` accepted, `c`'s create before the acceptance carried the protected binding (the constructed Server's identity key as a 32-byte word and the Server key ID), and no session matches `c`. `A1At` asks for no rejected bundle only before the acceptance, which `NoBundleFailureAt` implies (`noFailure_a1At`). The headline event `A1NoPinnedMatch` is `A1NoFailure` with the pinned matching `PinnedMatchAt`, which also requires that the matching build response reads back, with the Phase 2 reader, `c`'s pin as the Server identity (the Server-identity conjunct of CE-5's `Linked`). `A1NoFailure c` implies `A1NoPinnedMatch c` on every outcome (`noFailure_noPinnedMatch`), and on every outcome of the experiment the two coincide (`noPinnedMatch_noFailure`): every successful build response of the experiment is the Phase 2 writer output of fields carrying the constructed Server's identity (`Agree.builds`, kept from the initial state), which reads back as the pin of the protected binding (`build_pin`, with `Phase2ReadBack` and `construct_origin`). The headline matching is thus pinned to the Server identity. The public matching, pinned or not, is weaker than the plan's eleven-field common session, and it does not carry the KEM ciphertext; that link is not expected to be provable at the CE-A1 bound (an informal argument, not a Lean statement) and moves to CE-12 ("CE-8: decisions and deviations").

The reduction `R_c`. `reduction … c input` is the frozen compiled program under `embed … c input`, journaling its own ServerAuth queries. The `c`-th create becomes the ServerAuth target when its pin and key ID are the input's binding, and is simulated locally otherwise; the target's first bundle in Fresh answers the input's published Phase 1 (a failed one marks the slot failed), later target bundles answer nothing, and a target finish is the ServerAuth finish once the target is bundled. Every other create is a local Beacon drawn with the game's coins exactly as the native constructor draws it; its bundles run the native bundle program with its own signing seed, and its finish runs the native owner program with local preparation, decapsulation and DH, the root as one public HMAC on the zero-salt Extract slice followed by real Expand, local record HKDF and AEAD, and BLAKE2b through the public hash. Coins, accepts, builds, public HMACs, BLAKE2b queries and Server kernel reveals are forwarded unchanged, so Server records are the compiled program's own local work on revealed kernels (restriction 5). The program is frozen at the stop of `c` (`c` accepted, or a bundle call of `c` in Fresh was rejected): the frozen journal has the distribution of the full journal cut at the stop (`frozen_journal`), and the A1 failure of `c` is the same on both (`unmatched_frozen`).

The chain for one Beacon (`a1_le_ordered`). For every identity index of the constructed Server and every initialization runtime, `Pr[A1At c | CE] ≤ Pr[decision | orderedGame false (serverAuthAdversary … c)]`. CE-COMPILE turns the experiment into the native run of the compiled program from the constructed Server's initial state; a refused construction has no event, and the generated seed is matched with the seed draw of `generated_order`. For one seed, `seed_bound` moves the A1 failure to the frozen journal (`native_frozen`, through `journal_erase` and `compiled_regs`), draws the target's material before the run (`swap`), localizes the eager run to `R_c` routed into the source14 handler on the singleton that the early draw creates and bundles (`localize`, which needs `compiled_unread`: the compiled program never reads an identity, KEM, prekey or one-time field), identifies that singleton with the typed constructor and first bundle of the original R1 singleton (`typed_draw`) and so with the root-audited run (`typed_root`, from `typed_audit` and `audited_root`), and shows on every root-audited outcome that the A1 failure of `c` is a common authentication failure (`root_failure`: `R_c`'s own journal is the public journal of the outcome, `Track` gives a successful finish and no session with an earlier publication of the accepted decoded record and an accept carrying the target's published keys, and `game_failure` concludes; the generation receipt with `Phase1ReadBack` fixes the published keys). No step was found false or unprovable.

Per-Beacon bound (`a1_bound`) and the sum (`a1_sum`, `ce_a1`). With the premises of `common_authentication_with_resources` and the query caps of `R_c` (`ReductionCaps`), `(𝒟[a1Decision … c <$> CE] {true}).toReal ≤ serverAuthBound … (serverAuthAdversary … c) qCoin Q qH qHash fallback signatureFallback`, which is `GapDH(scanClient R_c qH) + (Q choose 2)·64/2^256 + Σ_{j<Q+1}(recordLoss + scheduleLoss) + jointEUF(reduction R_c)`, together with `ReductionBills` of every constructed reduction. For a budget-respecting adversary CE-A1 occurs only below `qCreate` (`a1_range`), so the exact union gives `(𝒟[decide (∃ c, A1At c) <$> CE] {true}).toReal ≤ Σ_{c<qCreate} serverAuthBound(R_c)`, and `ce_a1` states it for `∃ c, A1NoPinnedMatch c`: on the support, `noPinnedMatch_noFailure` turns `A1NoPinnedMatch c` into `A1NoFailure c`, and `noFailure_a1At` turns that into `A1At c`.

Bills (`reduction_caps`, `ce_a1_billed`). For a budget-respecting adversary, every `R_c` makes at most `qAccept` accepts, `qFinish` finishes, `hashCap = (qHash + S + R + qTest + qFresh) + 69·qFinish` BLAKE2b queries, `romCap = qHmac + 69·qFinish` random-oracle HMACs, `coinCap = (qCoin + qTest + qFreshBytes) + (64 + keygenCost)·qCreate + 32·qBundle + 69·decapsCost·qFinish` coins and `totalCap = (qCoin + qTest + qFreshBytes) + (64 + keygenCost)·qCreate + 32·qBundle + qAccept + qBuild + (1 + 69·(decapsCost + 2))·qFinish + qHmac + (qHash + S + R + qTest + qFresh) + (qServerSend + qServerRaw + qTest + qFresh)` queries in all, where `S` and `R` are the send and receive totals of the transported caps. The step costs are charged per source14 query (`embed_weight`): a local finish is charged its full 69-callback allowance (`native_owner_bound`), each callback one BLAKE2b query, one HMAC and the decapsulation coins. `ce_a1_billed` is `ce_a1` at these caps.

Injectivity (`injective_bound`, `ce_a1_injective`). `SharedMatch c c'` says that two distinct Beacons accepted and one session matches both. At identity index 0, sessions are positional (`session_unique`, from CE-9's `Traced.positional`), so both matches use one accept and one accepted candidate, and by `Agree` and `Phase1ReadBack` each first published bundle carries the identity key of its owner's final identity seed (`keys_identity`); hence the two owners' identity keys are equal (`shared_collision`). The owners' identity seeds are drawn by their creates; drawing them before the run is a distribution equality (`seeds_eager`) and they then form a prefix of the drawn list (`eager_identities`), so the probability of equal identity keys is at most that of a collision among `qCreate + 1` independently drawn seeds (`owners_collision`, with `compiled_creates`). The collision reduction `B^coll_t = collider joint qCreate t` is the zero-query reduction of owner input 17: a joint-use weak-EUF adversary that receives the challenge identity at index `t`, draws the seeds of the other `qCreate` identities itself and, when the challenge's Ed25519 key equals the key of one of its own seeds at a lower index, signs the empty message with that colliding seed itself and returns the signature as its forgery (`colliderForgery`). It wins whenever the identity at index `t` collides with a lower index (`lower_wins`, by signing correctness `joint_correct`, from the source wiring and the attached-signature correctness of the source primitives, and because it never asks the signing oracle, so the message is fresh). Drawing the seed at index `t` first is a distribution equality (`PqxdhJointIdentityFamily.sampleFamily_target_first`), so the probability that index `t` collides with a lower index is at most `Adv^{jointEUF}(B^coll_t)` (`lower_le_advantage`); every collision is a collision of its higher index with a lower one (`collision_lower`), and the union bound over the higher index gives `ε_inj = Σ_{t<qCreate+1} Adv^{jointEUF}(B^coll_t)` (`collision_bound`). `B^coll_t` makes no signing and no static-DH query and at most `32·qCreate` uniform queries (the bytes of its own seeds) on every challenge identity, and its whole joint-use weak-EUF game makes at most `32·(qCreate + 1)` uniform queries (`collider_queries`, `collider_bills`, from `PqxdhJointIdentityFamily.sampleFamily_lift_bound` and `jointGame_uniform_bound`). `ce_a1_injective` combines both by the union bound: at identity index 0, `Pr[(∃ c, A1NoPinnedMatch c) ∨ ∃ c c', SharedMatch c c']` (real form) is at most `Σ_{c<qCreate} serverAuthBound(R_c) at the transported caps + Σ_{t<qCreate+1} Adv^{jointEUF}(B^coll_t)`, with the bills of every `R_c` (`ReductionBills`) and of every `B^coll_t` (`collider_bills`). `SharedMatch` keeps the public matching `MatchedAt`; a pinned match is a match (`pinned_matched`), so its injectivity covers the pinned matching too.

## CE-8: decisions and deviations

- Public matching, a smaller event than planned. The plan states CE-A1 as "no eleven-field common session that published the decoded record earlier". The eleven-field session is not a CE observable, so CE-A1 is stated with a public matching relation: `MatchedAt` (an accepted key-carrying accept and an earlier build with the accepted decoded record) and, in the headlines, `PinnedMatchAt` (the same with the Server identity of the build response). This relation is weaker than the plan's, so the bounded event is smaller. `root_failure` shows one direction only: the public A1 failure implies the ServerAuth common authentication failure on the same run, that is, an eleven-field common session that published the record gives a public match. The converse does not hold, so the bounded event (no public match) is a sub-event of the planned failure (no eleven-field common session). The public matching states no root agreement and nothing about the KEM ciphertext or the ephemeral key of the response that `c` accepted; in particular it does not give section 9's Beacon-side ciphertext link, which plan section 30 moves to CE-12 (next bullets). The first-pass text called this "a restatement, not a weakening" and said that the public failure "is" the ServerAuth failure; both were wrong.
- Pinned matching in the headlines (fix pass). `ce_a1`, `ce_a1_billed` and `ce_a1_injective` bound `A1NoPinnedMatch`, so the public matching of the headlines is pinned to the Server identity; see "CE-8: review fix pass" for why these statements are at least as strong as the first-pass ones.
- KEM-ciphertext link (plan section 30, Claude's decision of 2026-10-07). The ciphertext conjunct of CE-5's `Linked` is not expected to be provable at the CE-A1 bound, by an informal argument that is recorded as a negative control and is not a Lean statement: no term of `serverAuthBound` is a KEM-security advantage, and with a KEM whose decapsulation ignores the ciphertext an adversary that relays session `i`'s build response to `c` with only the ciphertext replaced makes `c` derive the same key and accept, so `i` still pin-matches `c` while no build response carries `c`'s ciphertext. CE-8 therefore integrates with the pinned public matching and without the ciphertext link. The link moves to the Beacon-side symbolic-eligibility corollary of CE-12, which is planned to charge a separate term `ε_ct` next to `ε_A1`. `ε_ct` is planned to cover the event that an established Beacon of owner `k` accepts with a ciphertext different from session `i`'s while its root input equals session `i`'s (ServerAuth's ghost `Partner`) and accept `i` verified `k`'s published key, and is planned to be bounded by a per-pair `(i, k)` IND-CCA reduction (the decapsulation oracle on the Beacon's ciphertext detects the shared-secret collision) plus the ideal-world probability of the same event, bounded statistically through R1 lazy sampling of the uniform key. That event is stated on root inputs, which the public pinned match does not carry, so CE-12 must also connect the public pinned match to ServerAuth's `Partner` ("CE-8: open points"). IND-CCA is an existing assumption; no new assumption or contract is planned. CE-5's `designate` keeps its ciphertext-equality conjunct, so CE-9 is unaffected. Plan section 9's justification ("the eleven fields carry the ciphertext and the PQ key") is wrong and is corrected in section 30: `Session` has no ciphertext field, and the root input carries the KEM shared secret, not the ciphertext.
- `NoBundleFailure_c`: option (b) of section 19. `ce_a1` is stated on `A1NoPinnedMatch`, which like `A1NoFailure` asks for no rejected bundle call of `c` in Fresh in the whole run; the per-index theorems use the weaker condition before the acceptance (`A1At`), which the run-wide one implies.
- Range of the sum. The sum is over `c < qCreate` (`Fin budget.create`), not `qCreate + 1`: `a1_range` shows that CE-A1 occurs only below `qCreate`. The injectivity sum is over `qCreate + 1` identity indices, as the plan states.
- `B^coll_t` is the zero-query reduction of plan section 10 and owner input 17 (second fix pass): it generates the other seeds itself, signs with the colliding seed itself and makes no signing query (`collider_bills`). It is built directly as a joint-use weak-EUF adversary rather than as `PqxdhJointIdentityFamily.reduction` of a family adversary, because a family adversary sees only public keys and could sign only through the family's signing oracle. Each `B^coll_t` wins only on a collision of index `t` with a lower index, so every colliding pair is charged once, at its higher index.
- Identity index. `a1_le_ordered`, `a1_bound`, `a1_sum`, `ce_a1` and `ce_a1_billed` hold for every identity index of the constructed Server. `session_unique`, `injective_bound` and `ce_a1_injective` are stated at index 0, the index of CE-9's trace invariant.
- The R1 Extract random oracle is charged with every local root: local Beacons compute their roots through the ServerAuth HMAC, so `romCap` counts them (the plan's "counted in qH").
- The ServerAuth game is used with `revealSeed = false` and the generated Server seed (`supplied = none`).

## CE-8: names for consumers

- Events: `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthEvent.A1At`, `Unmatched`, `MatchedAt`, `Matched`, `accepted`, `publishedKeys`, `regsOf`; `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuth.A1NoFailure`, `PinnedMatchAt`, `PinnedMatch`, `A1NoPinnedMatch`; `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthInjectiveGame.SharedMatch`.
- Reduction: `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuth.reduction`, `serverAuthAdversary`, `serverAuthBound`, `ReductionCaps`; `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthEmbed.embed`, `adversary`; caps `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthBills.coinCap`, `romCap`, `hashCap`, `totalCap`; `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthInjective.collider` (`B^coll_t`), `colliderForgery`, `collision_bound`, `collider_queries`, `collider_bills`.
- Results: `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuth.a1_le_ordered`, `a1_bound`, `a1_sum`, `ce_a1`, `build_pin`, `matched_pinned`, `noPinnedMatch_noFailure`; `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthBills.reduction_caps`, `ce_a1_billed`; `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthInjectiveGame.injective_bound`, `ce_a1_injective`.
- Reusable tools: `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthFreeze.frozen_journal` (freezing at a journal stop, for any handler), `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthJournal.audited_log`, `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthInjective.seeds_eager` (identity seeds drawn first for any source14 program), `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthBills.simulate_weighted`.
- Use these short names qualified: `reduction`, `adversary`, `embed`, `accepted`, `Matched`, `collision_bound`, `Stop`, `compiled_unread`, `identities_merge`, `Inv`, `forward`, `State`, `Quiet`, `record_pure` and `SourceSpec`. Other namespaces declare the same short names: `reduction` in fifteen other modules, among them `PqxdhDeploymentProvenance`, `PqxdhDeploymentAcceptDetection`, `PqxdhServerAuthJointIdentityEuf` and `PqxdhJointIdentityFamily` (the last two appear in the CE-8 bounds); `adversary` in the KEM game modules, `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerKemHop` and `PqxdhServerAuthPresealControl`; `embed` (`PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthEmbed.embed`) in `PqxdhServerAuthSendFactor`, `PqxdhServerAuthCtxBuildSettlement` and `PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerSource`; `accepted` (`PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthEvent.accepted`) in `LivePublicObservation`, `PqxdhCommonOrdinaryObserver`, `PqxdhCommonSharedContinuation`, `PqxdhInitialEvaluatedCurrent`, `PqxdhInitialLegalObserver`, `PqxdhInitialPendingAccepted`, `PqxdhInitialPrivateCurrent`, `PqxdhInitialRootEventSecrecy`, `PqxdhInitialServiceExperiment`, `PqxdhRegistrationJointAcceptCutTag`, `PqxdhSourceSharedPreparationResources` and `RawCtxSelectedAuthenticationObserver`; `Matched` (`PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthEvent.Matched`) in `PqxdhServerAuthPublicationCaptureHistory` and `PqxdhServerAuthPublicationMarkedHistory`; `collision_bound` (`PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthInjective.collision_bound`) in `PqxdhInitialPreparedRisk` and `PqxdhInitialSourceLaterMixture`; `Stop` in `PqxdhDeploymentAcceptDetection`; `compiled_unread` in `PqxdhDeploymentQuiet` and `identities_merge` in `PqxdhDeploymentPublishedTuple`, both different statements; `Inv` in `PqxdhDeploymentCompileStep`; `forward` in `PqxdhDeploymentCompile`; `State` in `PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy` and `PqxdhDeploymentNative`; `Quiet` in `PqxdhDeploymentQuiet` and `PqxdhServerAuthGapDh`; `record_pure` in `PqxdhDeploymentCompileRecords`; `SourceSpec` in `PqxdhDeploymentRecordCache`. With two such namespaces open, the short name is ambiguous.

## CE-8: contracts used

The premises of `generated_authentication`: `X25519ClassLaw`, `X25519ScalarSpread`, `SourceFullCorrect`, `HasAttachedVerificationAdapter`, `ReadWritable`, `Phase2ReadBack` and `FrameReadBack`, and the KEM base caps `KemBound setup caps`; the bound charges Gap-DH, the R1 Extract random oracle and the BLAKE2b random oracle through `serverGapDhAdvantage`, the accept-collision term, the record losses (`recordLoss`, by `recordLoss_expanded`: three AEAD IND$ advantages, twice the AEAD INT-CTXT advantage and the statistical terms `(2H + 1)/2^128 + 1/2^512`) and the schedule losses (`scheduleLoss`: three HMAC-PRF advantages and two Extract-on-uniform advantages) of every record coordinate, and joint-use weak-EUF. `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack` (approved 2026-10-06) through CE-COMPILE, `Phase1ReadBack` in `generation_keys` and `keys_identity`, and `Phase2ReadBack` in `build_pin`. `ε_inj` uses only joint-use weak-EUF, against key-only adversaries (no signing and no static-DH query): signing correctness follows from the source wiring and the attached-signature correctness that `Setup` already carries. No new assumption: no SUF-CMA, no uniqueness, no distributional property of key generation, and no honest-target premise.

## CE-8: open points

- CE-8r: an unconditional CE-A1 without `NoBundleFailure_c` needs `phase1Total` or a retry-capable ServerAuth target (owner input 15).
- The HMAC, BLAKE2b and coin caps of `R_c` charge every local finish its full 69-callback allowance; per-callback-class counts would tighten `romCap` and `hashCap`.
- Injectivity at other Server identity indices needs CE-9's trace invariant for those indices.
- Section 9's Beacon-side corollary is CE-12's (plan section 30). For an established Beacon `c` pinned to the Server it needs a session `i` with CE-5's `Linked`: `i`'s build response carries `c`'s finish ciphertext and `c`'s pin, and `i`'s accept verifies to `c`'s published PQ key. CE-A1 gives the last two for the matched accept and build entries (`PinnedMatchAt`; `Linked` reads the first accept and build of the session, which needs a first-entry argument there). The ciphertext equality is planned to be charged to `ε_ct` in CE-12 ("CE-8: decisions and deviations"), with its detailed design at the start of CE-12.
- From the public pinned match to ServerAuth's ghost `Partner` (CE-12). Root, associated-data and registration-ID agreement of the matched session are not stated, because the outcome of the experiment keeps no root. The planned `ε_ct` event is stated on root inputs (the Beacon's root input equals session `i`'s), so CE-12 must also connect the public pinned match that CE-A1 bounds to ServerAuth's `Partner`. Two options: a ghost-level version of the CE-A1 chain, in which `root_failure` and `typed_root` carry the root inputs of the root-audited run through the embedding, or an `ε_ct` event stated on the public pinned match whose root-mismatch branch (the matched session's root input differs from the Beacon's) gets its own bound. Neither is designed yet, and the CE-12 estimate in "CE-8: re-estimate" excludes this step.
- The map from `PinnedMatchAt` and `SharedMatch` to the symbolic events (CE-12), and the run-level liveness witness, remain open.

## CE-8: re-estimate

CE-8 was planned at 14-22 agent-days and re-estimated at 12-20 by unit A; it was built in three working sessions, about 3-4 agent-days. The chain reused the singleton R1 theorems (`typed_audit`, `audited_root`, `generated_order`, `common_authentication_with_resources`) unchanged, and the new work was the embedding with its localization, the journal freeze, the tracking invariant and the identity-seed coupling. A review fix pass is estimated at 0.5-1 agent-days. If CE-8 accounted for 12-20 of the 23-41 agent-days that CE-9 left for item 3 proper without CE-9b, CE-10 and CE-12, about 11-22 remain, including the CE-8 fix pass. The fix pass took about half an agent-day and the second fix pass about a quarter. The ciphertext link's `ε_ct` belongs to CE-12 now; as a new reduction of about the size of one CE-9 transfer it adds roughly 3-6 agent-days there, a figure not derived from a design. This estimate excludes the step from the public pinned match to ServerAuth's `Partner` ("CE-8: open points"), which is not designed yet.

## CE-8: review fix pass

The review's findings and what changed, in commit order. Statements changed: `ce_a1`, `ce_a1_billed` and `ce_a1_injective`, each to a statement at least as strong (below). Eight statements are new: `pinned_matched`, `noFailure_noPinnedMatch`, `build_entry`, `build_pin`, `matched_pinned` and `noPinnedMatch_noFailure` in `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuth`, and `collider_queries` and `collider_bills` in `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthInjective`. Every other theorem keeps its statement up to whitespace (whitespace-normalized comparison with `9d9ea4cb`); no theorem was removed or weakened, and no module before CE-8 changed.

- The public matching omits the KEM-ciphertext and root link, so CE-A1 cannot support section 9's Beacon-side corollary, and the documentation called the public form "not a weakening" (semantic, major). Commit "Pin the CE-A1 matching to the Server identity". The documentation is corrected (guide, analysis, receipt and the docstrings of `A1NoFailure` and the main module): the public matching is weaker than the eleven-field session, and only the implication from the ServerAuth event to the public match is proved. The part of the requested extension that holds at the CE-A1 bound is added: the pinned matching `PinnedMatchAt` adds the Server-identity conjunct of `Linked`, and the headlines bound `A1NoPinnedMatch`. Before: `ce_a1` bounded `(𝒟[decide (∃ c, A1NoFailure setup codec c outcome) <$> game]{true}).toReal`; after: `(𝒟[decide (∃ c, A1NoPinnedMatch setup codec c outcome) <$> game]{true}).toReal`, with the same premises and right-hand side, and the same change in `ce_a1_billed` and in the first disjunct of `ce_a1_injective`. The new statements imply the old ones: `A1NoFailure c` implies `A1NoPinnedMatch c` on every outcome (`noFailure_noPinnedMatch`, through `pinned_matched`), so the old event is contained in the new one and its probability is at most the new left-hand side. On every outcome of the experiment the two events coincide (`noPinnedMatch_noFailure`), so the bound covers no new runs; what is gained is that the stated event carries the Server-identity conjunct. Not done: the KEM-ciphertext link and the root agreement. The ciphertext link is not expected to be provable at this bound, by an informal argument that is recorded as a negative control and is not a Lean statement. Every term of `serverAuthBound` is an advantage against Gap-DH, the random oracles, the record and schedule primitives or joint-use weak-EUF, or a statistical term. With a KEM whose decapsulation ignores the ciphertext, an adversary that relays session `i`'s build response to `c` with only the ciphertext field replaced makes `c` derive the same shared secret, root and record key and accept; session `i` still matches `c` with the pin, but no build response carries `c`'s ciphertext, so a ciphertext-extended failure has probability one while the bound consists of the terms above, none of which is a KEM-security advantage. The root agreement cannot even be stated on the event: the outcome keeps no root (the Server's pending token, which holds the root, is cleared at the build, and a Beacon keeps only its kernel), so it would need an instrumented experiment that logs roots and a state correspondence through the embedding. The gap and the ways to close it are under "CE-8: open points".
- No query bill for `B^coll_t`, and the "one signing query" claim was unproved (semantic, minor). Commit "Bill the collision reductions of CE-A1". New: `collider_queries` (the collision finder makes exactly one query, a signing query, for every family of identities) and `collider_bills` (`B^coll_t` draws at most `32·qCreate` uniform bytes, makes at most one signing query and no static-DH query, and its whole joint-use weak-EUF game makes at most `32·(qCreate + 1)` uniform queries). Before: `ce_a1_injective` concluded the bound `∧ ∀ c, ReductionBills … (R_c) …`; after: the bound `∧ (∀ c, ReductionBills … (R_c) …) ∧ ∀ t, [the four bills of B^coll_t]`, so it gains a conjunct. The one-query collision finder of this commit was replaced by the zero-query reduction of owner input 17 in the second fix pass ("CE-8: second fix pass").
- The guide attributed the wrong primitive games to `recordLoss` and none to `scheduleLoss` (semantic, nit). "CE-8: contracts used" now lists them per term from `recordLoss_expanded` and the definition of `scheduleLoss`.
- The names-for-consumers note did not say which short names to qualify (hygiene, nit). It now lists them with the namespaces that declare the same short names.
- The module table listed `coins_route` and `localRoot_route` under `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthStable` (hygiene, nit). They are now in the `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthEmbed` row; the table also has the new names and counts.

The analysis and the receipt describe the same changes.

## CE-8: second fix pass

Plan section 30 (Claude's decision of 2026-10-07) settles the two points the first fix pass left open: the collision reduction of `ε_inj` becomes the zero-query reduction of owner input 17, and the KEM-ciphertext link of section 9's Beacon-side corollary moves to CE-12 with its own term `ε_ct` ("CE-8: decisions and deviations"). The Lean commit "Make the CE-A1 collision reduction zero-query" changes `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthInjective`, `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuthInjectiveGame` and the module docstring of `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuth`; a documentation commit updates this guide, the analysis and the receipt. No module before CE-8 and no other CE-8 module changed.

- Definition `collider`. Before: the family collision finder `collider count publics`, a family adversary that sees only the family's public identities and signs the empty message once through the family's signing oracle; `B^coll_t` was `PqxdhJointIdentityFamily.reduction joint t (collider count)`, which forwards that signing query to the challenger when the signing identity is the target. After: `collider joint count t` is `B^coll_t` itself, a joint-use weak-EUF adversary that draws the other `count` seeds itself and signs with the colliding seed itself (`colliderForgery`).
- `collision_bound`. Before: `Pr[Collision joint pool | sampleSeeds (count + 1)] ≤ Σ_{t : Fin (count + 1)} advantage joint (PqxdhJointIdentityFamily.reduction joint t (collider count))`. After: `Pr[Collision joint pool | sampleSeeds (count + 1)] ≤ Σ_{t : Fin (count + 1)} advantage joint (collider joint count t)`.
- `collider_queries`. Before: `(collider count publics).IsQueryBoundP (familyClass (IsClass kind)) (if kind = .sign then 1 else 0)` for every family of public identities. After: `(collider joint count t jointPublic).IsQueryBoundP (IsClass kind) (if kind = .uniform then 32 * count else 0)` for every challenge identity.
- `collider_bills`. Before: for the family reduction, at most `32·count` uniform queries, at most 1 signing query and no static-DH query on every challenge identity, and at most `32·(count + 1)` uniform queries in its game. After: the same for the new `B^coll_t` with no signing query.
- `injective_bound` and `ce_a1_injective`. Before: `ε_inj = Σ_t Adv(PqxdhJointIdentityFamily.reduction setup.joint t (collider budget.create))`, and the bill conjunct of `ce_a1_injective` allowed 1 signing query. After: `ε_inj = Σ_t Adv(collider setup.joint budget.create t)`, and the bill conjunct states 0 signing queries; premises, the left-hand sides, the `R_c` terms and the other bills are unchanged.

Why the replacement is at least as strong. The right-hand sides name a different adversary, so as Lean propositions neither the old nor the new statements imply the others; they are replaced, as section 30 requires, not extended. As bounds to be used they are at least as strong: `ε_inj` is discharged by a joint-use weak-EUF assumption for adversaries within given bills, and every bill of the new `B^coll_t` is at most the old one's (uniform `32·qCreate`, signing 0 instead of 1, static-DH 0, game `32·(qCreate + 1)`). Any advantage bound `ε` for adversaries with at most one signing query also covers an adversary with none (`IsQueryBoundP.mono`), so every instantiation of the old statement gives the same `ε_inj ≤ (qCreate + 1)·ε` with the new one; the new one also gives it under the key-only form of the assumption (no signing query), which owner input 17 asks for and the old one could not use. Both sums are upper bounds on `Pr[Collision]`; each can include wins on fallback forgeries, and their exact values are not compared.

Statements: four new (`collider_game`, `lower_wins`, `lower_le_advantage`, `collision_lower`), two new definitions (`LowerCollision`, `colliderForgery`), and one removed: `probEvent_le_bind` ("an event that holds on every outcome of every continuation of the support points of another event is at least as likely"), a helper of the old `collision_bound` with no other use, never published. Every other theorem of the sixteen modules has the same statement as at `c4ad86ff` up to whitespace, checked by a whitespace-normalized comparison. The modules now carry 253 statements. The receipt records the exact before and after statements under `second_fix_pass`.

Confirming reviews of `1170bf02`, which covers both fix passes, with a semantic and a hygiene lens approved with four documentation nits. After the docstring change `PqxdhDeploymentServerAuth` was re-checked through `coord.sh lean claude` (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) into the CE-8 prefix with exit 0 and an empty log.

- The deferral of the ciphertext link to CE-12 left out the step from the public pinned match to ServerAuth's ghost `Partner`, and the documentation said that section 9's eligibility does not need root agreement. That remark is removed. "CE-8: decisions and deviations", "CE-8: open points", the analysis, the receipt and the plan note now state that CE-12 must also connect the public pinned match to `Partner`, with the two options (a ghost-level version of the CE-A1 chain, or an `ε_ct` event on the public pinned match whose root-mismatch branch gets its own bound), and "CE-8: re-estimate" says that the CE-12 estimate excludes this step.
- The comparison of the exact old and new advantage sums in "CE-8: second fix pass" was unproved and inexact. It is replaced by the statement that both sums are upper bounds on `Pr[Collision]`, that each can include wins on fallback forgeries, and that their exact values are not compared.
- Informal claims about the counterexample and `ε_ct` were worded as established facts. `ε_ct` and its bound are now described as planned, the counterexample is recorded as an informal argument and negative control that is not a Lean statement, and "stays what it is for a secure KEM" is replaced by "none of which is a KEM-security advantage".
- The list of short names to use qualified left out consumer-facing CE-8 names that other modules also declare. It now includes `embed`, `accepted`, `Matched` and `collision_bound`, each with the modules that declare the same short name, and gives the count for `reduction`.

## CE-9: what is proved

CE-9 joins the common deployment experiment to the KEM theorems of the accept/KEM join, one target at a time, as plan section 9 describes in steps 9.1-9.4. Three new modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` carry 70 guarded statements, 65 from the first pass and 5 from the review fix pass; no module from before CE-9 changed. With the 456 statements of unit A, unit 3 and the later-quantum mode, the deployment modules now carry 526.

| Module | Statements | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentRegistrationTrace` | 29 | The registration trace of any source14 program: the reply type `Logged`, the logging interpreter `logImpl`, `traceFrom`, `acceptsOf`, `Pooled` and the run invariant `Traced`; `traceFrom_erase`, `tag_map`, `tag_traceFrom`, `tag_traced`, `keys_step`, `nativeAccept_session`, `native_sessions`, `bundle_pooled`, `traced_step`, `traced_run`, `traced_source`, `source_map`, `source_support`, and their scan, tag and step lemmas. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentKemTransfer` | 24 | Steps 9.1-9.4: `registrations`, `compiled_trace`, the observation `Observed` with `observeCompiled` and `observeGame`, `target`, `decision`, `happens`, `designated`, `selectedKem`; `designate_keys`, `keymatch_point`, `eligible_actual` (9.1), `kem_transfer` (9.2-9.4), `kem_transfer_bills`, `kem_transfer_billed`, `kem_hop_close`, `kem_transfer_sum`, `transported_total`, and their trace lemmas. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentTargetRange` | 17 | The range of the designation: `classCount`, `eligible`; `ledger_count`, `accepts_count`, `owners_count`, `traced_range_create`, `traced_range`, `range_game_create`, `range_game`, `designateIn_range`, `eligible_designated`, `kem_transfer_eligible`, `designated_create`, `sum_last_le`, `kem_transfer_create`. |

Events and targets. A deployment event `event : Observed X → Bool` reads the adversary's output, the secrecy ledger (transcript, test session with the hidden bit, fresh messages) and the produced frames. A claim `claim : Observed X → Option Handle` names a record handle, and its target is `target crypto codec serverKid budget claim observed`, the budgeted public designation `designateIn` of that handle on the transcript: a pair `g = (i, k)` in `Fin qAccept × Fin (qCreate + 1)` of raw-accept ordinal and KEM creation index. On an outcome of the experiment, `happens event` is the event (false when no Server was constructed) and `designated … claim` the target. The per-target decision `decision … event claim g` is the same event and designation computed from the compiled program's result through `observeCompiled`; it reads only that result, as T4 requires. `selectedKem budget g` is the KEM index of `g` as T4's `Fin (count + 1)` with `count = qCreate`.

Step 9.1 (`eligible_actual`). For every setup, codec, check set, router, runtime initializer, freshness bound, mode, adversary, budget, event, claim, target `g = (i, k)` and runtime state, under `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack` and with no budget premise: the probability that `PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.game setup codec checks route ensureInit 0 bounds mode adversary` ends with `happens event` and `designated … claim = some g` equals the probability, in the annotated initialized execution of `tag (compileFull setup codec route bounds mode adversary) i` from empty pools with a generated Server seed (`supplied = none`), of T4's event `Decided (decision … g) ∧ GoodKem (selectedKem budget g) ∧ KeyMatchFinal setup codec checks (selectedKem budget g)`. This is the equality the plan states. `GoodKem` comes from `PqxdhDeploymentQuiet.approved_goodkem_unbudgeted`. The other two conjuncts need the selected raw accept to be the accept the designation names and its verified PQ key to be the pool key at index `k`; the next paragraphs describe how. The experiment is stated at Server identity index 0, the slot of the Server's seed in the empty identity pool from which T4's execution starts, as in unit 3's published-tuple statements.

The trace. T4's tag records the `i`-th raw accept request of the program, while the designation reads the accept entry of the transcript that returned session handle `i`. `traceFrom program log` runs any source14 program unchanged and logs the reply of every raw accept and every bundle call; forgetting the log gives back the program (`traceFrom_erase`) and tagging commutes with forgetting it (`tag_map`). `tag_traced` shows that the tag of a traced program at ordinal `i` is the `i`-th logged accept, and `compiled_trace` that the log of the traced compiled program is `registrations` of its final transcript, its accept and bundle entries in order. Both are facts of the programs alone, for every reply: the secrecy layer appends exactly one transcript entry per query, and the compiler issues exactly one source accept per accept command, one source bundle per bundle command and none for any other command (`step_trace`, which bounds the other commands with the existing per-step cost lemmas `wrap_step` and `step_bound`).

The bundle-to-pool invariant. On the annotated execution that T4 is stated for, `traced_source` shows that every traced program ends with `Traced log state`: the Server's session counter equals the number of logged raw accepts; a logged accept that returned session `j` is the `j`-th logged accept and its bytes pass the native verification prefix `RawSignatures.verified`; every logged successful bundle of owner `k` is a Phase 1 writer output whose key tuple carries the public key at KEM creation index `k` of the pool (`Pooled`); and the positional KEM indices of `PqxdhDeploymentKemIndex.Positional` and slot ownership hold. The session facts come from the native accept code: an accept advances the counter by one and a successful one returns the counter before the call (`nativeAccept_session`), and every other command keeps the counter (`native_sessions`, from `handler_sessions` and the monotonicity part of `authHandler_step`). The pool fact holds when the bundle is published, because the owner signs its live KEM pair, which ownership places at its KEM creation index and `Positional` places at index `k` (`bundle_pooled`), and it persists because every step keeps the KEM key list as a prefix (`keys_step`). This is the new invariant that unit A identified as missing: `PublishedKey` ties the published key to the native pair only until finish, while `Pooled` ties it to the pool, which keeps the key after finish.

Key match (`keymatch_point`). A designation `(i, k)` on either side names accept bytes of session `i` whose candidate verifies, under the source attached verification, to keys whose PQ key is the one read from owner `k`'s published bundle (`designate_keys`). Through the trace, that accept is the `i`-th logged accept, so the tag records it with reply `some i`, and its bytes pass `RawSignatures.verified`; `PqxdhDeploymentKeyMatch.verified_keys` identifies the verified PQ key with the designation's, and `Pooled` with `Phase1ReadBack` identifies owner `k`'s published key with the pool key at index `k`. This gives `Decided` and `KeyMatchFinal`.

Steps 9.2-9.4 (`kem_transfer`). For a budget-respecting adversary, the probability that the experiment ends with the event and target `g` is at most `𝒟[opaqueBranch setup codec checks ensureInit none (decision … g) (compileFull …) runtime qCreate (selectedKem budget g) i false] {true} + ENNReal.ofReal (Adv^{IND-CCA}(KemRawCertified.adversary setup codec checks ensureInit none (decision … g) (compileFull …) runtime qCreate (selectedKem budget g) i) + correctnessError setup)`. The chain is step 9.1, T4 `actual_le_real` with `budget = transported bounds budget`, `certified = compile_full_certified` and `room = transported_room`, the certified KEM hop `KemRawCertified.real_le_ideal` and T6 `branchRaw_opaque`. The losses are exactly one ordinary one-key ML-KEM IND-CCA advantage of the exact proof-carrying raw-ordinal adversary and one ML-KEM correctness error, both with coefficient one; T5 and T8 are not used, so no EUF term is charged per target. The premises are `FunctionalDecapsulation setup.kem` (the KEM hop's), `Phase1ReadBack`, `Phase2ReadBack` and the budget.

Bills (`kem_transfer_bills`, `kem_transfer_billed`). `KemRawBills.adversary_bound` at the transported caps: before the challenge, `(qInit + 32) + (keygenCost · qCreate + V)` uniform queries, `decapsBase · qFinish` base queries and `qFinish` logical decapsulations; after it, `V`, `decapsBase · qFinish` and `qFinish`; with `V = uniformCap caps qCreate qFinish (OpenUniform.total caps (transported bounds budget)) qAccept`. The premises are the existing cost premises `KemBound setup caps` and `initBound`. `transported_total` gives the native uniform allowance in closed form: `(qCoin + qTest + qFreshBytes) + 32·qCreate + 32·qBundle + (96 + encapsCost)·qAccept + 64·qBuild + 3584·qFinish + 64·qHmac + 64·(qHash + S + R + qTest + qFresh)`; Server kernel reveals and established-kernel reads add nothing. These bills carry the coin and BLAKE2b caps of unit A's plan change, which still awaits the owner's confirmation.

Headline (`kem_transfer_sum`, `kem_transfer_eligible`, `kem_transfer_create`). The probability that the experiment ends with the event and a designated claim is at most the sum, over all `g` in `Fin qAccept × Fin (qCreate + 1)`, of the per-target right side of `kem_transfer`. The first step is the exact split `UniformTargetSelection.split`; no selection factor and no EUF term is charged. `kem_transfer_sum` states it with the budgeted designation, and `kem_transfer_eligible` with the plain designation `designate`, using the range lemma below. The column `k = qCreate` of this domain is provably empty: on every outcome of a budget-respecting adversary no claim is designated with KEM creation index `qCreate` (`designated_create`, from `range_game_create`), so its `qAccept` targets contribute nothing on the left, while on the right each of them still adds its opaque-branch probability, one IND-CCA advantage and one correctness error. `kem_transfer_create` states the headline with plain eligibility over `Fin qAccept × Fin qCreate` instead, reading each `k` in `Fin (qCreate + 1)` through `Fin.castSucc`, and so drops those terms; T4 is still applied with `count = qCreate`, and `kem_transfer_sum` and `kem_transfer_eligible` keep the domain of plan section 3.8, `N = qAccept · (count + 1)`. `kem_hop_close` is the two-sided KEM hop at one target, `|Pr[sourceReal] − Pr[opaqueBranch false]| ≤ Adv^{IND-CCA} + correctnessError` for the per-target decision, which a two-sided T4 (CE-9b) would compose with.

Range of the designation (`range_game_create`, `range_game`). This closes unit A's open point. For a budget-respecting adversary, on every outcome of the experiment, every designation `(i, k)` of any handle has `i < qAccept` and `k < qCreate` (`range_game_create`), and so lies in the target domain `Fin qAccept × Fin (qCreate + 1)` (`range_game`). The session bound combines the positional accepts of `Traced` with a transcript count: the transcript has at most `qAccept` accept entries (`ledger_count`), and these are the logged accepts (`accepts_count`). The owner bound uses that a designated owner was created (`designate_created`) and that the summary's created owners are at most the create entries (`owners_count`). Hence `designateIn` agrees with `designate` (`designateIn_range`) and a claim is designated exactly when it is designated in the target domain (`eligible_designated`). It uses `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack` through CE-COMPILE.

## CE-9: decisions and deviations

- Trace instead of an annotated simulation relation. Unit A expected step 9.1 to need an annotated version of the CE-3 per-step relation or a reply-provenance argument for the compiled ledger. CE-9 uses reply provenance through the logging transformation: the program-level facts (`tag_traced`, `compiled_trace`) need no handler, and the handler-level invariant `Traced` is generic in the program, so it can be reused for `compileLater` (LQ-3) and other compiled programs.
- The equality of step 9.1 is proved as the plan states it; no step was found false or unprovable, and no statement was weakened.
- Events and claims. The event may read the hidden bit and the fresh messages, which secrecy claims need. The designation is the public function of unit A. The claim function is arbitrary, so the bounds hold for every claim; a public selection instantiates it with a public function of the observation, for example the handle of the first admitted test or a handle named in the adversary's output.
- The Server identity index is 0, as in unit 3's published-tuple statements: T4's execution starts from the empty identity pool, and `compile_full_source` gives the experiment the number of seeds of the starting pool. No lemma shows that the probabilities are the same for other indices.
- The per-target KEM adversary is built from the compiled program and the per-target decision, so the headline has one adversary per target, as the exact-sum form of plan section 7 requires. The `N · Adv(B_guess)` form is not provided (owner input 5).
- Both modes are covered; secrecy claims are to be instantiated in mode P, as before.

## CE-9: open points

- CE-9b: a two-sided or equality form of T4 for decisions that imply `accepted ∧ KeyMatchFinal ∧ GoodKem`, needed for left-or-right secrecy; the KEM hop is already two-sided (`kem_hop_close`) and T6 is an equality.
- Step 9.5 (item 2's T7) and CE-11, the un-compiled ideal world: the right side of the transfer is still the opaque branch of the compiled program.
- LQ-3: the same transfer for `compileLater`. `Traced` and the program-level trace lemmas are generic; `compiled_trace` needs an extension for the finish pre-reads, the terminal reads and the post part, which issue no raw accept and no bundle call.
- The CE-12 corollaries to the symbolic eligibility notions (with `ε_A2` and `ε_A1`), the signed-hop form and the run-level liveness witness remain open.
- The coin and BLAKE2b caps in the bills are those of unit A's plan change, which awaits the owner's confirmation.

## CE-9: re-estimate

Unit A re-estimated CE-9 at 5-10 agent-days and unit 3 lowered it to 3-7. CE-9, with the range lemma, was built in one working session: the trace kept every proof either generic or a fact of the program alone, and the existing footprints and cost lemmas covered every native step. On that evidence: LQ-3 0.5-1 agent-days (down from 0.5-1.5), because `Traced` and the program-level trace lemmas carry over and only `compiled_trace` needs extending. CE-9b keeps its 3-6: its precondition, a per-target decision that implies `accepted ∧ KeyMatchFinal ∧ GoodKem` on the compiled program, is now available (`keymatch_point`), but the new work is inside T4's selected-first game, which CE-9 did not touch. The remaining stages keep their estimates. Item 3 proper without CE-9b, CE-10 and CE-12 becomes about 23-41 agent-days remaining, against unit 3's 26-48.

No existing statement was changed or weakened: every theorem of the first pass keeps its statement up to whitespace, checked by a whitespace-normalized comparison with `09cca644`, and the five new statements are additions to `PqxdhDeploymentTargetRange`. `PqxdhDeploymentKemTransfer` changed only in its module docstring and `PqxdhDeploymentRegistrationTrace` did not change.

New: `designated_create` (the column `k = qCreate` is never designated), the sum lemma `sum_last_le` and the headline `kem_transfer_create` over `Fin qAccept × Fin qCreate`, described in "CE-9: what is proved". `kem_transfer_sum` and `kem_transfer_eligible` are unchanged.
- Premises in the module docstrings (semantic nit, hygiene nit). The module docstring of `PqxdhDeploymentTargetRange` now says that `range_game_create`, `range_game`, `designated_create` and `eligible_designated` use only `Phase1ReadBack`, `Phase2ReadBack` and the budget, and that `kem_transfer_eligible` and `kem_transfer_create` also use `FunctionalDecapsulation` and inherit the per-target IND-CCA advantage and correctness error of `kem_transfer`. The module docstring of `PqxdhDeploymentKemTransfer` adds the cost premises `KemBound setup caps` and `initBound` of the bills. The inventory comments of both modules say the same.

## CE-9b: what is proved

CE-9b makes theorem T4 (`PqxdhRegistrationJointKemRawBridge.actual_le_real`) an equality for the decisions that the deployment experiment uses, and with it turns the per-target KEM transfer of CE-9 into a two-sided bound, as plan section 9 (step 9.7 and the paragraph on the two-sided chain) requires for left-or-right secrecy. Two new modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` carry 39 guarded theorems; no existing proof module changed, and the root `BeaconcryptCore.lean` gains their two imports at the end of its import block. The `PqxdhDeployment*` modules at central `65e20738` carry 1306 `theorem` declarations, and 1345 with CE-9b.

| Module | Theorems | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentKemTwoSided` | 25 | Generic: the decidability-agnostic event helpers `chance_ext`, `chance_le_add`, `chance_eq_zero`, the real-number helpers `toReal_close`, `le_add_of_close`; the stopped-router monitor facts `source_step_good`, `stopped_step_good`, `stopped_good` and the final-state event `Completed`; the reverse chain of T4 `real_le_run`, `real_le_commands`, `real_le_actual`; `actual_eq_real`, `decided_eq_real`, `real_le_actual_add`, `actual_real_close`. Deployment: `decision_implies`, `defect_zero`, `eligible_real`, `kem_transfer_two_sided`, `kem_transfer_lower`, `abs_sum_sub_le`, `kem_transfer_sum_two_sided`, `sum_last_eq`, `split_create`, `kem_transfer_create_two_sided`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentLaterKemTwoSided` | 14 | The same for T4 of the logged source (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerBridge.actual_le_real`) and the LQ-F later game: `real_le_run`, `real_le_commands`, `real_le_actual`, `actual_eq_real`, `decided_eq_real`, `real_le_actual_add`, `actual_real_close`, the two-sided logged KEM hop `hop_close`, and `decision_implies_server`, `defect_zero_server`, `eligible_server_real`, `kem_transfer_server_two_sided`, `kem_transfer_server_lower`, `kem_transfer_server_sum_two_sided`. |

What the proof of T4 discards. T4 compares `Pr_actual[Decided ∧ GoodKem ∧ KeyMatchFinal]`, in the actual initialized execution of the tagged program, with `Pr[KemRawGame.sourceReal]`, the raw-ordinal ML-KEM real game, whose decision `KemGame.decision` is the tagged decision `decision ∧ accepted` of a completed continuation of an active cut and `false` otherwise. Its proof takes these steps. (1) `KemInitialization.actual_source_first` rewrites the actual execution into the selected-first game; this is an equality. (2) A failed Server construction gives `false` on both sides. (3) `commands_le_real` replaces T4's event by the equivalent `RunEvent` with a clear disclosure history and moves it to the stopped router with the existing equality `PqxdhDynamicKemJointStopping.history_event`, which adds the condition that the stopped run's monitor is clear; it then drops that condition. Nothing is lost there: a completed run of the stopped router from a clear state always ends clear, because the stopped router answers the selected reveal only by stopping (`stopped_good`). (4) `run_le_real` splits the stopped run at the KEM cut of the selected accept. A run that stops before the cut, completes before the cut, or reaches a cut that encapsulates to a key other than the reserved key at index `selected` contributes `false` to both sides, so nothing is lost. At an active cut the real game counts every completed continuation with `decision ∧ accepted`, while T4's event also asks that the recorded raw bytes verify, which holds at every active cut, and that their verified PQ key, which is then the reserved key at index `selected`, be the key at index `selected` of the created prefix of the reserved list. The last condition fails exactly when creation index `selected` is never created during the run. These runs, in which the selected accept encapsulates to the reserved key of a creation index that the run never creates, are the only ones the proof discards.

The reverse chain. `real_le_run`, `real_le_commands` and `real_le_actual` run the same steps backwards, on the same games and under T4's own premises (the program's query budget `certified` and `room`) and no other: `Pr[sourceReal] ≤ Pr_actual[Decided ∧ GoodKem]`. The stopped run's event is `Completed decision ∧ Good`, where `Completed` is the final-state form of the real game's decision (the run completed, the selected accept returned a session and the decision holds); `real_le_run` checks the four cases of the split, using `finish_stopped` for the active cut and `stopped_good` for the monitor, and `real_le_commands` moves the event back to the ordinary router with `history_event`.

The equality and the defect. Together with T4, the reverse chain gives `Pr_actual[Decided ∧ GoodKem ∧ KeyMatchFinal] ≤ Pr[sourceReal] ≤ Pr_actual[Decided ∧ GoodKem]`. Hence, for every decision and with no further premise, `Pr[sourceReal] ≤ Pr_actual[Decided ∧ GoodKem ∧ KeyMatchFinal] + Pr_actual[Decided ∧ GoodKem ∧ ¬KeyMatchFinal]` (`real_le_actual_add`) and `|Pr[sourceReal] − Pr_actual[Decided ∧ GoodKem ∧ KeyMatchFinal]| ≤ Pr_actual[Decided ∧ GoodKem ∧ ¬KeyMatchFinal]` in real numbers (`actual_real_close`): every run that the proof of T4 discards is a run of `Decided ∧ GoodKem ∧ ¬KeyMatchFinal`. If, on the support of the actual execution, `Decided ∧ GoodKem` implies `KeyMatchFinal`, T4 is an equality (`actual_eq_real`). For the class that plan section 9 names, decisions that imply `accepted ∧ KeyMatchFinal ∧ GoodKem` (on the support, `Decided` implies `GoodKem ∧ KeyMatchFinal`; `Decided` contains `accepted`), the left side is simply `Pr_actual[Decided]` (`decided_eq_real`).

Without such a hypothesis the equality can fail. In a defect run the selected accept carries an ML-KEM public key equal to the reserved key of a creation index that the run never creates; T4's event excludes the run and the real game counts it. Its probability is the chance that an adversary-chosen PQ key equals a key the run never generated, and bounding it would need a min-entropy property of ML-KEM key generation, which is not an approved assumption: `MlKemScheme` does not exclude a key generation that puts positive mass on one public key, and with such a key generation a program that creates no Beacon and submits a verifying accept with that key, for the decision that is always `true` and creation index 0, has T4's left side 0 and a positive real game. This counterexample is an informal argument, not a Lean statement; no Lean statement depends on it.

The deployment decision is in the class. `decision_implies` shows that on the support of the actual execution of `tag (compileFull …) i` from empty pools with a generated Server seed, `Decided (decision … g)` implies `GoodKem (selectedKem budget g)`, by `PqxdhDeploymentQuiet.approved_goodkem_unbudgeted`, and `KeyMatchFinal (selectedKem budget g)`, by `PqxdhDeploymentKemTransfer.keymatch_point` under `Phase1ReadBack` (through the traced program, as in `eligible_actual`). So T4's defect for the per-target decision has probability zero (`defect_zero`), and steps 9.1-9.2 become one equality (`eligible_real`): for a budget-respecting adversary, `Pr_CE[happens event ∧ designated claim = some g] = Pr[sourceReal … (decision … g) (compileFull …) runtime qCreate (selectedKem budget g) i]`, at the transported caps of CE-9 (`transported`, `compile_full_certified`, `transported_room`).

Two-sided transfer. With the two-sided KEM hop `PqxdhDeploymentKemTransfer.kem_hop_close` (the certified hop `KemRawCertified.ideal_close` with T6), `kem_transfer_two_sided` states, for every event, claim and target `g = (i, k)`: `|Pr_CE[happens event ∧ designated claim = some g] − Pr[opaqueBranch … (decision … g) (compileFull …) runtime qCreate (selectedKem budget g) i false]| ≤ Adv^{IND-CCA}(KemRawCertified.adversary … (decision … g) …) + correctnessError setup`, in real numbers, with exactly the adversary and terms of `kem_transfer`. For a left-or-right claim one of the events is `b' = b`, read from the adversary's output and the hidden bit in the ledger; the advantage needs a second event per target, as "CE-9b: open points" explains. `kem_transfer_lower` is the reverse of `kem_transfer` in `ENNReal`. Summing is exact on both sides: `kem_transfer_sum_two_sided` bounds `|Pr_CE[happens event ∧ (designated claim).isSome] − Σ_g Pr[opaqueBranch_g false]|` by `Σ_g (Adv_g + correctnessError)` over `Fin qAccept × Fin (qCreate + 1)` with the budgeted designation, and `kem_transfer_create_two_sided` does the same over `Fin qAccept × Fin qCreate` with plain eligibility, through the exact split `split_create`, which drops the never-designated column `k = qCreate` (`designated_create`) on the experiment side before the per-target bounds are applied. The bills of CE-9 (`kem_transfer_bills`) are bills of the same adversaries, so they apply unchanged.

The LQ-F later game. The logged source of the later-quantum mode has its own T4 (`PqxdhDeploymentLaterServerBridge.actual_le_real`), whose proof is that of `KemRawBridge` with the logged definitions and so discards the same runs. `PqxdhDeploymentLaterKemTwoSided` proves its reverse chain with the logged command compiler, the logged exposure (`LaterServerBridge.exposeAt_tag`, `scanned_map`), the logged selected-first game (`LaterServerInitialized.actual_source_first`) and the generic KEM hop for scanned programs (`LaterServerKemHop.sourceReal`), reusing `stopped_good` and `Completed`, under the logged T4's creation cap; the same equality, `Decided` form, defect bound and real-number form; the two-sided logged KEM hop `hop_close` (`LaterServerKemHop.certified_advantage` with `LaterServerOpaque.branch_opaque`); and, for the LQ-F game, `decision_implies_server` (from `LaterServerTransfer.keymatch_server` and `LaterServerTrace.goodKem_later`), `defect_zero_server`, `eligible_server_real`, `kem_transfer_server_two_sided`, `kem_transfer_server_lower` and `kem_transfer_server_sum_two_sided`, with exactly the adversary and terms of `kem_transfer_server`. These are confidentiality statements: the event may read the post-run adversary's output, which depends on the post-run reveal, and no authentication is claimed.

## CE-9b: decisions and deviations

- Equality rather than a two-sided bound. Plan section 9 asked for an equality or two-sided form of T4 for decisions that imply `accepted ∧ KeyMatchFinal ∧ GoodKem`. The equality holds under the weaker hypothesis that `Decided ∧ GoodKem` implies `KeyMatchFinal` on the support of the actual execution (`actual_eq_real`), and the plan's class is the special case `decided_eq_real`. The unconditional defect bound (`real_le_actual_add`, `actual_real_close`) is added, so that the discarded runs are identified in Lean, not only in prose.
- The class hypothesis is stated on the support of the actual execution, which is where `keymatch_point` and `approved_goodkem_unbudgeted` hold, not as a property of the decision function alone; `Decided` reads the program's result and its tag, and `GoodKem` and `KeyMatchFinal` read the final private state.
- A second module for the LQ-F later game, beyond the module that the task named. The logged source has its own copy of T4, and left-or-right confidentiality in the later-quantum mode needs its two-sided form as much as the run-phase claims need T4's. The logged module has no tight-domain form, because the LQ-F line has no `designated_create`; its sum is over `Fin qAccept × Fin (qCreate + 1)` as `kem_transfer_server_sum` is.
- New definitions: only `Completed`. All other statements are about existing games and events; no game, adversary or event of the KEM line is redefined.
- The helpers `chance_ext`, `chance_le_add` and `chance_eq_zero` take their decidability instances as implicit arguments instead of instance arguments. The KEM statements use `Classical.propDecidable` through `letI`, and a statement that synthesizes instances under `classical` gets different, not definitionally equal, instances for conjunctions, so `UniformTargetSelection.chance_eq` does not rewrite them.
- No `N · Adv(B_guess)` form (owner input 5) and no signed-hop combinator: the exact-sum forms keep one adversary per target.
- The Server identity index is 0, as in CE-9; the experiment is stated in both modes, as before.
- The working plan file was not edited.

## CE-9b: names for consumers

- Generic, run phase: `PqxdhDeploymentKemTwoSided.real_le_actual`, `actual_eq_real`, `decided_eq_real`, `real_le_actual_add`, `actual_real_close`, `Completed`, `stopped_good`; the helpers `chance_ext`, `chance_le_add`, `chance_eq_zero`, `toReal_close`, `le_add_of_close`, `abs_sum_sub_le`, `sum_last_eq`.
- Deployment, run phase: `PqxdhDeploymentKemTwoSided.decision_implies`, `defect_zero`, `eligible_real`, `kem_transfer_two_sided`, `kem_transfer_lower`, `kem_transfer_sum_two_sided`, `split_create`, `kem_transfer_create_two_sided`.
- Later game: `PqxdhDeploymentLaterKemTwoSided.actual_eq_real`, `decided_eq_real`, `real_le_actual_add`, `actual_real_close`, `hop_close`, `decision_implies_server`, `defect_zero_server`, `eligible_server_real`, `kem_transfer_server_two_sided`, `kem_transfer_server_lower`, `kem_transfer_server_sum_two_sided`.
- Use the generic names qualified: `real_le_run`, `real_le_commands`, `real_le_actual`, `actual_eq_real`, `decided_eq_real`, `real_le_actual_add` and `actual_real_close` are declared in both new modules, and `real_le_run`, `commands_le_real` and `actual_le_real` in `KemRawBridge` and `LaterServerBridge`.
- Item 2's T7 (step 9.5, two-sided by design) continues from the right side of `kem_transfer_two_sided`, the opaque branch of `(decision … g)` for `compileFull …`, exactly as from `kem_transfer`.

## CE-9b: contracts used

None new. The generic statements of both modules use only the premises of the T4 they reverse: the query budget `certified` and `room` of `KemRawBridge.actual_le_real`, and the creation cap `bounded` of `LaterServerBridge.actual_le_real`. The deployment statements use what CE-9 and the LQ-F transfer use: the approved codec contracts `Phase1ReadBack` (in `decision_implies`, `decision_implies_server` and everything after them) and `Phase2ReadBack` (through `eligible_actual` and `eligible_server`), `FunctionalDecapsulation setup.kem` (the KEM hop's contract, in the two-sided transfers and `kem_transfer_lower`), and the adversary budget. `defect_zero`, `decision_implies` and their later forms need only `Phase1ReadBack`. No hardness assumption, idealization or advantage term is added; the IND-CCA advantage and the correctness error are those of the existing KEM hops.

## CE-9b: open points

- CE-12's left-or-right headline still needs item 2's T7 (two-sided) after the KEM hop, CE-11's un-compiled ideal world, items 4 and 5, and a proof that the final world's left-or-right advantage is 0 (step 9.7).
- A left-or-right advantage is stated relative to one half of the eligibility probability, per target `Pr_CE[b' = b ∧ designated claim = some g] − ½ · Pr_CE[designated claim = some g]`, so both probabilities must be transferred: the two-sided transfer is used for two events per target, `b' = b` and `true` (or `b' = b` and `b' ≠ b`). The per-target decision `decision … event claim g` contains the event and `KemRawCertified.adversary` takes the decision, so each of the two transfers has its own certified IND-CCA adversary and bills its own IND-CCA advantage and correctness error, unless CE-12 defines one combined adversary that flips a coin between the two decisions. One application of `kem_transfer_sum_two_sided` or `kem_transfer_create_two_sided` with the event `b' = b` alone counts too few KEM terms for the advantage. The Lean statements need no change for this, since the event is arbitrary.
- The per-target argument that the final world's left-or-right advantage is 0 compares the ideal branches of the two events of a target. `opaqueBranch` passes the decision through `decideTag` inside the exposure (`PqxdhRegistrationJointKemRawOpaque.opaqueBranch`, `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemRawChallenge.rawScan`), so the two branches are not by construction readings of one run; the argument needs both to be images of one ideal run that does not depend on the decision, and this decision-generic form of the ideal run has to come from CE-11's un-compiled `CE.idealGame g`.
- The counterexample to the unconditional equality is an informal argument (above), not a Lean statement.
- The run-level liveness witness and the symbolic-eligibility corollaries of CE-12 remain open, as in CE-9.
- The review fix pass was checked by a confirming review of `40ba37e5`, which approved it; not integrated into central at this commit.

## CE-9b: re-estimate

The plan estimated CE-9b at 3-6 agent-days (section 14) and CE-9 kept that estimate. It was built, including the later game that the estimate did not cover, in one working session of under one agent-day: the only lossy step of T4 is the key-match conjunct at an active cut, every other step reverses with existing equalities, and the one new fact, `stopped_good`, is a short induction. The other stages keep their estimates; once CE-9b is reviewed and integrated, CE-12's left-or-right part no longer waits on it. The two left-or-right open points above are not estimated separately: the second event per target is statement work inside CE-12, and the decision-generic ideal run belongs to CE-11's un-compiled ideal world.
