The experiment. `PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.game … bounds mode A` constructs the Server from a generated seed and runs the adversary of mode `mode` against the CE handler from an empty ledger. The CE handler is the trial's native handler behind the secrecy layer. The layer forwards every coin and command unchanged and records it with its mode O reply. `test handle left right` requires equal nonzero lengths. The first admitted test draws the hidden bit with one experiment coin and fixes the test handle. A later test is admitted only on the same handle or on a handle with the same public designation, computed on the transcript so far. An admitted test is one send of `left` or `right` by the bit. `fresh handle length` requires `min ≤ length ≤ max`, draws a uniform message of that many bytes with one coin per byte when the command is issued, keeps it private and sends it. A rejected secrecy command changes nothing but the transcript, and the experiment's own draws never enter the transcript. Mode P is mode O behind `observeFull`, whose replies are the mode P projections of the mode O replies (`observeFull_project`). Secrecy claims (left-or-right and fresh-message secrecy) are stated only for mode P, as plan section 3.6 and owner input 4 (accepted on 2026-10-06) require: in mode O a raw receive returns the actual plaintext, so forwarding a test or fresh ciphertext to the partner's raw receive reveals the hidden message, and the secrecy commands are just sends there. Authentication claims may use either mode. `game_wrap` shows that the experiment is the trial's native game run on the wrapped adversary, with the ledger moved into the final state. Every record seal handed to the frame writer is logged with the record handle of its command, including seals whose writer then fails, and `logged_forget` shows that the log changes no result.

Logs. Both components of `Logs` are analysis logs, not public views. The transcript stores the mode O reply of every query in both modes, so in mode P it contains the plaintexts of raw receives, including those of test or fresh ciphertexts that the adversary forwarded; the adversary's view of an entry is `Entry.view mode`. The produced-frame log contains frames whose writer failed and that were never published, each tagged with a `FrameTag`: the peer key ID of a Server send, the owner handle of every Beacon record command, and `none` for a Server raw receive, which names no handle before routing. The tag never contains a plaintext, so the hidden messages of `test` and `fresh` never enter `Logs.produced`; together with the frame's sequence number and sender it gives the plan's (side, handle, sequence) tuple. Events meant to be public must be built from `Entry.view` and from the frames and tags only.

The compile lemma. `compile_full_game` and `compile_full_source` state that the experiment in either mode equals the merged initialized execution of `compileFull A` (the trial compiler applied to the wrapped adversary) jointly over the Server's public data, the adversary's output, the whole ledger (transcript, test session, fresh messages), the whole deployment state with the produced frames, and the runtime state. They use exactly the trial's two codec contracts, `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`. Because the wrapped adversary draws the bit and the fresh messages at the same positions as the experiment, no sampling-swap lemma is needed.

Budget transport. `wrap_bound` and `compile_full_bound` transport per-class caps through the secrecy layer and the compiler; `PqxdhDeploymentCompileCaps.transported` gives the closed form. The compiled program and its tagged form at any raw-accept ordinal make at most `qCoin + qTest + qFreshBytes` coins with `qFreshBytes = ℓmax · qFresh`, the registration and HMAC caps unchanged, `qHash + S + R + qTest + qFresh` BLAKE2b queries, `qSS + qSR + qTest + qFresh` Server kernel reveals, `qBS + qBR + qTest + qFresh` established-kernel reads and no identity, KEM, prekey or one-time read. `tag_certified` is the `certified` premise of T4 at these caps, and `transported_room` its `room` premise for `count + 1 = qCreate + 1`. The coin and BLAKE2b caps are looser than the plan; this is listed below as a plan change for the owner to confirm.

Selection (CE-4). For a run with outcome space `Ω` and a public `designate : Ω → Option G`, `split` writes the probability of an event on eligible outcomes as the exact sum over targets of the probability of the event with that target designated; `guess` writes it as `card G` times the probability that a uniform guess drawn before the run is the designated target; `advantage` is the split form for a left-or-right bit with the eligibility-weighted half subtracted. `sum_eq_card_mul` and `signed` turn a sum of per-target probabilities or of signed per-target differences into `card G` times the probability or signed difference of the run with a uniformly guessed target; with signed per-target hops this gives the owner's `N · Adv(B_guess)` wording. All statements are exact and use the measure form `𝒟[f <$> run] {true}` of the KEM theorems.

Publicity (CE-5). `agree_game` shows that every outcome of the experiment, in either mode, satisfies `Agree`: the transcript summary counts exactly the native owners, the public phase of every handle is the class of its native phase, every created binding is the owner's pinned Server identity and key ID, every successful bundle in the transcript is the Phase 1 writer output of the candidate the owner signed with its own identity key and the KEM public key it holds until finish, and every successful build response is the Phase 2 writer output of fields carrying the Server identity, which never changes. `phase_public`, `owner_public` and `failure_public` are its readings for `phase`, `owner` and `NoBundleFailure`: a rejected bundle sets an owner's failure flag exactly when the owner is natively Fresh. `owner_kemIndex` shows that in the annotated execution of any program the owner created `k`-th holds KEM creation index `k` and the KEM pool has one key per owner. The transcript functions read only queries and the replies of create, bundle, accept, build and finish commands, never a raw-receive reply. `summarize_view`, `phase_view` and `designate_view` prove it: two transcripts with the same mode P view (`SameView`) have the same summary, public phases, owner numbering, `NoBundleFailure`, designation and budgeted designation. So these functions are public in mode P although the transcript stores mode O replies.

Designation and key match (CE-5). The designation compares keys, as plan section 7 says, not signed fields. On the Server side, the accept bytes of the session that built the peer are read into a raw candidate, and `acceptedKeys` applies to it the source attached verification and parsing of the setup's crypto (`PqxdhJointPhase1Reports.sourceAccept`), which yields the same identity and keys as the native verification prefix `RawSignatures.verified` whenever that succeeds (`verified_keys`); only accepts that returned a session handle are looked up. The designated owner is the smallest created owner whose published bundle carries the candidate's identity field and whose published PQ field, split and parsed without a signature check (`publishedPq`), is the verified PQ key. On the Beacon side, the session must also verify to the PQ key read from the Beacon's published bundle. An accept that carries an honest owner's identity and published PQ key under a different valid signature is therefore designated like the owner's own bytes, and `designate_peer_complete` and `designate_owner_complete` show that the designation is made whenever its conditions hold. `designate_keymatch` (with `Phase1ReadBack`): for a Server peer designated `(i, k)`, every verification of the accept bytes of session `i` by the native verification prefix `RawSignatures.verified` yields owner `k`'s identity key and the PQ key owner `k` signed in its honest published bundle (`PublishedKey`). `owner_link` (with `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`): an owner designated `(i, c)` is natively Established and pinned to the protected key ID and to the Server's own identity; the response of session `i` is the Phase 2 writer output of fields carrying the Server identity; the ciphertext read from the owner's accepted finish input is the KEM ciphertext of those fields; and the PQ key verified at accept `i` is the one the owner published. `keymatch_game` and `link_game` state both on every outcome of the experiment. Both proofs read the verified key through `verified_keys` (the candidate and source attached verification inside `RawSignatures.verified`) and the published key through `publishedPq_candidate`; they use no signature-scheme property and no wiring law.

What `PublishedKey` does not give. It ties owner `k`'s published key to the owner's native KEM pair only while the owner is not finished: `kemPublic` is `none` for Established and Aborted owners, so in the designated case, where owner `k` has usually finished, `designate_keymatch` says nothing about KEM creation index `k` of the pool. Plan section 9 step 9.1 expected CE-5's `designate_keymatch` to yield `KeyMatchFinal k`, which compares with the final KEM pool of the annotated execution. The existing ownership invariant `PqxdhRegistrationJointOwnership.Owned` does not close this gap, because it constrains only the live KEM pair, which finish also erases. The step needs a new invariant on the pool-carrying annotated execution of `tag (compileFull A) i`, together with a relation that carries the compiled program's transcript into that execution: every bundle of owner `k` in the transcript carries pool key `k`, which holds when the bundle is produced (ownership and `owner_kemIndex`) and persists because the pool only appends. Unit A does not prove it. Strengthening `BundleShaped` with a ghost of the published key is not available within unit A: the native deployment state keeps no KEM key after finish, and a ghost added to it would have to be reproduced by `merge` from the source14 state, which does not keep it either. The step is re-estimated below as part of CE-9.

Freshness (CE-5). `approved_goodkem`: in the exact execution that T4 is stated for, the tagged compiled program of a complete-interface adversary, in either mode and at any raw-accept ordinal, ends every run with `GoodKem` for every KEM creation index. `kernel_reads_unrevealed`: in the same execution no identity index is ever revealed (`revealedAt` is false). Both follow from `quiet_initialized`: every annotated step other than an identity or KEM field read records only creation, signing, static DH and decapsulation observations, and the compiled program makes no such read. The forms named in the plan carry a budget that the adversary respects, which T4's `certified` premise needs anyway; the plan states them without one. `approved_goodkem_unbudgeted` and `kernel_reads_unrevealed_unbudgeted` are the plan's unconditional statements: they hold for every adversary, because no compiled command issues an identity or KEM read (`compile_unread`, `compile_full_unread`, via the generic `simulate_zero`).

## Unit A: decisions and deviations

Fidelity deviations from production Rust. `R/` is `beaconcrypt/src/`. D1-D4 were approved on 2026-10-06 (plan section 19, owner input 10); D5 is the owner's decision on F1, option (a), recorded in plan section 21 with central `4037fae3`.

| # | Deviation | Rust anchor | Owner decision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D1 | `create` does not re-run runtime initialization; `ensureInit` runs once at Server birth, and a wrong-width pin is rejected with no draw. | `Beacon::try_new` calls `ensure_init` (`R/beacon.rs:65-66`); `Server::try_new` (`R/server.rs:154-155`) | 2026-10-06, plan sections 3.9 and 19 |
| D2 | No public getters: Server `identity_key_kid`, `identity_pk`, `server_kid`, `pk_by_kid`, `ratchet_status`; Beacon `identity_key_kid`, `identity_pk`, `server_id`, `server_kid`, `pq_pk`, `associated_data`, `ratchet_status`, `get_prekey_pk`, `get_onetime_pk`, including the pre-bundle identity, PQ and prekey getters. | `R/server.rs:173-207`; `R/beacon.rs:84-202` | 2026-10-06, plan sections 3.9 and 19 |
| D3 | No snapshot-returning record APIs (`*_and_update`, `*_json`) and no pre-Phase-1 helpers, so `FreshWithCoins` is unreachable in CE; `phaseClass` still maps it to the public Fresh phase. | `R/server.rs:131-142`; `R/beacon.rs:222-296` | 2026-10-06, plan sections 3.9 and 19 |
| D4 | The adversary chooses the build payload: `build session (payload : Option Bytes)`, with `none` selecting `REGISTRATION_WITNESS`. In Rust the host chooses it. | `build_registration_response`, `R/server.rs:424-502` (payload default at line 459) | 2026-10-06, plan sections 3.9 and 19 |
| D5 | The adversary takes no input and learns the Server identity key through the protocol, from build responses. | `Server::identity_pk` (`R/server.rs:176`) would give it directly | F1 option (a), plan section 21 |

Other decisions taken where the plan left a choice:

- Layering. The secrecy commands and the transcript are a layer over the trial's native handler, and the CE handler is their flattened composition. The produced-frame log needs the record steps themselves, so the trial's native and compiled record commands were extended in place: the definitions of `State`, `Local`, `serverStep`, `beaconStep`, `serverLocal`, `beaconLocal` and `merge` now carry the produced frames. The trial statements that mention them changed only by this extra component; in particular `compile_run` and `compile_native` now start from `⟨native, 0, fun _ => 0, []⟩`. No trial statement was weakened or removed.
- Logs. Publication and receipt are functions of the transcript, because they are exactly the send, test and fresh replies and the raw-receive entries. Produced frames are tagged with `FrameTag` (see "Logs" above) rather than with the plan's `(side, handle, seq, context)`; the handle includes the side, and the frame carries its sequence number and sender.
- Secrecy commands. A rejected test or fresh command is recorded in the transcript with its `none` reply and changes nothing else. The fresh handle of a claim is the position of the admitted fresh message in the ledger. `Claim` has only the fresh-message claim, since the optional root-guess endpoint CE-RG is not built. Secrecy claims are made only in mode P.
- Designation. Keys are compared on both sides (see above); the identity is compared through the published and accepted identity fields, which encode the key uniquely (`parseIdentity_eq_some_iff`). The Beacon side requires the public Established phase, the protected key ID, the finish ciphertext equal to the ciphertext of session `i`'s build response, the Server identity in that response equal to the Beacon's pin, and the accept of session `i` verifying to the Beacon's published PQ key. The pin is compared with the identity in the build response because the adversary takes no input (D5); `owner_link` proves with `Phase2ReadBack` that it is the Server's own identity. Because keys rather than signed bytes are compared, the CE-12 corollary for Server-side symbolic eligibility needs only the weak-EUF term `ε_A2` of plan section 9: a successful accept under an honest owner's identity that is not designated verifies a PQ message that the owner did not sign in a published bundle (the owner published another key or none), and on `NoBundleFailure` runs the owner signed no other PQ message, so it is a weak forgery. No SUF-CMA or signature-uniqueness assumption is needed.
- Router. The Server header router stays a free parameter of the game, as in production, where the header router can route frames the full decoder rejects. The approved contract `RawCtxServerRouting.DecoderAgreement route codec.frameRead` is the premise for later claims that need it; no unit A statement uses it.
- Selection statements use the measure form over `ProbComp` runs; the event and guessing forms need `SampleableType G`, so the target domain must be nonempty (see "Names for consumers").

## Unit A: plan changes for owner confirmation

- Coin and BLAKE2b caps, in addition to the read caps already accepted in plan section 20. The per-class transport charges one coin per test, although only the first admitted test draws the bit, and counts each test and fresh message both as a possible Server send and as a possible Beacon send, because `wrapCost` charges every test and fresh query to both send classes. The BLAKE2b cap is therefore `qHash + S + R + qTest + qFresh`, exceeding the plan's `qHash + S + R` by `qTest + qFresh`, and the coin cap is `qCoin + qTest + qFreshBytes`, which charges `qTest` coins for the bit where the plan's `qCoin + qFreshBytes + 1` charges one. Section 20 kept these rows of the table unchanged, so this is a further plan change for the owner to confirm. Both caps are sound, and later KEM and CTX bills (CE-9 and later) will carry the extra `qTest + qFresh` BLAKE2b queries and the per-test coins. Tightening them needs a bound of the composed per-query program instead of the per-class composition.
- The step from the deployment-level key match to `KeyMatchFinal k` moves from CE-5 to CE-9 step 9.1, with the new invariant described above and the re-estimate below.

## Unit A: names for consumers

Use these names qualified; several short names are generic and overlap. `State`, `handler` and `game` exist in both `PqxdhDeploymentNative` and `PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy`, `produce` in `PqxdhDeploymentNative` and as `PqxdhDeploymentCompile.Local.produce`, `view` in `PqxdhDeploymentEligibility` and as `PqxdhDeploymentInterface.Entry.view`, and `empty` in four places.

- Experiment: `PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.game setup codec checks route ensureInit identityIndex bounds mode A` (not `PqxdhDeploymentNative.game`). State `PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.State` is `Ledger × PqxdhDeploymentNative.State`; handler `PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.handler`; logs `PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.logs`.
- Compile: `PqxdhDeploymentCompileSecrecy.compileFull`, `mergeFull`, `compile_full_game`, `compile_full_source`, `compile_full_bound`, `compile_full_unread`.
- Budgets: the adversary budget is `PqxdhDeploymentInterface.Budget` (fourteen classes) with `Respects`, `cap`, `freshBytes`, `capacity` and `targets`. The source14 caps `PqxdhDeploymentCompileCaps.transported` are a different type, `PqxdhRegistrationJointKemCommands.Budget`; `tag_certified` and `transported_room` give the T4 premises.
- Transcript functions: `PqxdhDeploymentTranscript.phase`, `owner`, `NoBundleFailure`, `designate crypto codec serverKid`, `designateIn`, with `crypto = setup.source.crypto` in the experiment; publicity in mode P via `designate_view`.
- Publicity and key match: `PqxdhDeploymentEligibility.agree_game`, `phase_public`, `owner_public`, `failure_public`, `designate_owner_native`; `PqxdhDeploymentKeyMatch.keymatch_game`, `link_game`, `PublishedKey`.
- Freshness and KEM index: `PqxdhDeploymentQuiet.approved_goodkem_unbudgeted`, `kernel_reads_unrevealed_unbudgeted` (or the budgeted forms), `quiet_initialized`; `PqxdhDeploymentKemIndex.owner_kemIndex`.
- Selection: `UniformTargetSelection.split`, `split_of_eligible`, `guess`, `advantage`, `sum_eq_card_mul`, `signed`. `guess`, `sum_eq_card_mul` and `signed` need `SampleableType G`. For the target domain `Fin budget.accept × Fin budget.capacity` no instance is found automatically, even with `[NeZero budget.accept]`, because `Budget.capacity` does not unfold. A consumer must assume `[NeZero budget.accept]` (a positive accept budget) and supply `NeZero budget.capacity` locally, for example `⟨by simp [PqxdhDeploymentInterface.Budget.capacity]⟩`; with both, the instance is found (checked by a scratch probe).

## Unit A: contracts used

`Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`, both approved on 2026-10-06 and listed in both assumption lists, are premises of `compile_full_game`, `compile_full_source`, `designate_keymatch` (only `Phase1ReadBack`), `owner_link`, `keymatch_game` and `link_game`. After the fix pass, `designate_keymatch` and `owner_link` no longer use the attached-verification wiring `setup.wiring.verifyAttached`; the lemma `verified_signed_pq`, which uses it, remains as a separate fact. `FrameReadBack` and `DecoderAgreement` remain approved but unused. No hardness assumption, idealization or advantage term is added.

## Unit A: open points

- CE-6 onwards were not started in unit A. Unit 3 (below) completes CE-6 (CE-R1, CE-C3, CE-R1') and CE-7 (CE-A2, CE-A2pub), and CE-9 (above) the per-target KEM transfer; the A1 embedding and every later stage are not started.
- `KeyMatchFinal k` in the annotated execution of `tag (compileFull A) i` needs the new bundle-to-pool invariant described above (CE-9 step 9.1). Closed in CE-9: `PqxdhDeploymentRegistrationTrace.Traced` with `Pooled`, and `PqxdhDeploymentKemTransfer.keymatch_point`.
- A range lemma for `designateIn`: that `designateIn` agrees with `designate` and that budget-respecting runs designate inside `Fin budget.accept × Fin budget.capacity` (session below the accept attempts, owner below the creates). Closed in CE-9: `PqxdhDeploymentTargetRange.range_game_create` (owner below the creates), `range_game`, `designateIn_range` and `eligible_designated`.
- A run-level liveness witness: an honest-relay adversary whose run designates `(0, 0)` on the witness setup. `designate_peer_complete` and `designate_owner_complete` show non-vacuity at the level of the definition; the run-level witness needs bundle, accept, build and finish to succeed on a concrete setup and is left to CE-13.
- The post-run "later quantum" reveal mode was still undesigned in unit A. Unit A left the interface open to it: `compile_full_game` equates the whole final state, so the mode can be added after the run without changing the command set. It has since been designed, and its stages LQ-1 and LQ-2 are described above.
- One live Server object; no adversary input (D5).

## Unit A: re-estimate for the remaining units

Unit A covered CE-1, CE-2, the CE-3 remainder, CE-4 and CE-5, which the plan and the trial estimated at about 12-23 agent-days, in one working session; this agrees with the trial's speed on operational equalities and invariants. Remaining by reviewable unit of the plan, in agent-days: unit 3 (CE-6 replay and CE-7 provenance) 5-9, because the `Agree`, `Quiet` and `Positional` invariant patterns and the footprints are reusable; unit 4 (CE-8, the A1 embedding over all Beacons) 12-20, a new reduction; unit 5 (CE-9 per-target KEM transfer) 5-10, raised from 2-4 because step 9.1 needs the bundle-to-pool invariant on the annotated execution and a relation carrying the compiled program's transcript into it (3-6 of the 5-10; the trial's `compile_simulation` is stated for the native schedule, so either an annotated version of its per-step relation or a reply-provenance argument for the compiled ledger is needed), while `compile_full_source`, `tag_certified`, `transported_room`, `approved_goodkem_unbudgeted`, `owner_kemIndex` and `designate_keymatch` supply the other inputs, plus CE-9b 3-6; unit 6 (item 2 as CE-10, 50-86 as planned, and CE-11, 6-12); unit 7 (CE-12, 8-14, after items 4 and 5); documentation and bridges (CE-13, CE-14) 5-9. Item 3 proper without CE-9b, CE-10 and CE-12 is then about 33-60 agent-days remaining. The figures for CE-8, CE-10 and CE-11 keep most of the uncertainty, because they need new reductions rather than equalities.

## Unit A: review fix pass

The review's findings and what changed, in commit order:

- Designation by keys (semantic, minor). `designate` compared the attached-signature PQ fields byte for byte, so an accept with an honest owner's identity and published key under a different valid signature was not eligible, and the CE-12 corollary would have needed SUF-CMA or signature uniqueness. It now compares the verified PQ key with the parsed published key on both sides, as described above. `designate_keymatch`, `owner_link`, `keymatch_game`, `link_game` and `designate_owner_native` were re-proved with unchanged conclusions; their designation hypothesis now refers to the new definition with `setup.source.crypto` (or an explicit `crypto`). `designate_peer` and `designate_owner` unfold the new definition, so their conclusions now list its conditions (the verified keys and the published PQ key). The secrecy layer `ledgerImpl`, `SameSession` and `wrap` gained the `crypto` argument, which the experiment instantiates with `setup.source.crypto`, so `secrecy_frame`, `ledgerImpl_forward`, `run_wrap`, `game_wrap`, `wrap_step`, `wrap_bound`, `ledger_secrecy` and `handler_run` changed only by that argument. New: `acceptedKeys`, `publishedPq`, `designate_peer_complete`, `designate_owner_complete`, `verified_keys`, `publishedPq_candidate`.
- Mode P publicity (semantic, nit). New: `SameView`, `view_respects`, `findSome_view`, `summarize_view`, `phase_view`, `designate_view`.
- Budget premise of the freshness lemmas (hygiene, nit). New budget-free forms `simulate_zero`, `compile_unread`, `compile_full_unread`, `compiled_unread_unbudgeted`, `approved_goodkem_unbudgeted`, `kernel_reads_unrevealed_unbudgeted`; the budgeted forms are unchanged.
- Plaintext in the produced-frame tag (semantic nit and hygiene minor). The tag is now the record handle (`FrameTag`, `serverTag`, `beaconTag`) instead of the command with its plaintext; `serverStep_run`, `serverLocal_none`, `serverLocal_some`, `beaconLocal_present`, `beaconStep_present`, `merge_produce`, `merge_put` and `merge_owner_put` changed only by the tag in place of the command.
- Documentation: the trial-module change counts (hygiene, minor), secrecy claims only in mode P (semantic, nit), the D1-D5 table (semantic, nit), the coin and BLAKE2b caps as a plan change (semantic, nit), names for consumers with the `NeZero` requirement and the `designateIn` range lemma (hygiene, nit), the `KeyMatchFinal` gap and the CE-9 re-estimate (semantic, minor), and the liveness witness as an open point (hygiene, nit).

## Unit A: checks

Every module was checked on its own with Lean 4.34 (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) under the shared lock, in import order, into the unit A scratch prefix mirrored from the trial prefix; after the fix pass all 21 modules were re-checked in import order and every guarded check exited 0 with an empty log. All 250 statements carry exact `#print axioms` guards: 225 depend on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, 14 on `propext` alone, 10 on `propext` and `Quot.sound`, and one on no axiom. No module uses `sorry`, `admit`, an axiom declaration, `native_decide`, `implemented_by`, `extern`, `unsafe` or `opaque`. After each module commit the root import check of `BeaconcryptCore.lean` exited 0 with an empty log, and the focused `check-inventory` (minimal Nix flake, all files of the commit staged, other changes held aside) passed. No full `lake build`, `make verify`, mutation testing or `nix-collect-garbage` was run.

## Unit 3: what is proved

Unit 3 completes CE-6 (replay) and CE-7 (provenance) of the plan. Seven new modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` carry 148 guarded statements, 129 from the first pass and 19 from the review fix pass; no unit A or trial module was modified. In commit order: `PqxdhDeploymentReplay` (12, CE-R1), `PqxdhDeploymentAcceptDetection` (14, the mid-run report detection lemma), `PqxdhDeploymentProvenance` (16, CE-A2, its discharged reveal qualifier and the query bills of its reductions), `PqxdhDeploymentSigningHistory` (33, the `NoBundleFailure` signing-history lemma), `PqxdhDeploymentPublishedTuple` (31, CE-A2pub and CE-R1', per owner and on `NoBundleFailure` runs), `PqxdhDeploymentRecordKernels` (14, kernel provenance for CE-C3) and `PqxdhDeploymentRecordReplay` (28, CE-C3). Every statement below quantifies over every setup, codec, check set, router, runtime initializer, freshness bound and mode, and over every adversary of that mode.

CE-R1 (`PqxdhDeploymentReplay.replay_game`). On every outcome of `PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.game setup codec checks route ensureInit identityIndex bounds mode adversary` that constructed a Server and ended in `final`, and for every byte string `rid`, at most one transcript entry is a successful accept whose registration ID is `rid`, and the live Server object's consumed list equals the list of those registration IDs in transcript order: `final.1.transcript.countP (fun entry => entryRegistration setup.source.crypto codec entry = some rid) ≤ 1 ∧ final.2.native.shared.2.server.consumed = acceptIds setup.source.crypto codec final.1.transcript`. The registration ID of an accept entry (`registrationOf`) is read from public bytes: the identity key followed by the one-time key that the codec reader and the source attached verification yield from the raw candidate, which is exactly the pair the native accept consumes (`accept_registration`, the new per-accept `nativeAccept` lemma). The proof is the run invariant `Registered` over the experiment handler, from source14's admission and count lemmas (`PqxdhRegistrationSchedule.handler_admission`, `PqxdhRegistrationSchedule.handler_consumed_count`; `PqxdhRegistrationAdmission` has different lemmas with the same short names) for the other commands; no budget, codec law or assumption is used.

CE-C3 (`PqxdhDeploymentRecordReplay.record_replay_game`). On the same outcomes, `(∀ kid, (peerSequences final.1.transcript kid).Nodup) ∧ ∀ index, (ownerSequences final.1.transcript index).Nodup`. `peerSequences T kid` lists the sequence numbers of the successful Server raw receives of `T` whose decrypted record names key ID `kid`; `ownerSequences T index` lists those of the successful raw receives of owner `index` and, for sequence one, its successful finish, which accepted the registration record. The run invariant `Tracked` says that every peer and every owner with a kernel carries `RawCtxReceiveConsumption.ConsumedRefines` with exactly the receive positions of these sequence numbers consumed, and that a handle without a kernel accepted nothing. `PqxdhDeploymentRecordKernels` supplies the kernel provenance: a build installs a peer only at the free key ID of its committed candidate, with the candidate's initial kernel after the first registration send (`build_live`, `buildOracle_peers`); a finish establishes an owner with the initial kernel of its root after the source receive that accepted sequence one, so exactly that position is consumed (`tail_finished`, `finish_owners`); every other registration command keeps every kernel (`source_step`). Raw receives go through `RawCtxReceiveConsumption.raw_spent` on the routed peer or the addressed owner, and sends, including those of the secrecy commands, through `send_spent`. Zero loss: no budget, probability, codec law or assumption.

CE-A2 (`PqxdhDeploymentProvenance.a2_bound`). For an adversary that respects `budget` and with the attached-signature format rule `shortRejects` (the existing premise, with `setup.wiring`):

```lean
letI := Classical.propDecidable
𝒟[(fun final => decide (NativeForgery setup codec checks ensureInit none (budget.create + 1)
    (compileFull setup codec route bounds mode adversary) runtime final)) <$>
  PqxdhJointPhase1Reports.run setup.joint (budget.create + 1)
    (PqxdhRegistrationJointReportCompiler.initialized setup codec checks ensureInit none (budget.create + 1)
      (compileFull setup codec route bounds mode adversary) runtime)] {true} ≤
  ∑ target : Fin (budget.create + 1), PqxdhJointIdentity.advantage setup.joint
    (reduction setup codec checks route ensureInit bounds mode adversary budget.create runtime fallback
      ordinaryFallback target)
```

The probability space is the report-instrumented initialized execution of the compiled program with a generated Server seed. The bound itself uses no codec law. Reading it as a statement about the deployment experiment uses `report_game`, which shows, under the approved codec laws `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack` (needed through `compile_full_source`), that the projection of this execution to the constructed Server's public data, the adversary's output and the whole ledger is exactly the deployment experiment with the Server's identity index 0: `compile_full_source` gives the deployment game the identity index equal to the number of seeds in the pool at which the instrumented execution starts, and that pool is empty. `NativeForgery` (`PqxdhDeploymentAcceptDetection`) holds for a final outcome when the run passes through a successful raw accept at some position, the accept's raw bytes pass the native verification prefix `RawSignatures.verified` with the identity key of a generated owner of the owner registry, that owner is unrevealed, and the verified key tuple is not among the owner's chronological signing attempts, failed publications included, all read in the state reached when the accept returned. The bound is one ordinary joint-use weak-EUF term per generated identity position, summed over the `qCreate + 1` positions (the Beacons and the generated Server seed), with coefficient one: no accept-position, owner, field or report-position factor and no collision term. `fallback` and `ordinaryFallback` are the arbitrary default forgeries of `fieldOrigin_advantage_le_sum`. The per-ordinal fallback of the plan was not needed.

The unrevealed conjunct of `NativeForgery` holds on every run of the compiled program, so CE-A2 holds for every generated owner (`PqxdhDeploymentProvenance.a2_generated_bound`). Stopped right after its raw accept at any position, the compiled program issues no identity-field, KEM-field, prekey or one-time read (`upto_unread`: the scan keeps the zero caps of `compile_full_unread` and the stopping step issues only the accept). By `PqxdhDeploymentQuiet.quiet_initialized`, the source execution of such a program records no reveal observation, so `revealedAt` is false for every identity index on every stopped run (`stopped_unrevealed`). Hence `NativeForgery` of the compiled program is the same set of final outcomes as `GeneratedForgery`, the event without the unrevealed conjunct (`generated_forgery_iff`), and `a2_generated_bound` bounds `GeneratedForgery` by the same sum, with the same premises; since `GeneratedForgery` contains `NativeForgery` for every program, it is at least as strong as `a2_bound`. So no identity is revealed during any run of the compiled program; unit A's `kernel_reads_unrevealed_unbudgeted` shows the same at the end of every run of its tagged form. The owner's post-run reveal mode, which discloses honest Ed25519 identity secrets after the run, does not change this event, which is about accepts during the run.

Query bills of the A2 reductions (`PqxdhDeploymentProvenance.a2_bills`). These are the reductions of `a2_bound`, `a2_generated_bound`, CE-A2pub and CE-R1', and the bills have the form of the accept/KEM join's T5b. For an adversary that respects `budget`, every reduction at every generated position, for every challenger public identity, makes at most `3 * budget.bundle` signing requests and at most `budget.finish + budget.accept` converted-DH requests (`a2_service_bound`). With T5b's uniform-bill premises (`initBound`, which bounds `ensureInit` by `qInit` queries, and `KemBound setup caps`), the A2 client pays at most the uniform bill `PqxdhRegistrationJointIdentityUniform.total caps qInit (PqxdhDeploymentCompileCaps.transported bounds budget)` (`a2_client_uniform`), every reduction makes at most `32 * budget.create` more uniform requests for the other reserved seeds (`a2_reduction_uniform`), and every endpoint game `PqxdhJointIdentity.game setup.joint (reduction … target)` makes at most `32 * (budget.create + 1)` queries plus the client bill in total (`a2_game_uniform`). The proofs transport the compiled program's caps (`compile_full_certified`) through the report compiler and the terminal batches, which issue no request of any source class (`client_class_bound`), and apply `PqxdhJointPhase1Reports.fieldReduction_query_bound` and `fieldReductionGame_uniform_bound`. The four per-reduction lemmas hold for every reduction count; `a2_bills` collects them at the count `budget.create` of the bounds.

The `NoBundleFailure` signing-history lemma (`PqxdhDeploymentSigningHistory.signing_history`). On every outcome of the initialized source execution of the compiled program from empty pools, for every owner of the registry with `NoBundleFailureAt T owner`, either the owner published no bundle and its whole signing history is empty, or its first published bundle is `codec.write1` of the candidate it signed with its own identity key over one key tuple `keys`, and its whole signing history is exactly the three messages of `keys`. It is proved with the invariant `Signed` along the stacked execution of the secrecy layer, the compiler and the annotated Server schedule (`execution`); no codec law and no assumption is used.

CE-A2pub (`PqxdhDeploymentPublishedTuple.a2pub_owner_bound` and its corollary `a2pub_bound`), with `shortRejects`, the approved codec laws `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack` and the budget: `𝒟[(fun outcome => decide (OwnerEvent setup codec outcome)) <$> (PqxdhDeploymentSecrecy.game setup codec checks route ensureInit 0 bounds mode adversary).run runtime] {true}` is at most the same sum as CE-A2, over the same reductions. `OwnerEvent` holds when the outcome constructed a Server and, for some created owner that made no rejected bundle call in Fresh (`NoBundleFailureAt`), some successful accept carries a raw candidate that the source attached verification of `setup.source.crypto` accepts with that owner's identity key and a key tuple other than the tuple of that owner's published bundle, read from public bytes without a signature check (`publishedTuple`), or that owner published none (`OwnerForgery`). A failed bundle call of another owner does not void the bound. The planned event `GameEvent` (`PublishedForgery` with `NoBundleFailure` for every owner) is contained in `OwnerEvent` (`game_owner`), so `a2pub_bound`, whose statement is unchanged, is now a corollary. The proof uses only the forged owner's `NoBundleFailureAt` (`owner_published_failure`), and `game_report_holds` carries any property of the final transcript and the owners' identity keys from the deployment game to the instrumented execution.

CE-R1' (`PqxdhDeploymentPublishedTuple.r1prime_owner_bound` and its corollary `r1prime_bound`), with the same premises: the probability of `ReplayOwnerEvent` (a constructed Server and two successful accepts at distinct transcript positions whose raw candidates the source attached verification accepts with the identity key of one created owner that made no rejected bundle call in Fresh, `DoubleAcceptAt`) is at most the same sum. `replay_owner_published` derives the event inclusion deterministically from CE-R1: two accepts of an owner's published tuple carry one registration ID, which the live Server object consumes once, so one of the two is a published-tuple forgery at that owner. The planned event `ReplayEvent` (with `NoBundleFailure` for every owner) is contained in `ReplayOwnerEvent` (`replay_owner`), so `r1prime_bound` and `replay_published` keep their statements and are corollaries.

## Unit 3: decisions and deviations

- CE-A2 is detected mid-run, not by a selection argument. `upto` stops the program right after its raw accept at a chosen position, so the report run of the stopped program ends exactly after that accept and its own public report batch; `upto_detects` shows that the stopped run's final snapshot is the one at which that batch was evaluated, and `violation_detects` turns a native violation there into a detected original-field failure through `AttemptReports.owner_origin`, the report-run form of `raw_origin`'s argument. The report flag is sticky (`continuation_sticky`), so every final outcome reachable through a violating accept has the flag raised (`forgery_detected`).
- `NativeForgery` is a reachability event on final outcomes: an outcome belongs to it when some execution path through a violating accept reaches it. It contains every outcome of a path that actually passes through a violating accept, so the bound covers the path event and can only be larger than it. The violation is read in the state reached when the accept returned; the accept changes no Beacon owner's signing history, reveal history or identity handle.
- CE-A2 is stated on the report-instrumented execution, not on the deployment game's state, because its event refers to the private signing log, reveal history and identity handles, which exist only there; `report_game` identifies the public projection with the deployment game under the approved codec laws `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`, which it needs through `compile_full_source`. CE-A2pub and CE-R1' are the public-transcript corollaries and are stated on the deployment game itself.
- `NativeForgery` keeps its unrevealed conjunct because `PqxdhDeploymentAcceptDetection` is stated for every source14 program, some of which reveal identities. For the compiled program the conjunct is proved to hold (`stopped_unrevealed`), so CE-A2 is also stated without it (`a2_generated_bound`).
- The A2 client (`PqxdhDeploymentProvenance.client`) is the instrumented execution followed by one terminal public batch for the raw candidate of every successful accept of the final transcript (`announceAll`). The batches add no identity request (`client_creates`), so CE-A2, CE-A2pub and CE-R1' share one family of reductions and one sum, and the published-tuple corollaries need only final-state facts.
- CE-A2pub and CE-R1' are stated for the Server's identity index 0. The reason is the proof route: `compile_full_source` identifies the compiled program's initialized execution with the deployment game at the identity index equal to the number of identity seeds in the starting pool, and the report-instrumented execution starts from empty pools. In the deployment game the index enters only through `PqxdhLiveServerConstruction.initialized`, which stores it in the constructed Server's metadata next to its key ID, but no lemma shows that the probabilities of the CE-A2pub and CE-R1' events do not depend on it, so other indices are not claimed. CE-R1 and CE-C3 hold for every identity index.
- CE-A2pub and CE-R1' are proved per owner (`a2pub_owner_bound`, `r1prime_owner_bound`): the forged or doubly accepted owner must have made no rejected bundle call in Fresh, and other owners are unrestricted. This is stronger than the plan's events, which require `NoBundleFailure` for every owner; those planned forms (`a2pub_bound`, `r1prime_bound`) keep their statements and are corollaries.
- CE-A2pub compares key tuples, never signature bytes: the verified tuple of the accept against the tuple parsed from the published bundle. No SUF-CMA or signature-uniqueness assumption is used.
- CE-R1' needs no reduction of its own: its event is contained in the CE-A2pub event on every outcome (`replay_published`), so its bound is the CE-A2pub bound, not twice it.
- CE-C3 is proved with the consumption relation of `RawCtxReceiveConsumption` (`ConsumedRefines`, `raw_spent`, `send_spent`, `initialized_spent`) rather than HB-105's private history monitor: the deployment handler runs owners and peers directly, so the consumed positions are read from the transcript. The owner side includes the finish's acceptance of sequence one, so the registration record cannot be accepted again by a raw receive; this is stronger than the plan's raw-receive-only reading. Peers are separated by the key ID the decrypted record names, which is the key ID the Server routed to (`rawGame_success`).
- No planned statement was found false or unprovable, and none was weakened.

## Unit 3: names for consumers

- CE-R1: `PqxdhDeploymentReplay.replay_game`, `registered_game`, `Registered`, `registrationOf`, `entryRegistration`, `acceptIds`, `accept_registration`.
- CE-C3: `PqxdhDeploymentRecordReplay.record_replay_game`, `tracked_game`, `Tracked`, `Holds`, `peerSequences`, `ownerSequences`; kernel provenance `PqxdhDeploymentRecordKernels.Live`, `Finished`, `build_live`, `tail_finished`, `finish_owners`, `source_step`.
- CE-A2: `PqxdhDeploymentProvenance.a2_bound`, `a2_generated_bound`, `GeneratedForgery`, `GeneratedViolation`, `generated_forgery_iff`, `stopped_unrevealed`, `upto_unread`, `reduction`, `client`, `report_game`, `client_transfer`; query bills `a2_bills`, `a2_service_bound`, `a2_client_uniform`, `a2_reduction_uniform`, `a2_game_uniform`, `client_class_bound`; generic detection `PqxdhDeploymentAcceptDetection.NativeForgery`, `Violation`, `ViolationAt`, `upto`, `forgery_detected`, `forgery_bound`.
- Signing history: `PqxdhDeploymentSigningHistory.signing_history`, `Signed`, `signed_source`, `execution`.
- CE-A2pub and CE-R1': `PqxdhDeploymentPublishedTuple.a2pub_owner_bound`, `r1prime_owner_bound`, `OwnerEvent`, `ReplayOwnerEvent`, `OwnerForgery`, `DoubleAcceptAt`, `owner_published_failure`, `replay_owner_published`, `game_owner`, `replay_owner`, `published_owner`, `double_owner`, `game_report_holds`, `GameHolds`, `ReportHolds`; the planned forms `a2pub_bound`, `r1prime_bound`, `GameEvent`, `ReplayEvent`, `PublishedForgery`, `DoubleAccept`, `game_report`, `replay_published`; `publishedTuple`, `ownerIdentities`; with a generated Server seed every identity handle is generated (`report_generated`) and below the creation cap (`generated_lt`).

## Unit 3: contracts used

CE-R1, CE-C3, the signing-history lemma and the detection lemma use no codec law. The CE-A2 bounds (`a2_bound`, `a2_generated_bound`) use only the existing attached-signature format rule `shortRejects` with `setup.wiring` and the adversary's budget. Reading them as statements about the deployment experiment uses `report_game`, which needs the approved codec laws `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack` through `compile_full_source`. CE-A2pub and CE-R1' are stated on the deployment game and take both laws, which `game_report_holds` needs for the same reason. The query bills take the budget and, for the uniform bills, T5b's existing cost premises `initBound` and `KemBound setup caps`; these bound query counts and are not security assumptions. `FrameReadBack` and `DecoderAgreement` are not used; the router stays a free parameter. No new assumption is introduced; SUF-CMA and signature uniqueness are not used. The supplied Server seed is `none` in every security statement.

## Unit 3: open points

- CE-A2pub and CE-R1' hold only for owners that made no rejected bundle call in Fresh (per owner, stronger than the planned `NoBundleFailure` runs): a Fresh owner whose bundle call fails may sign several tuples, and then a second accepted tuple need not be a forgery.
- CE-A2pub and CE-R1' are not claimed for a Server identity index other than 0 (see the decisions above).
- The A2 client's public candidate reports are not counted. T5b counts at most `(count + 1) * (qAccept + 1)` reports for its single terminal candidate; `announceAll` makes one batch per successful accept of the final transcript, and bounding their number by `qAccept` needs a transcript-counting invariant that is not proved. The reports are not queries of the joint weak-EUF game, whose adversary has only the signing, converted-DH and uniform classes, so the bills above cover every query of the reductions and of their games.
- CE-C3 is per live object; replay across copies, restorations or persistent state is outside the experiment (the plan's R4 limitation, CE-M).
- No run-level witness that the events are reachable (an honest relay accepting records, or a run reaching a violating accept); the bounds and invariants are not vacuous by construction, but no concrete run is exhibited.
- The open points of unit A that concern later stages (the `designateIn` range lemma, the liveness witness for `designate`, the post-run reveal mode, `KeyMatchFinal` for CE-9 step 9.1) are unchanged.

## Unit 3: re-estimate for the remaining units

Unit 3 was estimated at 5-9 agent-days and took one working session. The signing-history invariant `Signed` relates the compiled program's transcript to the private identity pool along the stacked execution; the bundle-to-KEM-pool invariant that CE-9 step 9.1 needs for `KeyMatchFinal` can follow the same pattern over the same execution, which lowers CE-9 from 5-10 to 3-7 agent-days (CE-9b unchanged at 3-6). The other figures of unit A are unchanged: unit 4 (CE-8) 12-20, CE-11 6-12, CE-13 and CE-14 5-9. Item 3 proper without CE-9b, CE-10 and CE-12 is then about 26-48 agent-days remaining.

## Unit 3: review fix pass

The review's findings and what changed, in commit order. No existing statement was changed or weakened: every theorem of the first pass keeps its statement up to whitespace (line breaks and indentation), checked by a whitespace-normalized comparison with 6bd221ba, and the new statements are additions. The only removed declaration is the proof-internal definition `PqxdhDeploymentPublishedTuple.DecodedEvent`, which the new generic `DecodedHolds` replaces inside the proof of `game_report_holds`.

- Query bills of the CE-A2, CE-A2pub and CE-R1' reductions (semantic, minor). New in `PqxdhDeploymentProvenance`: `client_class_bound`, `a2_service_bound`, `a2_client_uniform`, `a2_reduction_uniform`, `a2_game_uniform` and `a2_bills`, described above. The public candidate report count is recorded as an open point.
- Unrevealed conjunct of the CE-A2 event (semantic, minor). New in `PqxdhDeploymentProvenance`: `upto_unread`, `stopped_unrevealed`, `GeneratedViolation`, `GeneratedForgery`, `generated_forgery_iff` and `a2_generated_bound`, described above. `a2_bound` is unchanged; `a2_generated_bound` has the same premises and bound for the event without the conjunct, which contains `NativeForgery`, so it is at least as strong.
- Codec laws of `report_game` (semantic minor, hygiene minor). The module docstring of `PqxdhDeploymentProvenance`, the docstrings of `a2_bound` and `report_game`, the CE-A2 paragraph, the decisions and the contracts above, the analysis and the receipt now say that the bounds use only `shortRejects` and the budget, and that reading CE-A2 as a statement about the deployment experiment uses `report_game` with the approved `Phase1ReadBack` and `Phase2ReadBack`.
- Per-owner `NoBundleFailure` (semantic, nit). New in `PqxdhDeploymentPublishedTuple`: `OwnerForgery`, `published_owner`, `owner_published_failure`, `GameHolds`, `ReportHolds`, `DecodedHolds`, `game_report_holds`, `OwnerEvent`, `game_owner`, `a2pub_owner_bound`, `DoubleAcceptAt`, `double_owner`, `ReplayOwnerEvent`, `replay_owner`, `replay_owner_published` and `r1prime_owner_bound`. `published_failure`, `game_report`, `a2pub_bound`, `replay_published` and `r1prime_bound` keep their statements and are now derived from the per-owner forms. Three `set_option linter.deprecated false in` lines that the re-proved theorems no longer need were dropped; one remains, on `game_report_holds`.
- Server identity index 0 (semantic, nit). Not proved; the decisions and the open points above now state the reason accurately: it comes from `compile_full_source` starting from empty pools, the index enters the game only through the Server's metadata, and no invariance lemma is claimed.
- Docstrings for `kept_refl` and `kept_trans` (hygiene, nit). Added; no statement changed.
- Stray line breaks in the `record_replay_game` signature (hygiene, nit). Reflowed as in `replay_game`, together with the same breaks in `tracked_game`; the statements are unchanged and the receipt reproduces the new layout.
- Namespaced citations (hygiene, nit). The CE-R1 paragraph now cites `PqxdhRegistrationSchedule.handler_admission` and `PqxdhRegistrationSchedule.handler_consumed_count`.
- The identity-index claim about the signing-history lemma (hygiene, nit) was in the handoff report only. `PqxdhDeploymentSigningHistory.signing_history` has no identity-index parameter: it is stated on the initialized source execution of the compiled program from empty pools, in which the Server takes identity index 0. The guide, the analysis and the receipt already said so.

## Unit 3: checks

Every module was checked on its own with Lean 4.34 (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) under the shared lock into the unit 3 scratch prefix, a symlink mirror of the unit A prefix in which an artifact is unlinked before it is written. After each source change of the fix pass the changed module and every unit 3 module that imports it were re-checked in import order, and after the last source change all seven modules were re-checked in import order; every check exited 0 with an empty log. All 148 statements carry exact `#print axioms` guards: 138 depend on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, 7 on `propext` and `Quot.sound`, and 3 on `propext` alone. No module uses `sorry`, `admit`, an axiom declaration, `native_decide`, `implemented_by`, `extern`, `unsafe` or `opaque`; the only `set_option` lines are `autoImplicit false` and one theorem-scoped `linter.deprecated false in` on `game_report_holds`. After each module commit the root import check of `BeaconcryptCore.lean` exited 0 with an empty log, and the focused `check-inventory` (minimal Nix flake, all files of the commit staged, other files held aside) passed; `git diff --check` is clean. No full `lake build`, `make verify`, mutation testing or `nix-collect-garbage` was run.

## CE-3 trial (history)

Trial status (superseded where unit A says otherwise): CE-3 trial of handoff item 3 (the common computational/symbolic experiment), on branch `codex/deployment-experiment` over central `bccd853e`, not pushed and not integrated. An independent review returned APPROVE_WITH_NITS with one major and four minor findings; all are fixed in documentation, and none needed a Lean change. The plain-English explanation is the section "Common deployment experiment and its compile lemma (CE-3 trial)" of [the analysis](../formal-verification-analysis.md).

### What is proved

Twelve modules under `beaconcrypt-core/proofs/lean/BeaconcryptCore/Computational/` define a first version of the common deployment experiment and prove that it compiles into an ordinary program of the existing initialized registration schedule (source14). The adversary talks to one Server, built from a generated seed, and to Beacons it creates with pins of its choice. It can issue coins, `create`, bundle, accept, build, finish, HMAC and BLAKE2b commands, and send and receive raw records on Server peers and Beacons. It gets no reveal command, no getter, no snapshot and no start input.

| Module | Statements | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `PqxdhDeploymentInterface` | 3 | Command set, replies, mode O and the mode-P wrapper `observe`, `parsePin` (CE-1 subset). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentNative` | 5 | Native state with ghost raw counters, record, Server and Beacon steps, handler, generated-seed `game` (CE-2 subset). |
| `PqxdhDeploymentRecordCache` | 6 | Local HKDF, AEAD and frame writer with BLAKE2b sent to source14; `local_record`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentCompile` | 1 | Public log, local copies, `peerView`, `ownerView`, `compile`, `merge`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentCompileRecords` | 23 | `server_record`, `beacon_record`: local record commands agree with the native steps after merging. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentCompileRegistration` | 30 | Registration never reads copied kernels; every registration command commutes with `merge`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentCompileStep` | 9 | `step_merge` for all commands under an invariant; generic `compile_simulation`, `compile_run`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentCompileFootprint` | 12 | Accept, build, bundle and finish footprints reused from the existing registration proofs. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentCompileInvariant` | 24 | Invariant `Good`, its preservation per command, `compile_native`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentCompileGame` | 3 | `construct_fields`, `compile_game`, `compile_source`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentReadHistory` | 4 | `kernel_read`, `server_reveal`, `kernel_unreported`, `reveal_unreported`. |
| `PqxdhDeploymentCompileBudget` | 10 | `compile_bound` and its per-command lemmas. |

`compile_game` states that the generated-seed deployment game and the initialized native run of `compile A` have the same joint distribution of the Server's public data, the adversary's output, the whole merged deployment state and the runtime state. `compile_source` states the same for the annotated initialized execution that the KEM theorem T4, the root theorems and the report machinery use. `kernel_read` and `server_reveal` show that the compiled program's two internal reads leave the whole annotated state unchanged, and `kernel_unreported` and `reveal_unreported` show that the report wrapper emits nothing for them. `compile_bound` transports per-class command budgets to caps on the fourteen source14 query kinds. The identity, KEM, prekey and one-time read caps are zero.

All 130 statements carry exact `#print axioms` guards: 126 depend on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, two on `propext` alone, one on `propext` and `Quot.sound`, and one on no axiom. No module uses `sorry`, `admit`, an axiom declaration, `native_decide`, `implemented_by`, `extern`, `unsafe` or `opaque`.

### Contracts used

The owner approved four codec contracts of the common experiment on 2026-10-06. They are listed in both assumption lists: the functional contracts under "Assumptions made by the proofs" in [the analysis](../formal-verification-analysis.md#assumptions-made-by-the-proofs) and "Primitive guarantees, additional assumptions and idealizations" in [the composition assumptions](../composition-assumptions.md#primitive-guarantees-additional-assumptions-and-idealizations).

- Used by CE-3 (premises of `compile_native`, `good_step`, `compile_game` and `compile_source`): `Phase1ReadBack`, which gives a Beacon's identity key from its bundle bytes, and `Phase2ReadBack`, which gives the Server identity from a build response.
- Approved but not yet used: `FrameReadBack`, and the one-codec rule with `RawCtxServerRouting.DecoderAgreement` for the Server header router. The experiment and the compiled program already share the codec's frame reader and writer by definition, and the router stays a free parameter.

These are abstract message-model laws about the codec parameter, not facts about Cap'n Proto or any serialization implementation. The trial adds no hardness assumption, idealization or advantage term.

### Plan changes recorded

- Read caps. The plan's section 8 table bounds Server reveals by `qBuild` and established-kernel reads by `min(qCreate, qFinish)`. `compile_bound` gives looser caps: reveals at most the budget of Server send and raw-receive commands, and kernel reads at most the budget of Beacon send and raw-receive commands. `peerKernel` and `ownerKernel` read whenever no local copy exists, including for key IDs that are not installed and Beacons that are not established, and an empty answer is not kept, so such reads repeat. Soundness is unaffected: T4 `actual_le_real` instantiates on `compile A` with `compile_bound` as its budget premise, and its creation-count premise uses only the create cap. Later bills that count reveals or kernel reads use the record-command budgets. Gating the reads on the public log would need an amortized whole-run count in place of the per-command `weighted_bound` argument and changes to several checked modules, so it was not done.
- The local Server metadata placeholder (plan section 8 b) is unnecessary: Server metadata is `Unit`, and the invariant proves that the sender ID equals `setup.serverKeyId`.
- A peer's identity needs only the identity parse of the accept bytes, not the full signature check.

### Open points

- F1, decided by the owner on 2026-10-06: option (a), recorded as deviation D5. The experiment's adversary takes no input; it learns the Server identity key through the protocol, for example by registering one of its own Beacons and reading the build response, which costs registration queries and shifts session ordinals and key IDs, so it is not an exact reduction from an adversary that receives the key as input. Background: The plan gives the adversary the Server's public identity key as input, but T4-T6, the report lines and `InitializedSchedule.source` run a fixed program with no input after the Server is generated, and no source14 command returns the Server key. In the trial the adversary therefore has no input and can learn the Server key only by parsing build responses. Learning it by registering first also shifts session ordinals and key IDs, so this is not an exact reduction from the plan's adversary. Options: (a) accept the input-free adversary as a recorded deviation, at no proof cost; (b) generalize the T4-T6 and report lines to programs that depend on the generated public identity; (c) add a Server-identity getter to source14 and re-prove the line over the extended interface. With the key as input only `Phase1ReadBack` would be needed; the trial needs `Phase2ReadBack` because it reads the Server identity from a build response.
- Post-run reveal mode. On 2026-10-06 the owner chose a post-run "later quantum" mode: after the run, every honest X25519 secret, every DH output and every honest Ed25519 identity secret is revealed, and only confidentiality is claimed. The interface stays open to it. Because `compile_game` equates the whole final deployment state, the mode can be added as a phase after the run that is a function of the final state, without changing `Command` or the compile cases. Open: on the compiled side the secrets are reachable only through source14's identity, prekey and one-time field reads, which `compile_bound` caps at zero, which interact with `revealedAt` and `GoodKem`, and which follow native erasure; the Server identity seed, ephemeral scalars and DH outputs have no read. Its cost is to be designed and confirmed before it is built.
- Not done: the `test` and `fresh` commands with the sampling swap, `CE.Logs`, mode P beyond the wrapper, the public functions `phase`, `owner`, `NoBundleFailure` and `designate`, the comparison of the transported caps with T4's ledgers, and the step from `compile A` to `tag work index`.

### Re-estimate

Only CE-3 is revised on the trial's evidence. CE-3 is about 7-12 agent-days in total against the plan's 18-30; about 4-6 were spent in the trial, and 3-6 remain: `test` and `fresh` with their budgets (1-2), the `CE.Logs` extension (1-3) and re-checking the existing modules (1). The CE-1 and CE-2 parts listed above were done inside the same time box. Their remaining parts and every other stage (CE-4 to CE-9, CE-11, CE-13, CE-14) keep the plan's ranges, unrevised. With only CE-3 revised, item 3 proper (plan 78-128) is about 67-110 agent-days in total and about 63-104 remaining. A lower figure would assume that the trial's speed carries over to stages it did not touch, which is speculative; CE-8, CE-11 and item 2 need new reductions rather than operational equalities. For F1, option (a) costs nothing; figures for (b) and (c) are speculative, and the plan's only comparable figure, for extending source14 and re-proving the T1-T6 line (section 8 fallback (b)), is 25-45 agent-days.

### Checks

Each module was checked on its own with Lean 4.34 (`-j1`, `-M8000`, timeout 180) under the shared lock, the root import was checked after each module commit, and the focused `check-inventory` passed after each commit. The review recompiled all twelve modules from source in a fresh prefix with the same results. The fix pass changed only documentation; `check-inventory` and `git diff --check` were run again. No full `lake build`, `make verify`, mutation testing or `nix-collect-garbage` was run.
